First, the one stanza starting with "If a bodhisattva": "If" whatever "bodhisattva", "while not" "thoroughly" "knowing" the mode of the profound meaning of the Mother, free from conceptual elaboration, as it has been explained, "thoroughly" "teaches" that these five aggregates of "form" "and" "perception", "feeling" "and" "volition", and "consciousness" are "impermanent" and, as illustrated by that, suffering, and merely the selflessness of persons, and merely a limited emptiness, and says, 'this is the supreme perfection of wisdom', that person "is engaging in" a reflection of the perfection of wisdom, or a "fabricated" or pseudo perfection of wisdom; by teaching in a way that is discordant with dharmatā, that squanders the intent of the supreme vehicle, whereas "the wise" who realize the meaning of the Mother exactly as it is unmistakably teach the "phenomena" established by the profound pristine awareness of the Victors, endowed with meaning and letters, and "never" cause the profound teaching of the Tathāgata to decline or "be destroyed". ^1-110

###### How to Explain the Authentic Perfection of Wisdom That Is to Be Adopted ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-3-2-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-111]]

Second, "In whatever, not form" and so forth: "In whatever" teaching of Dharma there is "not" strong attachment "to form", and likewise "not" "to feeling", "and" there is "no" "apprehension" "of perception", "and" "consciousness" is "not" apprehended, "and" there is "no" "apprehension" of "volition"; "thoroughly" "knowing", in a "manner" that transcends all objects "of conceptual thought", that "all" "phenomena" of saṃsāra and nirvāṇa—illustrated by the essence of phenomena like form and so forth and their particularities like impermanence and so forth—are primordially "unborn" and "empty", and "teaching" them like that to others, "this" "is" "engaging" "in" the "supreme" "perfection" of "wisdom". ^1-111

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-112]]

The benefits of explaining it in that way. ^1-112

###### The Benefits of Explaining the Authentic Perfection of Wisdom ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-3-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-113]]

Second, regarding the benefits of explaining the genuine perfection of wisdom, there are two parts: the actual benefits, and the reason for them. ^1-113

###### The Actual Subject ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-3-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-114]]

First, the one stanza starting with "Someone ... of the Ganges": "Compared to" "someone" who establishes or "tames" "in" "arhatship", the ultimate fruition of hearers and solitary realizers, "however many" "beings" there are "residing" "in" "fields" "as numerous as" the "sand" "of the river" "Ganges", "if" "someone" "writes down" "in letters" "this" "perfection" "of wisdom" and "gives" that volume "to" a "supreme being"—a bodhisattva who realizes its meaning and is able to "explain" it to others—"this" "merit" "is" much more "distinguished" than the former. ^1-114

###### The Reason ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-3-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-115]]

Second, regarding the reason, there are two parts: the actual reason, and demonstrating it by means of examples. ^1-115

###### The Actual Reason ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-3-2-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-116]]

First, the one stanza starting with "If one asks why": "If one asks why" that merit is distinguished in that way: it is because "those" "supreme speakers", the bodhisattvas, "having trained" "in this" path, "teach" "in this" great vehicle that "all" "phenomena" are "emptiness", and "having heard" that mode of emptiness, even those of inferior intellect understand form to be like a mass of foam and so forth, so that those of the "hearer" family also "swiftly" "reach" "liberation" from saṃsāra; "and" those of medium faculties, having seen the meaning of dependent origination, attain "solitary realizer" "enlightenment"; "and" those of sharp faculties, having realized all phenomena as equality, "will reach" "the enlightenment" "of a buddha". ^1-116

###### Showing It by an Example ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-3-2-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-117]]

Second, there are two parts: a brief indication through the example of a shoot, and a detailed explanation through the examples of the sun and a nāga. ^1-117

###### Showing It Briefly through the Example of a Sprout ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-3-2-2-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-118]]

First, the one stanza starting with "Without a shoot": Just as "without a shoot" a "tree" "could" "never" "appear" "in the world", and "for that" non-existent tree, "how" "could" "branches", "leaves", "flowers", "and" "fruit" "appear"? They could not appear. "Likewise", "without" the ultimate and relative "mind" "of awakening", which is like a shoot, taught in the Mother of the Victors, a "Victor", who is like a tree, "could" "never" "appear" "in the world"; "and" without that, "how" "could" "Śakra", "Brahmā", "and" the "hearers" "appear" as the "fruit" of the Dharma taught by that buddha, similar to branches and leaves? Since there is no cause, they could not. ^1-118

###### Explaining It Extensively through the Examples of the Sun and a Nāga ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-119]]

Second, there are two parts: the positive example, illustrated by the sun, and the negative example, illustrated by a nāga lord. ^1-119

###### Illustrating the Example of Positive Concomitance through the Sun ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-120]]

First, the one stanza starting with "The orb of the sun": Thus, "when" a "net" "of light" shines or "is emitted" "from the orb" "of the sun" in the ten directions, "then" "beings" "strive" "to perform" various endpoints of "actions". "Likewise", "if" "the wise" generate the "mind" of aspiration and application "in the world" in order to obtain the omniscient pristine awareness of the perfection of "wisdom" of the Victors, from that arises the fruition, the unsurpassable pristine awareness of a Victor, and "by" "that very" "pristine awareness", through observing all knowable things unmistakably, they emit rays of light of Dharma targeting those to be tamed, so that "beings" "will come to possess" "qualities", the "phenomena" of the provisional states and the ultimate. ^1-120

###### Illustrating the Example of Negative Concomitance through a Nāga King ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-121]]

Second, the two stanzas starting with "For example, Anavatapta": "If, for example," the "lord of nāgas" himself "did not exist" "in lake" "Anavatapta", "how could" the "rivers" arising from the power of that nāga "flow" "in this" "Jambudvīpa"? They would not flow. And "if" there are "no" "rivers", since there is no moisture, "flowers" "and" "fruits" "would not appear" either. Furthermore, "also in" the great "oceans" that arise from the meeting of mother and son waters, "many" different "forms" of various "jewels" "would not exist" either. "Likewise as in that example", "if" "in this" "world" "too" there is "no" "mind of awakening" uniting the two and having the essence of emptiness and compassion, which is like the nāga lord, "how could" "the pristine awareness" "of the Sugatas", which is similar to a river's stream through increasing the seed of awakening and so forth, "appear" "in all" "these" "worlds"? It would be illogical to appear. "If" that river-like "pristine awareness" is "not" present, then through the disciples hearing and so forth the Dharma taught by it, the "qualities" of virtue, which are like flowers and become the causes for higher realms and definitive goodness, would "not" be accomplished and "flourish", and if they are absent, their fruits, the three "awakenings", would not exist either. The immeasurable "phenomena of a buddha" such as the powers, "which are like" an "ocean" filled with jewels, "would not exist" either. Therefore, if the precious mind of awakening, inseparable in the two truths and having the essence of emptiness and compassion, exists, all the qualities of the path and the fruition follow upon it, and if it is absent, all others are repelled, so this alone constitutes the root of all pure phenomena. ^1-121

###### How to Generate Its Nature in the Mind-Stream ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-122]]

The way its nature is generated in the mind-stream: Fourth, regarding generating the nature of that perfection of wisdom in the mind-stream, there are two parts: what is to be done during the post-meditation, and what is to be meditated upon in meditative equipoise. ^1-122

###### What Is to Be Done in Post-Meditation ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-123]]

What is to be done during the post-meditation: Regarding the first, there are three parts: rejoicing, which is the cause for merit to increase; dedication, which is the cause for it not to be wasted; and aspiration, which is the cause for it to progress higher and higher. Rejoicing, which is the cause for merit to increase: ^1-123

###### The Nature of the Cause for Increasing Merit ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-124]]

Regarding the first, there are two parts: indicating the benefits of a bodhisattva's rejoicing, and indicating in common the basis for rejoicing and dedication. ^1-124

###### Showing the Benefits of a Bodhisattva's Rejoicing ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-1-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-125]]

Regarding the first, there are two parts: the example and the meaning. ^1-125

###### The Example ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-1-1-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-126]]

First, the one stanza starting with "In this world": "In this" "world" system, whatever luminous stars, fires, lamps, and so forth that "illuminate", and "all" "however many" "beings" there are such as fireflies and those holding lamps, even if the "lights" of their respective bodies and substances "appeared" in one place "in order to" "illuminate" the directions, a "single" "ray of light" arising from the "orb of the sun" "is" much "more supreme" "than" that; "all" those other "lights" "of" the "mass" of illuminators "cannot match a fraction" in competing with its clarity and pervasiveness. ^1-126

###### The Meaning ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-1-1-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-127]]

Second, the one stanza starting with "The multitudes of hearers": Just as in the example just explained, "however much" the "multitudes" "of hearers" "generate" "accumulations" of the three bases for performing "meritorious deeds" "fully endowed with" "generosity", "discipline", "and" "meditation", "the mass" "of merit" "of the multitudes" "of hearers" "cannot match even a fraction" "of" generating a "single" "instant" of the "mind" of "rejoicing" "of a bodhisattva". This is because, no matter how great the actual roots of virtue of the hearers may be, their focal objects and aspects are small in scope, they are not purified of the stain of conceptualization, and they are not embraced by skillful dedication, so their fruition is small in scope. ^1-127

###### Showing the Common Basis of Rejoicing and Dedication ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-1-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-128]]

Second, the basis for rejoicing and dedication: The three stanzas starting with "Of former past times": "Whatever" "tens of millions" "of buddhas" "of former" "past times" "who resided" "in" "many" "boundless" "thousands of millions" of "realms", which illustrates the boundless buddhas of the past, "and" "whoever" "has passed beyond sorrow" from saṃsāra, and "those" "protectors of the world" who are currently living and remaining, "who" "teach the precious dharma" "in order to" "exhaust suffering"—from "initially" "generating" the "supreme mind of awakening" in their own mind-streams until "the time when the true dharma of those guides" "vanishes" or becomes exhausted, "whatever merit" those "victors" have from their intention of generating bodhicitta, "and" their application "endowed with the six perfections", "and" the fruitional "qualities of a buddha" such as the ten powers that they have attained, "and" "whatever other" "virtues" of "the sons of the buddhas", the bodhisattvas, "and" "the hearers and solitary realizers", both those in "training" "and" "beyond training", "with defilements" "and" "without defilements"—all those, having been "gathered" "into one" by one's own mind, "that bodhisattva" "rejoices" "in them" with joy from the depths of their intention, "and" generating the mind toward unsurpassed awakening, in order to attain "unsurpassed great awakening" "as the cause" to accomplish "the welfare of boundless beings", they must "dedicate" "all those" roots of virtue of themselves and others having gathered them into one. Relatively, the essence of dedication is to transform those roots of virtue toward that goal, like shooting an arrow, through volition accompanied by aspiration focused on the authentic object of dedication. Dedication, the cause for merit not to be wasted: ^1-128

###### Dedication as the Cause for Not Going to Waste ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-129]]

Second, regarding dedication, there are two parts: the actual dedication and the benefits of dedicating in that way. ^1-129

###### The Actual Meaning ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-1-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-130]]

Regarding the first, there are two parts: dedicating in the manner of ultimate non-apprehension, and dedication that trains in the footsteps of the buddhas relatively. ^1-130

###### Dedication by Way of Ultimate Non-Apprehension ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-1-2-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-131]]

Regarding the first, there are two parts: individually explaining the refutation and the establishment, and indicating the summary of its meaning along with an example. ^1-131

###### Explaining Refutation and Affirmation Separately ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-1-2-1-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-132]]

Regarding the first, there are two parts: the object to be refuted, which is dedication with apprehension, and the object to be established, which is dedication without apprehension. ^1-132

###### Referential Dedication, the Object of Refutation ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-1-2-1-1-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-133]]

First, the one stanza starting with "If one fully dedicates": "If", in "fully dedicating", there "arises" "the perception" of true grasping "in the mind" that dedicates, "and" the "perception" of grasping at "awakening", the state to which one dedicates, and "if" the "perception" of the "beings" who are the objects to which one "fully dedicates" arises, then "because" they are bound by "the perception" of manifest grasping, they "abide" in a "view" with apprehension. Since "that mind" "has" the "apprehension" of being "attached" through true grasping "to" the "three spheres" of dedication, they do "not" "engage in" "the full dedication" of an unmistakable nature, and it is not included in the number of completely pure dedications. ^1-133

###### Non-Referential Dedication, the Object of Affirmation ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-1-2-1-1-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-134]]

Second, the one stanza starting with "If thus": "If", reversing that manner of dedicating with apprehension, one "knows" "thus" that relatively, "these" "dharmas" of what is dedicated and by what it is dedicated—the virtue to be dedicated and the dedicating mind—are momentary and therefore "cease" "and" are by nature "exhausted", "and" that "awakening", the state "to which" one "fully dedicates", also does not arise simultaneously with the former and therefore is "exhausted" or non-existent; and because ultimately those three dharmas of what is dedicated, what dedicates, and the dedication are not established even as a single moment or an atom, they are unborn in their own essence or primordially ceased and exhausted, meaning they have never existed, therefore one "knows" the manner in which ultimately a "dharma" "never" "dedicates" "to a dharma", or there is nothing to dedicate. Embraced by "thoroughly knowing" "thus" without being attached to the three spheres of dedication, "that is" what is called "excellently" "fully dedicated". ^1-134

###### Summary of the Meaning ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-1-2-1-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-135]]

Third, the summary of the meaning of those two: the one stanza starting with "If as a sign": In short, one dedicates through aspiration by making the three dharmas—who dedicates, to what they dedicate, and so forth—the object, but one must know that to be in the manner of an illusion relatively. "If" one apprehends those three dharmas "as a sign" and "dedicates" them, "that" "is" "not" perfect "dedication". "But if" one knows those three dharmas to be of a "signless" nature and "dedicates" them, "that" "is" perfect "dedication" to "awakening". "Just as" when one "eats" "good" "food" "mixed" "with" "poison", though the food is good, one is harmed by the poison; similarly, even though they are "white" "dharmas", "apprehending" "them" with the perception of true grasping is "said" by the "Victorious One" to be "like" "that" food mixed with poison. ^1-135

###### Relative Dedication ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-1-2-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-136]]

Second, dedication relatively: the one stanza starting with "Therefore thus": Because of "that" being whether the dedication is completely pure or not, having "thus" known the faults and qualities, one "should" "train" in "full" "dedication". "Just as" "those" "victorious ones" realised or "knew" that unmistakable "virtue" of rejoicing and dedication to be like this, "whatever" "aspect" it "is"—meaning, whatever the essence or aspect of dedication is relatively—"and" "whatever" and what kind of infallible result "arises" from that conventionally, "and" "whatever" "characteristic" of their non-apprehension there "is" ultimately; in short, having realised the nature of the indivisibility of the two truths, "to" how the victorious ones rejoice and dedicate, "I too" will correspondingly "rejoice" "like" "that" "and" "fully" "dedicate" "thus". By thinking that one dedicates just as the victorious ones dedicate, even if one is unskilled in how to dedicate oneself, by dedicating after other skilled ones, the dedication will accord with perfect dedication, just as is stated in the two stanzas starting with "As the hero Mañjuśrī..." from the Aspiration of Good Conduct. ^1-136

###### The Benefits of Dedicating in This Way ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-1-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-137]]

Second, regarding the benefits of dedicating in that way, there are two parts: the actual benefits and explaining them extensively by way of an example. ^1-137

###### The Actual Meaning ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-1-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-138]]

First: the one stanza starting with "Thus merit": "If" one "fully" "dedicates" all "merit" to great "awakening" "thus" as just explained, the cause, which is dedication, is "poisonless", and its result, which is "buddhahood", is "not" "abandoned" or is attained, and one "speaks" unmistakably in accordance with the dharma of the Great Vehicle "taught" "by" the "victorious ones". "That" "heroic" bodhisattva who "fully" "dedicates" "thus" in the manner of non-apprehension "outshines" "all" "the bodhisattvas" "with" "apprehension" "as many as there are" "in" the "world". ^1-138

###### Detailed Explanation by Way of Examples ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-1-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-139]]

Regarding the second, there are two parts: the manner of attaining the result, which is omniscience, and the manner of attaining the pure path of the perfections. ^1-139

###### Attaining the Result of Omniscience ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-1-2-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-140]]

Regarding the first, there are two parts: the other perfections being led to omniscience through the example of a guide, and the manner in which, because the eyes have not been drawn, one does not attain omniscience even if the other perfections are complete. ^1-140

###### Leading the Other Perfections to Omniscience by the Example of a Guide ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-1-2-2-2-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-141]]

First, the one stanza starting with "Blind without a guide": Just as if "tens of millions" of "blind" people without eyes who "lack" a "guide" to lead them on the path were gathered together, yet "do" "not" see or "know" the "path" that lies before them, "how" "could" they enter that path and "enter" into another "city"? It is impossible. "Likewise", "if" they are "without" this eye-like "perfection" of "wisdom", "these" "five" "perfections" of generosity and so forth, which are like being "without" "eyes" from their own side, are not grasped by wisdom, which is like a "guide" to lead them in a direction; since they are "without" that companion, they do not know the noble path, and therefore they are "not" "able" to "touch" upon the city of perfect "awakening". ^1-141

###### How Omniscience Is Not Attained Even When the Other Perfections Are Complete if the Eyes Are Not Painted ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-1-2-2-2-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-142]]

Second, the one stanza starting with "When wisdom": "When" those other positive dharmas like generosity "are" "fully" "grasped" "by" "wisdom", which does not conceptualize the three spheres, "at" "that" "time" they "acquire" "eyes" "and" "obtain" "the" "name" of this "perfection", thereby attaining what allows one to effortlessly reach the city of omniscience. To "illustrate" that with an "example": "if" "forms" such as humans and so on in a "painting" have been drawn "completely", part by part, yet they "lack" "only" "eyes", meaning they are incomplete, "as" "long" "as" the "eyes" have "not" been "drawn", the painting is incomplete, and therefore one "does" "not" "receive" "its" "wage"; similarly, even if the other virtues are complete, if there is no wisdom, one does not attain the result. Thus, one should know that it is the essence of the path of the Great Vehicle to possess non-apprehending dedication through a mind of omniscience that is intent upon the Mother of the result itself—without being diverted by other paths—by means of an intelligence that is certain to ripen exclusively into the wakefulness of emptiness endowed with all supreme aspects, which is the result of transforming all paths into emptiness endowed with all supreme aspects in dependence on the wisdom that knows the suchness of all dharmas. ^1-142

###### How the Pure Path of the Perfections Is Attained ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-1-2-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-143]]

Regarding the second, there are two parts: demonstrating through the example of space that by non-apprehension one enters the ranks of the perfection of wisdom, and the manner in which, if separated from that, the path is not completely pure. ^1-143

###### Teaching by Way of the Example of Space How One Is Included in the Ranks of the Perfection of Wisdom Through Non-Apprehension ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-1-2-2-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-144]]

First, the one stanza starting with "When conditioned": "When" all things belonging to "conditioned" "and" "unconditioned", "and" "white" "and" "black" "dharmas" are "analyzed" "by" "wisdom" that investigates the ultimate abiding nature, "and" the apprehension of marks has been "dismantled", "when" one "does" "not" "apprehend" them to be truly established or to be objects of marks "even" as much as a "subtle" "particle", but sees the reality of not abiding anywhere, they "go" into the "number" known "in" "the" "world" as the "perfection" of "wisdom". For example, since "space" has no nature of its own, it "does" "not" rely upon or "abide" "even" "slightly" "on" "anything" else; it is "like" "that". ^1-144

###### How the Path Is Not Completely Pure If Separated from That ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-1-2-2-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-145]]

Second, the one stanza starting with "If I": "If", despite being embraced by compassion, through failing to dismantle referential focus one goes astray into a state of apprehension, thinking, "I" "shall" "practice" this "perfection" of "wisdom" of the "victorious ones", "and" has a "mind" of evident clinging, thinking, "I will free" "many" "tens of millions" of "sentient beings" who are "touched" by "suffering", because of having the "notion" of "sentient beings" subsumed by self and other "thus", one is called a "bodhisattva" "with" apprehension who conceptualises "fully"; "such" a person "is" "not" "practicing" the "supreme" "perfection" of "wisdom". ^1-145

###### Devotion as the Cause for Increasing Higher and Higher ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-1-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-146]]

Admiration, the cause of increasing higher and higher. Regarding the third, admiration, there are three parts: the benefits of faith, the disadvantages of a lack of faith, and therefore the instruction to exert oneself through faith in the Mother. ^1-146

###### The Benefits of Faith ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-1-3-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-147]]

First, the one stanza starting with "A bodhisattva": "Whichever" "bodhisattva" "previously" in their past lives "when" "engaging" in practice "practiced" this profound meaning of the "perfection" of "wisdom" by way of listening, retaining, reflecting and so forth, having been born in this world, due to the power of those previous causes, becomes "learned" in the meaning of the perfection of wisdom and "without" "doubt" about it; "that" person, "as" "soon" as they "hear" this scripture, "generates" the "notion" of the "Teacher", thinking "I have seen the Teacher", and through that cause "will" "swiftly" "realize" the great "peace" of "awakening". ^1-147

###### The Disadvantages of Lack of Faith ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-1-3-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-148]]

Second, the one stanza starting with "When previously engaging": "If", "when" someone "previously" "engaged" in practice, they "revered" many "tens of millions" of "buddhas" and made offerings, thus accumulating merit, yet they "lacked faith" "in" the intent of the Great Vehicle, the "perfection" of "wisdom" of the "victorious ones", and did not make aspiration prayers to attain it, now, the moment they "hear" the profound perfection of wisdom, "that" person of "inferior intellect" "will reject" "this" profound supreme dharma through the wrong conduct of their three doors, and will not show respect. By rejecting that, they "will have rejected" all the omniscient dharmas of the buddhas of the three times, and having "become destitute" of the "refuge" of the noble dharma, "they" "will go" to the "Avīci" hell and experience suffering continuously throughout many eons of world-formation and destruction; thus it is taught that the disadvantages are immeasurable. ^1-148

###### Therefore, Advice to Exert Oneself with Faith in the Mother ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-1-3-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-149]]

Third, the one stanza starting with "Therefore": Since having faith in it has great benefits and lacking faith has great disadvantages, "therefore", "if" you "wish" to "touch" the "supreme" "wakefulness" of a "buddha", "generate faith" "here" in the "Mother" of the "victorious ones"! This is its unique indispensable cause. Now, at this time of encountering the Tathāgata's teaching, which is like an island of jewels, one must make it fruitful; "for example", it "would be" "improper" if "merchants" "went" "to" an "island" of "jewels" and, having "exhausted" their previous "goods", "returned" empty-handed without newly obtaining any profit of jewels. Likewise, even though the jewel of the profound perfection of wisdom is in one's hand, one discards it without admiring it, and through the cause of rejecting the dharma one's former merit becomes exhausted—one should not act like that. ^1-149

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-150]]

Meditating in meditative equipoise. ^1-150

###### How to Meditate in Meditative Equipoise ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-151]]

Regarding the second, the manner of meditating in meditative equipoise, there are four parts: the essence of the completely pure wakefulness, its function, its sphere of activity, and its aspects. ^1-151

###### The Essence of Perfectly Pure Wisdom ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-152]]

The essence of the completely pure wakefulness. First, regarding the essence, there is one stanza starting with "the purity of form": all dharmas are primordially completely pure, naturally passed beyond suffering, fully awakened, naturally luminous, and primordially unarisen and unceasing; through this essence of equality, meditating on everything as having a single flavor in the single sphere, the great dharmadhātu, luminous and free from obscurations, is the essence of the wakefulness of meditative equipoise. If one asks what its manner is: illustrated here by the words "the purity of form," all dharmas, from form as an object of knowledge up to omniscience, are primordially unestablished in any dualistic nature such as apprehended and apprehender, completely afflicted and completely purified, self and other, entity and non-entity, and so forth; and although they exist as the single sphere of the dharmadhātu, primordially free from arising and ceasing, as the equality that is naturally completely pure and the great state free from obscurations, when adventitious delusion that fails to realize this arises, for the defiled mind that imputes self and dharmas, the objects appear as impure, and since the mind apprehends them as such, those who have defilements and obscurations on the side of complete affliction are called impure sentient beings. By entering the paths of the three vehicles taught in order to clear away those adventitious defilements, from the manner of seeing the true nature of dharmatā, which is naturally completely pure, more and more clearly, realizations arise—such as the four fruits of the śrāvakas, the fruit of the pratyekabuddhas, the path of seeing of the bodhisattvas, and the nine bhūmis of the path of meditation—and from the aspect of abandoning the defilements to be abandoned, they are called small, medium, and great purity; and those are called sentient beings pure of whatever defilements. Furthermore, although the nature of objects such as form has primordially never been impure, in accordance with the stages of purifying the adventitious defilements of apprehending them erroneously, just as when an eye defect is cleared away one sees objects more clearly, that subject sees the purity of the object, and through the power of seeing that, the subject is also called pure, but other than that there is no separate way of being pure; thus, although in the mode of appearance it seems that the subject becomes increasingly pure and the object also appears to be newly purified, in the mode of abiding, one "should" "know" "that" seeing that the nature "of" objects such as "form" is primordially "pure" is itself the subject's release from adventitious defilements, the primordial "purity" of the "fruit". Since those two share the same crucial point of primordially abiding in great purity without duality of object and subject, one should know that those two purities are indivisible in essence. Likewise, the purity of the "fruit" and the "purity" of "form" and so forth "become" the "purity" "of" "omniscient" wakefulness "itself", which is completely pure, having thoroughly abandoned the two obscurations, the culmination of the purity of separation from the adventitious; and all those purities share the same root crucial point in the meaning of natural complete purity, just as explained a moment ago. Thus, the ultimate purity, the purity of "omniscience" "itself", "and" the temporary "purity" of attaining the "fruit" of the various paths, and the "purity" of the basic "form" and so forth, the dharmas of the aggregates, elements, and sense sources—although these three are posited as distinct in the mode of appearance, in the mode of abiding they "are like" the "element" "of space", which has no distinctness. They are "indivisible" within the single essence of the naturally completely pure dharmadhātu, and "cannot" be "divided" as distinct even by the mind. For the wakefulness of a buddha, which directly sees the nature of that completely pure dharmatā exactly as it is, all dharmas are seen as fully awakened or as primordially passed beyond suffering by nature; there is nothing impure. And since that wakefulness which realizes this is also the ultimate purity that has permanently abandoned all obscurations along with the latencies of adventitious defilements, to know that just as it sees, all dharmas are unestablished as the nature of dualistic phenomena such as object and subject, but are a single flavor, undifferentiated in being primordially pure and fully awakened in the great non-dual equality—this is the ultimate intent of the perfection of wisdom. And the manner of sustaining the meaning of that genuine condition by naturally resting effortlessly, one should understand in accordance with this statement: "In this there is nothing to be removed; / There is nothing whatsoever to be posited. / Look genuinely at the genuine itself. / Seeing genuinely, one is completely liberated." ^1-152

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-153]]

The function of that wakefulness of meditative equipoise. ^1-153

###### The Function of Perfectly Pure Wisdom ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-154]]

Second, regarding the function: there are the two functions of not abiding in the extreme of peace and not abiding in the extreme of existence. ^1-154

###### The Function of Not Abiding in the Extreme of Peace ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-155]]

First, there is one stanza starting with "hero" and "wherever they act": through abiding in the "perfection" "of" "wisdom", the great primordial complete purity without duality of existence and peace, whatever the occasion and "wherever" the "heroic" bodhisattvas "act", they have "genuinely" "passed beyond" all the afflictions "of the three" "realms", "and" even so, they "do" "not" abide in a one-sided nirvāṇa that is "completely" "liberated" from existence like the śrāvakas; even "though" the "afflictions" that cause one to be born powerlessly in existence "have been cleared away", through skill in means they completely "demonstrate" "birth" in existence, and even "though" they do "not" "age", "fall ill", "and" "die", through the power of compassion they "demonstrate" "passing away" and so forth for the benefit of others; for when one oneself realizes the dharmadhātu that does not abide in existence and peace, it is the nature of things that great compassion—the natural result of realizing the profound dharma—arises for those who cling to the extremes of existence and peace, and boundless skill in means is attained effortlessly. ^1-155

###### The Function of Not Abiding in the Extreme of Existence ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-156]]

Second, there is one stanza starting with "these beings": "These" "beings" who do not possess the wisdom that realizes non-nature are "attached" "to" the difficult-to-cross "mud" "of" the five aggregates "of" "name" "and" "form", and thus "wander" "in" the "wheel" "of" "saṃsāra", "which" "is" "like" the "wheel" "of" "wind" of the sphere propelling the sun and moon that "revolves" again and again; in this way, the twelve links of dependent origination—which have the nature of the three: karma, afflictions, and birth—are connected continuously like a chain, revolving like a firebrand. "Knowing" that "these" "deluded" "beings" are caught in the snare of their own bewildered minds, "just as" "wild animals" are "caught in a snare" with no escape, suffering and difficult to liberate, those who "possess" the "wisdom" that realizes reality are not bound by the snare of delusion and "roam" in this realm of beings, for example, "like" a "bird" "in" the "sky". ^1-156

###### The Domain of That Wisdom of Meditative Equipoise ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-2-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-157]]

The domain of that wakefulness of meditative equipoise. Third, regarding the domain of that wakefulness of meditative equipoise, there are two parts: teaching the domain of non-engagement, and explaining the benefits of engaging in that way through an example. ^1-157

###### Teaching the Domain of Non-Engagement ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-2-3-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-158]]

First, there is one stanza starting with "Completely pure conduct": "whichever" bodhisattva, whose "conduct" is "completely" "pure", "does" "not" "engage" "in" "form" by observing the essence of form and its features such as impermanence, "and" likewise "does" "not" "engage" "in" "consciousness", "perception", "feeling", "and" "formation" either; "engaging" "thus", without engaging in anything, "entirely" "abandons" "all" subtle and gross "attachments" of clinging to reality, "and", "completely" "liberated" "from" all "attachments", "engages" in the "wisdom" "of" the "sugatas". ^1-158

###### Explaining the Benefits of Engaging in That Way through an Example ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-2-3-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-159]]

Second, there is one stanza on the accumulation of engaging in that way: The "wise" "and" "brilliant" "bodhisattva" "engaging" "thus", "having" "severed" all "attachments" of conceited grasping—reading "proceeds", though in some Indian manuscripts it also appears as "liberated"—"proceeds" higher and higher, "without" "attachment" to equality; for example, "just as" the "sun" "radiates clearly" when "freed" "from" the "planet" called "Rāhu", "and" "just as" a "fire" "set" ablaze "burns" all "grass", "wood", "and" "forests", the great wisdom, like light and fire, destroys the darkness and firewood of afflictions. The aspect of that wakefulness of meditative equipoise. ^1-159

###### Teaching the Complete Non-Apprehension of That Wisdom of Meditative Equipoise ^1-3-2-2-1-1-2-4-2-4-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-160]]

Fourth, teaching the aspect of that wakefulness of meditative equipoise as objectless. There is one stanza starting with "all dharmas": Thus, since "all" "dharmas" subsumed by affliction and purification are by their own essence "pure" of the "nature" of afflictions, "and" since the observation of objects of knowledge has become "completely" "pure", if "bodhisattvas" "observe" and abide in the supreme meaning of the "perfection" "of" "wisdom" which realizes the nature of things to be exactly like that, the observer, the "one" "who" "meditates" on the perfection of wisdom, is "not" "observed", and "all" "dharmas" to be observed are also "not" "observed"; abiding in the expanse where proliferations are pacified, "this" "is" "engaging" in the "supreme" "perfection" "of" "wisdom". The approach of the extensive teaching. ^1-160

###### The Door to the Extensive Teaching ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-161]]

Third, regarding the approach of the extensive teaching, there are five parts: the stages of training in the application; the way the result of that application is genuinely established; teaching the person who applies themselves to that; the greatness of the person who enters in that way; and the greatness of the dharma they train in. From among these five, regarding the first: ^1-161

###### The Stages of Training in the Application ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-162]]

The stages of training in the application. There are five parts: the essence of the application to be meditated upon; the benefits arising from meditating on the application; the faults that cause interruptions; the function of the application; and explaining the condition, the spiritual friend. ^1-162

###### The Nature of the Application to be Meditated Upon ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-163]]

The essence of the application to be meditated upon. First, there is one stanza starting with "To the victor": "To" the "victor", the lion of men, "Śakra", the "king" "of" the "gods", "asked": "How" should the "bodhisattvas" who have served the past victors, who have generated the roots of virtue from that, and who are guided by spiritual friends, "exert" themselves in "engaging" in the perfection of "wisdom"? In response to this question, he declared: they "do" "not" "exert" themselves by way of observing or abiding even to the slightest degree in the dharmas subsumed by the "aggregates" and elements, "and" "not" observing, not abiding, and "not" "exerting" themselves in the "aggregates" and so forth, "that" "is" the genuine "exertion" of the "bodhisattvas". The benefits arising from meditating on the application. ^1-163

###### The Qualities Arising from Meditating on the Application ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-164]]

Second, regarding the benefits, there are three parts: the benefit of respect, which is the cause of the application; the benefit of the result arising from the application; and the benefit of the essence of the application on the occasion. ^1-164

###### The Quality of Respect, the Cause of the Application ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-165]]

First, there is one stanza starting with "Whichever [sentient being] ... this dharma": "Whichever" person, "having heard" it explained from this text of the perfection of wisdom that "these" "dharmas" all lack an essence but their aspects are unceasing, "like" an "emanation" "and" an "illusion", has "no" "doubt" about its meaning and, with conviction, "furthermore" earnestly "applies" themselves "to" "training" in it—"that" "sentient being" "should be known" to have "entered" the Mahāyāna "long" "ago" in the past, and "should be known" to "have" exceedingly "served" "many" "tens of millions" and hundreds of billions of "buddhas" in the past by way of honoring and revering them and so forth; for, without relying on such a pre-existing cause, one does not become inclined toward the profound meaning of the perfection of wisdom upon hearing it. ^1-165

###### The Qualities of the Result Arising from the Application ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-166]]

Second, there are two parts: the benefit of not being harmed by interruptions, and the benefit of attaining the three kāyas. ^1-166

###### The Quality of Not Being Harmed by Obstacles ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-167]]

First, there are two stanzas starting with "The path of a wilderness": When a "person" "who" has "entered" "the" "path" "of" a "wilderness" isolated from people, which has "many" "yojanas", "having seen", after walking and walking along that path, an agreeable and "excellent" "forest" right at the "border" of the wilderness and the village where the "cowherds" of the city dwell, thinks this—"they" "think", "'These' 'are' 'signs' of 'nearing' a 'village' 'and' a 'city'", "and" they "find" "relief" from the fears of tigers and so forth that are in the wilderness, "and" from then on they "have" "no" mental "fear" of "robbers". "Likewise", "when", desiring to attain genuine "awakening", they "completely" "seek" its method, "whoever" "attains" the fortune of "hearing" "this" "perfection" "of" "wisdom", the mother "of" "the" "victors", which is the supreme path, "they" "find" "relief" from the suffering of saṃsāra "and" have "no" "fear" of falling into the extremes of existence and peace; they "do" "not" fall "into" "the" "ground" "of" a śrāvaka "arhat", "nor" do they fall "into" "the" "ground" "of" a "pratyekabuddha". ^1-167

###### The Quality of Obtaining the Three Bodies ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-168]]

Second, there are three parts: the benefit of attaining the dharmakāya; the benefit of attaining the saṃbhogakāya; and the benefit of attaining the nirmāṇakāya. ^1-168

###### The Quality of Obtaining the Dharmakāya ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-2-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-169]]

First, there are two stanzas starting with "A person, of the ocean": "When" a "person" "goes" "to" "look" at "the" "water" "of" "the" "ocean", "if" "they" "see" "trees", "and" "forests", and "mountains" in the direction ahead, they know they are "still" "far" from the ocean. "But" "if" "they" "do" "not" "see" "those" "signs" of being far away, such as mountains and so forth, and all the ground gradually becomes even and slopes down, and they do not see the far end, "they" "think", "'Now' 'the' 'great' 'ocean' 'has' 'become' 'near' me", and they "have" "no" "doubt" "about" "that"; for since the great ocean is vastly wide and even without any visible far end, it is impossible for mountains and so forth to exist in its expanse, and therefore they know it "thus", even from afar. "Just as" in that example, when those who have "fully" "entered" into genuine "awakening" attain the fortune of "hearing" "this" "perfection" "of" "wisdom" "of" "the" "victor", they "should" "know" they are near the great ocean of the pristine wisdom of the dharmakāya, which is free from all extremes; for, through this, the marks of conceptualization, like forests and mountains and so forth, are gradually abandoned, and the manifest occurrences of conceptualization are gradually pacified, so it is infallible that they attain pristine wisdom free from extremes and without apprehension. Therefore, even "if" that person who is not separated from this perfection of wisdom "has" "not" "been" "prophesied" "directly" "by" "the" "guides" with the words, "You will awaken", they think, "'I' 'will' 'touch' or attain 'the' 'awakening' 'of' a 'buddha' 'before' 'long'", because of the infallible way of cause and result. ^1-169

###### The Quality of Obtaining the Saṃbhogakāya ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-2-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-170]]

Second, the way of attaining the saṃbhogakāya. There is one stanza starting with "In the fine springtime": "In" "the" "fine" "springtime", "when" the old "leaves" have been replaced and "fallen" to the ground from the "trunks" of trees, the "branches" of the trees "will" "produce" new leaves, "fruits", and "flowers" "before" "long". "Likewise", "whoever" "attains" "this" "perfection" "of" "wisdom" "in" "their" "hands", they "attain" "the" "awakening" "of" "the" "guides" "before" "long"; from the time of attaining the genuine oral instructions, the result of generating the mind of the supreme vehicle is that they will attain the saṃbhogakāya, the form body which is like a wish-fulfilling tree, possessing the cooling shade of thick leaves of compassion, blooming with the flowers of the major and minor marks, and possessing the fruits of benefiting others. ^1-170

###### The Quality of Obtaining the Nirmāṇakāya ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-2-2-2-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-171]]

Third, the way of attaining the nirmāṇakāya. There is one stanza starting with "For example, a woman": "For example", "when" "a" pregnant "woman" who has conceived "experiences" the "suffering" of the pains of giving birth and is "afflicted" because the months in the womb are complete, that state "is" what is "called" "the" "time" "having" "come" "for" "her" "to" "give" "birth". "Likewise", "any" "bodhisattva" who attains the "hearing" of "this" "wisdom" "of" "the" "victor" "and" "generates" a clear mind of "joy" "and" the "desire" to attain it "will" "swiftly" "touch" great "awakening"; for through this path, having nourished the seed of the disposition and completed the accumulations, just as a child is born from a pregnant woman, the nirmāṇakāya "will" "manifest" in actuality in the world. ^1-171

###### The Quality of the Nature of the Temporary Application ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-2-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-172]]

Third, the benefits of the essence. There are two parts: although one does not conceptualize increase, decrease, and so forth, one does not touch the one-sided extreme of nirvāṇa; and although one is free from conceptualization, one accomplishes the benefit of beings. ^1-172

###### Though Not Conceptualizing Increase and Decrease and so forth, Not Touching Upon a One-Sided Nirvāṇa ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-2-3-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-173]]

First, there is one stanza starting with "Perfection of wisdom": "When" properly "practicing" the meaning of the "supreme" "perfection" "of" "wisdom", that "yogi" of the supreme vehicle "does" "not" "see" the "increase"—the arising or the accumulation of a continuity—"and" the "decrease"—the ceasing or the becoming lower than before—"of" "form" and so forth, all the phenomena of complete purification; for in genuine reality, all phenomena are free from increase, decrease, waning, and waxing, and abide in equality. "Likewise", "since" "they" "do" "not" "see", in the manner of manifest clinging, "virtuous" "phenomena" "and" "non-virtuous" "non-phenomena", or even what is called the dharmatā or "dharmadhātu" of phenomena, all apprehensions are completely pacified "and" they abide in non-proliferation. Yet, "that" bodhisattva "does" "not" "touch" a one-sided "nirvāṇa" that is a mere expanse of peace, but "abides" in the equality of "wisdom" that does not abide in the extremes of existence and peace. ^1-173

###### Though Non-Conceptual, the Benefit of Beings Arises ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-2-3-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-174]]

Second, there is one stanza starting with "Whoever practices here": "Whoever" properly "practices" "here" in the perfection of wisdom "does" "not" "conceptualize" even "the" "phenomena" "of" "a" "buddha", such as the powers and so forth, by fully apprehending them as this or that. "Likewise", "they" "do" "not" "conceptualize" the path, such as the five "powers" "and" the four "bases" "of" "miraculous" "power" and so forth, or the result or reality, the "peace" of "awakening". "They" "do" "not" "conceptualize" either the aggregates and elements such as form and so forth. In meaning, this teaches the way of being free from the four marks of conceptualization. As for how this is, if we arrange them in order according to what is taught in the Dhāraṇī of Entering Non-Conceptualization, it teaches freedom from conceptualizing the phenomena of saṃsāra, such as the aggregates and so forth, as having an inherent nature; conceptualizing the path of the perfection of wisdom as an antidote; conceptualizing reality as dharmatā; and conceptualizing the phenomena of a buddha as something to be attained. Thus, although "free" from all "conceptualization", through the power of that non-conceptual, spontaneously present samādhi and the force of aspiration, or by its "blessing", one effortlessly "practices" the taming of māras and all deeds for the benefit of others; for by abiding in this non-conceptual samādhi, one attains the power of being blessed by the buddhas in a non-dual manner through dharmatā, so its awakened activity is never wasted in the slightest. "Such" a way "is" "the" "practice" of the non-conceptual pristine wisdom of the "supreme" "perfection" "of" "wisdom". The faults of interruption. ^1-174

###### Teaching What Constitutes Obstacles to the Application ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-175]]

Third, teaching the interruptions to the application. There are two parts: a brief presentation, and a detailed explanation. ^1-175

###### Brief Explanation ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-3-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-176]]

First, there is one stanza starting with "Moon of speech": To "that" "buddha", the Bhagavān who is renowned as the "moon" "of" "speech" because he dispels the darkness of ignorance and the torment of the afflictions of those to be tamed by emitting rays of light of speech from the face of his full maṇḍala of major and minor marks, the elder "Subhūti" "asked": "What" "are" "the" "interruptions" to the application "of" "bodhisattvas" "who" "delight" "in" the "qualities" of the Mahāyāna? When he asked this, the "Teacher" "spoke": "The" things that "will" "interrupt" "are" "many"; "but" "out" "of" "those", "if" "I" "fully" "explain" "here" "for" "a" "while" "just" "a" "few". ^1-176

###### Extensive Explanation ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-3-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-177]]

Second, there are three parts: the actual interruptions, their reasons, and teaching the causes of being harmed or not harmed by interruptions. ^1-177

###### The Actual ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-3-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-178]]

First, there are four parts: the degeneration of the application itself which is the cause; the degeneration from the Mahāyāna path; lacking the condition of the gathering of teaching and hearing; and teaching other faults illustrated by these. ^1-178

###### Degeneration of the Causal Application Itself ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-3-2-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-179]]

First, there are two parts: degeneration due to the intellect being unworkable, and degeneration due to doubting the Mother. ^1-179

###### Degeneration Due to an Unworkable Intellect ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-3-2-1-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-180]]

First, there is one stanza starting with "Wisdom of the victor": Illustrated by writing, "when" "writing" "in" "letters" "this" very "perfection" "of" "wisdom" "of" "the" "victor", and when retaining, reading, reciting, teaching, and hearing it, and so forth, "various" "flashes of inspiration" conceptualizing the objects of the three times, such as form and so forth, and "many" "forms" of agitation, dullness, sleep, regret, attachment, aversion, doubt, and so forth, "will" "arise". "And", "having" "not" "accomplished" the "benefit" "of" oneself and other "beings" through that application, interrupting one's diligence "swiftly" "just" "like" "a" "flash" "of" "lightning", one "will" "completely" "degenerate" from the application; "this" should be known as the "work" "of" "māra". ^1-180

###### Degeneration Due to Doubting the Mother ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-3-2-1-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-181]]

Second, there is one stanza starting with "When this is explained": "When" "this" Dharma "is" "explained", "some" "will" "have" "doubts" about it. Thinking, "The" "Guide" "has" "not" "fully" "declared" "even" "my" "name" "here", "and" "he" "has" "not" "fully" "declared" my "caste", "the" "place" where I live, "and" my "clan"", grasping at reasons to turn away from the Mother, such as thinking, "Since I was not clearly prophesied in this way, I do not see the reason why I will definitely accomplish awakening," through those doubts "they" "will" "not" "hear" "this" profound Dharma "and" "will" "abandon" it; this is the "work" "of" "māra". ^1-181

###### Degeneration from the Mahāyāna ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-3-2-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-182]]

Second, there are three parts: teaching with an example the abandoning of the Mahāyāna, which is the cause of omniscience; teaching with an example the seeking of the lower path; and teaching with an example the seeking of great awakening from the lower vehicle. ^1-182

###### Teaching through an Example the Abandonment of the Mahāyāna, the Cause of Omniscience ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-3-2-1-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-183]]

First, there are two lines starting with "Not knowing thus": "Not" "knowing" "thus" that the perfection of wisdom is the sole basis of all phenomena, "these" foolish ones, "having" "abandoned" this perfection of wisdom which is like the "root" of all the phenomena of a genuine buddha, "foolishly" "will" "seek" other phenomena like "branches" "and" "leaves"; this is also the work of māra. ^1-183

###### Teaching through an Example the Seeking of the Inferior Path ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-3-2-1-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-184]]

Second, there are two lines starting with "Having found the elephant": "Just" "as" one seeking an "elephant", "having" actually "found" the elephant, leaves it and "again" "seeks" "the" "footprint" "of" the "elephant", so it should be known by this example that someone who, having "heard" the "perfection" "of" "wisdom" which explicitly teaches the profound supreme Dharma, does not earnestly seek it, but seeks the meaning of entering the perfection of wisdom indirectly through other "sūtras"; "likewise", this is also the work of māra. ^1-184

###### Teaching through an Example the Seeking of Great Awakening from the Inferior Vehicle ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-3-2-1-2-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-185]]

Third, there is one stanza starting with "For example, someone": "For" "example", "someone", "having" "found" "food" "with" "a" "hundred" "flavours", without tasting its flavour, "even" "having" "found" the "supreme" "food", "again" "seeks" "bad" "food". "Likewise" with the above "example", a beginner "bodhisattva", "having" "found" "this" supreme Dharma of the "perfection", without practicing its meaning correctly, "seeks" ultimate "awakening" through the scriptures that teach the path of the "level" or state of an "arhat"; this is also the work of māra. ^1-185

###### Lacking an Assembly for Teaching and Listening ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-3-2-1-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-186]]

Third, regarding lacking the condition of the gathering of teaching and hearing, there are two parts: depending on oneself and depending on others. ^1-186

###### Relying on Oneself ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-3-2-1-3-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-187]]

First, there is one stanza starting with "Desiring respect": "Desiring" one's own fame and "respect" from others, "and" also "desiring" to taste the flavour of "gain" such as food and wealth, "and" "having" "a" "mind" that desires to "look" at outer objects, one associates and becomes "familiar" with "homes" in the town; "and" "having" "abandoned" writing, hearing, and reflecting on this profound "Dharma" through various distracting activities like those, one "will" "engage" in "actions" "not" in accordance with the "Dharma", whichever basis of the wrong path it may be. "Having" "abandoned" the genuine "path" and strayed into going on a "non-path", "this" is the "work" "of" "māra". ^1-187

###### Relying on Others ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-3-2-1-3-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-188]]

Second, there is one stanza starting with "Whoever at that": When "those" hearers, "at" "that" "time" when the teacher is distracted by gain and reverence and so forth, "having" "become" "eager" "and" "faithful" toward the Mother, "go" "to" "hear" "this" "excellent" "Dharma", "those" hearers, "knowing" that the "preacher" "of" "Dharma" is attached to bad "actions" such as delighting in gain and reverence, "have" "no" "joy" in hearing the Dharma from that preacher "and", thinking, "Now I will not request the Dharma," "become" "unhappy" "and" "go" back; this is also the work of māra, acting as an obstacle to hearing the perfection of wisdom and so forth. ^1-188

###### Teaching Other Faults Illustrated by That ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-3-2-1-4-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-189]]

Fourth, there is one stanza starting with "At that time": "At" "that" "time" when hearing this profound Dharma and so forth, "this" "work" "of" "māra", which creates obstacles to the genuine application such as lacking the condition of the gathering of teaching and hearing, "will" "arise". What is this? "At" "that" "time" of hearing the profound Dharma and so forth, "those" sinful māras whose minds are hostile toward the Dharma, who are as if pierced by a thorn, "disturb" the minds of "many" "monks" who hold the sūtras, "and" through various means of causing obstacles, "will" also "produce" "many" "other" "forms" of "phenomena" "that" "obstruct" one from "retaining" "and" reflecting on "this" profound "perfection" "of" "wisdom" properly, which are not explained here. In short, whatever comes to the side of harming the retention and so forth of this profound Dharma, it should all be known as the work of māra, because acting as an obstacle is exactly what is designated as the work of māra. As it is said in the *Gaganagañjaparipṛcchā-sūtra*: "Son of noble family, as much as one engages in non-virtuous phenomena and abandons virtuous phenomena, all that is the work of māra." ^1-189

###### The Reason They Arise from Māra ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-3-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-190]]

Second, the reason for the occurrence of the work of māra. There is one stanza starting with "Jewels of price": "Just as" "whatever" "jewels" "of incalculable price" "are found", "they" are "rare" because they are not found at all times, and at the "times" they are found and possessed, "always" "harm" "is frequent" "to them" from covetous others, enemies, and so forth; "likewise", regarding "this" "supreme", "precious" "Dharma" of the "perfection of wisdom" "of the victors", it is also very "rare" to occur, and because it is difficult to be mastered by the fortune of those with perfect merit, "always" "harm" "is frequent". ^1-190

###### Teaching the Cause of Being Harmed or Unharmed by Obstacles ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-3-2-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-191]]

Third, regarding this, there are two parts: teaching the object to which those obstacles occur, and the manner in which the buddhas protect from those obstacles. ^1-191

###### Teaching the Objects for Whom Those Obstacles Arise ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-3-2-3-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-192]]

First, there are three lines starting with "Newly in the vehicle": "Whoever" are "beings" "of small intellect" "newly" "entered" "into" the "vehicle", who have "not" "found" before, i.e., not brought into their experience, "this" "like" very "rare" and "profound" precious "Dharma" which causes one to transcend the domain of māra, "in" "order" "to" "create" "obstacles" "to" "them", "māra" "will" "be" "joyful", having seen that he has found an opportunity. ^1-192

###### The Way Buddhas Protect from Those Obstacles ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-3-2-3-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-193]]

Second, there are two parts: the actual meaning and teaching it through an example. ^1-193

###### The Actual Meaning ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-3-2-3-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-194]]

First, there is one line starting with "Buddhas of the ten directions": If one thinks: "Well then, everyone who initially practices the Mother is a beginner, and if that many obstacles of māra occur to them, it would be difficult for bodhisattvas to complete the meditation on the perfection of wisdom," just as some weak persons who have found a jewel are protected by the king of the country, the "buddhas" dwelling in the worldly realms "of the ten" "directions", all those who possess unobstructed vision of wisdom, make effort or "apply" themselves "to" "completely" "uphold" "them", those who are initially practicing this Dharma and have not found the realization of transcending the domain of māra, from the abyss of obstacles. However, one should know that depending on the cause of that person's previous merit and the condition of their present faith and so forth, from whether or not they have become an object for the entry of the buddhas' blessings, whether or not they are harmed by obstacles will occur in various ways. ^1-194

###### Teaching That Through an Example ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-3-2-3-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-195]]

Second, there is one stanza starting with "A mother who is ill": The reason why the buddhas protect is also, for example, for "a" "mother" "who" "has become" "ill", "having" "many" "sons", "all" of them are "unhappy" "and" "perform" "service" "to" that mother; "likewise", "the" "buddhas" "of the" "worldly" "realms" "of the ten" "directions" "also" "keep" their "attention" "upon" "this" "excellent" "wisdom", which has become the "mother" who gave birth to each of them. The Function of Application. ^1-195

###### Explaining the Function of the Application ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-4-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-196]]

Fourth, regarding the explanation of the function of application, there are two parts: performing the action of giving birth to her own sons, and performing the action of joining her own sons to qualities. ^1-196

###### Performing the Action of Generating Her Own Children ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-4-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-197]]

Performing the action of giving birth to her own sons. First, there are two lines starting with "Whatever past and": If one asks, as just explained, since this is called the Mother of the Victors, how does it become a mother? In the world, one is posited as a mother through two ways: a mother giving birth to a son, and having given birth, establishing him in his tasks; here also, "whatever" "past" "and" present buddhas, the transcendent conquerors, who are the "protectors" "of" "the" "world" residing in all the fields of the "ten" "directions", "as many as there are", "and" however many buddhas "will" "appear" "in" "the" "future" "time", since "they" all "arise" "from" "this" very perfection of wisdom, it has become the mother of all the victors of the three times. Performing the action of joining her own sons to qualities. ^1-197

###### Performing the Action of Applying Her Own Children to Qualities ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-4-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-198]]

Second, there are three parts: joining to the knowledge of the mode of the basis, saṃsāra and nirvāṇa; joining to the deeds of the fruition, omniscience; and joining to the method of blocking the two extremes of the path. ^1-198

###### Applying to the Knowledge of the Way of the Ground, Saṃsāra and Nirvāṇa ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-4-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-199]]

Joining to the knowledge of the mode of the basis, saṃsāra and nirvāṇa. First, there are two parts: relying upon the perfection of wisdom, being skilled in the two truths of the world, and attaining the name through realizing suchness. ^1-199

###### Being Learned in the Two Truths of the World in Reliance on the Perfection of Wisdom ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-4-2-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-200]]

First, there are one and a half stanzas starting with "Shows the world": "The world" refers to all the phenomena renowned as the aggregates, elements, and sense sources, as many as there are, and whatever the nature of their conventional cause, effect, and essence may be, the perfection of wisdom "shows" them to her own sons, the buddhas; because relying upon this perfection of wisdom, the "mother" "who gives birth" "to the" "victors", those buddhas see those phenomena as if placing a fresh myrobalan fruit in the palm of their hand. Furthermore, making the purpose specific for performing the benefit of those to be tamed, even the boundless "behavior" "of the minds" "of others", "sentient" "beings" of limitless realms, as many as there are—such as minds drawn inward, scattered outward, with attachment, free from attachment, and so forth without end—she "shows" to her own sons by the power of their familiarity with the perfection of wisdom; this is showing the relative truth. The "suchness" "of" the "world", the five aggregates, "and" the "suchness" "of arhats", and the "suchness" of "pratyekabuddhas" "and" the "suchness" of the "victors' sons", the bodhisattvas, are "one" because they are not established as different; since they are all "free" from "substantial entities", they are "not" existent as other and "other", thus they are called "suchness". The "perfection of wisdom", which is the abiding mode of all such phenomena of thorough affliction and purification, this suchness, the "tathāgatas" "know" and comprehend it just as it is. Thus, with these one and a half stanzas, it is said that, in short, relying upon the perfection of wisdom, all the phenomenal modes of the two truths have come to be seen by the buddhas. ^1-200

###### Attaining the Name Through Realizing Suchness ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-4-2-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-201]]

Second, there is one stanza starting with "The wise [...] the world": Just as in the world a mother gives a name to her son, attaining the name "tathāgata" in reliance upon the Mother of the Victors is as follows: Whether the buddhas, "wise" in reality, "abide" living in this "world" realm "or" pass into "nirvāṇa", this "flawless" "dharmatā", which is expressed as the "emptiness" "of phenomena", "abides" like space, never changing and incapable of being disturbed by anyone; "bodhisattvas" mentally "realize" unmistakably the mode of being of "this" "suchness" which abides naturally from the beginning, and when eventually their realization reaches perfection, "because" they have gone or realized in accordance with suchness, the "buddhas" have been "bestowed with the name" "tathāgata". Joining to the deeds of the fruition, omniscience. ^1-201

###### Applying to the Activity of the Result, Omniscience ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-4-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-202]]

Second, regarding the method of joining to the fruition, omniscience, there are two parts: accomplishing the two benefits in reliance upon the perfection of wisdom, and in particular, the method of teaching the Dharma for the benefit of others. ^1-202

###### Accomplishing the Two Aims in Reliance on the Perfection of Wisdom ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-4-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-203]]

First, there is one stanza starting with "Perfection of wisdom": Since limitless undefiled "joy" arises if one abides in the "perfection of wisdom", it is like the joyful "grove" of the gods; "relying upon" and "abiding" in it, the "domain of experience" of the "guides", the "buddhas" who possess the "ten powers", "is like this": If one asks what that is, even though they "completely extract" limitless "sentient beings" "from" various "suffering" and the "three lower realms", for "those" buddhas there "never" "arises" the "concept" of "sentient beings", because with great objectless compassion they perform the teaching of Dharma and so forth through constant, all-pervasive, and spontaneously accomplished enlightened activity. ^1-203

###### In Particular, the Way of Teaching the Dharma for the Benefit of Others ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-4-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-204]]

Second, there are two parts: vanquishing what is not the path, and the method of showing the authentic path. ^1-204

###### Subduing What is Not the Path ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-4-2-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-205]]

First, there is one stanza starting with "For example, a lion": "For example", the king of beasts, the "lion", "relying on" the snow "mountain" "gorges", abides "without fear" of anyone else; he "frightens" "many" "lesser" "beasts" "and" "roars" his roar. "Similarly", the "lion" "of men", the Buddha, "relying on" the meaning of profound emptiness, the "perfection of wisdom", "frightens" "many" "tīrthikas" who hold the view of a self, and also "roars" the sound of the true Dharma in the "world". ^1-205

###### The Way of Teaching the Authentic Path ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-4-2-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-206]]

Second, there is one stanza starting with "For example, in space": "For example", the "light rays" "of the sun" "abiding" in "space" "dry up" the dampness on "this" "earth" "and" "clearly" "show" "forms"; "likewise", the "king" "of Dharma", the sun of Dharma, the Bhagavan Buddha, and "those" who "rely on" the "perfection of wisdom" which is like the expanse of space, "dry up" the "river" "of craving" for "existence" "and" also authentically and "clearly" "show" the "phenomena" of objects of knowledge. Joining to the method of blocking the two extremes of the path. ^1-206

###### Applying to the Way of Stopping the Two Extremes of the Path ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-4-2-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-207]]

Third, there are two parts: the essence of the path and its function. ^1-207

###### The Nature of the Path ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-4-2-3-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-208]]

First, of the two, the meaning and the example: ^1-208

###### Meaning ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-4-2-3-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-209]]

First, there is one stanza starting with "Not seeing forms": Because "forms" lack their own essence, they are objectless or "not" "seen". Likewise, "feelings" "too" are "not" "seen", "and" "discriminations" are "not" "seen", "and" "mental formations" are "not" "seen"; in the domain of experience resting in equipoise on reality, "wherever" one looks, there is "nothing" "seen" that is the basis for what is called "consciousness", "and" what is called "mind", and what is called "mentality". Regarding this, it is explained in other commentaries that they are expressed in this way from the perspective of mind engaging the past, consciousness engaging the future, and mentality functioning in the present, or they are expressed as synonyms; whereas Jñānapāda states that, as is widely renowned, mind is the all-ground consciousness, mentality is the afflicted mentality, and consciousness refers to the six engaging consciousnesses. Thus, since all phenomena, as represented by the five aggregates, are objectless, not seeing them as the essence of anything whatsoever—"this" "is" "seeing" the genuine nature "of phenomena", "taught" the "Tathāgata", just as flawless eyes not seeing floaters is seeing the nature of floaters. ^1-209

###### Example ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-4-2-3-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-210]]

Second, there is one stanza starting with "Seeing space": What is the example for that seeing of what is not seen? In the world, "beings" may indeed think in that way and "verbally" "express" it "in words", saying, "I" "see" "space". However, how can one say "how" is "space" "seen"? Since in space there is no form of shape or colour whatsoever to be seen that becomes an object of the eye, "examine" for "yourselves" "what" is "this" "meaning" that is thought and expressed in that way! By doing so, you will understand that the mere non-seeing of any corporeal phenomenon is designated as seeing space. "Like" "that" example, what is called "seeing" the reality "of phenomena" is also "taught" by the "Tathāgata" as seeing phenomena when, having known with the eye of wisdom that no phenomenon whatsoever is established, one does not observe or see them as the essence of anything whatsoever. In the world, whatever is called "seeing" is all designated in terms of whatever subject sees whatever object, whereas this seeing that does not observe any extreme and does not see any feature of conceptual proliferation is only partially taught by the example of seeing space; but authentically, regarding the "seeing" by individual self-aware wisdom of the dharmadhātu which transcends all extremes such as existence and non-existence, being and non-being, "it is not" "possible" to fully "illustrate" it "by" "another" "example", because it is inconceivable and therefore authentically transcends being measured and enumerated by examples, logical reasons, and so forth. ^1-210

###### The Function of That Path ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-4-2-3-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-211]]

Second, regarding the function of that path, there are two parts: gathering all the qualities of the path and fruit, and the method of keeping at a distance from a mistaken path. ^1-211

###### Gathering All the Qualities of the Path and Result ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-4-2-3-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-212]]

First, there are two parts: performing all activities through the example of a minister, and gathering all qualities through the example of a king. ^1-212

###### Performing All Activities through the Example of a Minister ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-4-2-3-2-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-213]]

First, there is one stanza starting with "Whoever, like that": "Whoever" among bodhisattvas does not see any observable feature whatsoever, by "that" "seeing" "like" "that" with the eye of wisdom, "all" "phenomena" are "seen", and acquiring great activity from that is "like" what? "Like" a "king" "sitting" in "equanimity" without effort "while" his excellent "minister" "does" "all" the tasks of the king, "all" "those" "phenomena", "however" "many", of the "Buddha's" "activities" "and" the qualities of the "śrāvakas" are "done" in reliance on the non-conceptual "perfection" "of" "wisdom"; because if one possesses the wisdom that realizes selflessness, all paths of liberation are accomplished easily, whereas otherwise there is no method for accomplishing them. ^1-213

###### Performing All Activities through the Example of a King ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-4-2-3-2-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-214]]

Second, there is one stanza starting with "A king to villages": Just "as" a great "king" "does" "not" "go" "to" "villages" and "does" "not" "go" to other great "provinces", "but" "staying" there in "his" "own" "place", the palace, "gathers" "all" the "assemblies" of taxes and so forth; "likewise", the realization of profound dharmatā in the mindstream "of" a "bodhisattva" is unwavering "and" "does" "not" "go" "anywhere" to another place, yet by the power of dharmatā "all" the "qualities" "of" the "Buddha" "ground", "whatever" they "may be", are "gathered" in that realizing mindstream—which is a wondrous activity. ^1-214

###### The Way of Distancing from Mistaken Paths ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-4-2-3-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-215]]

Second, there are two parts: the teaching and the explanation. ^1-215

###### Brief Explanation ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-4-2-3-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-216]]

First, there is one stanza starting with "Whoever [...] Sugatas": "Whoever" among persons "has" "firm" and irreversible "faith" of belief and longing toward the "Sugatas" and their sons, the "bodhisattvas", "and" who "intends" upon—seeking out through intention—and "endeavours" through "application" in the "supreme" "perfection" "of" "wisdom", which is the path to become a bodhisattva and a Buddha, is replete with both method and wisdom; therefore, "having" "thoroughly" "passed" "beyond" the mistaken paths of the "two" "grounds" "of" "śrāvakas" "and" "pratyekabuddhas", they "will" "swiftly" "attain" the holy "enlightenment" "of" a "Jina", which is "not" "overcome" by the obscurations of existence and peace. Thus, faith in the supreme vehicle is the initial door of entry, and wisdom ensures that one does not decline from attaining the fruit in accordance with faith; hence, these two, method and wisdom, are primarily taught. Although beginners may have faith, if they lack the perfection of wisdom, it is possible for them to decline from the path; however, once the intent of the profound perfection of wisdom has been assimilated into their experience, they attain unassailable faith, and it is impossible to decline from the path. ^1-216

###### Detailed Explanation ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-4-2-3-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-217]]

Second, regarding the detailed explanation of that method, for each of the four taught through the examples of sinking in water, an unbaked pot, launching a ship, and an aged person, both the example and the meaning are taught. ^1-217

###### Sinking in Water ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-4-2-3-2-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-218]]

First, there are three stanzas starting with "For example, in the ocean": "For example," when the "ship" of people "entering" the "ocean" is "broken" and "becomes" destroyed, there "whoever" among "people" "does" "not" "hold" "a corpse", "grass", "or" "wood", but remains as they are, "will" "thus" "perish" "in" the "middle" "of" the "water", meaning they will die, and "will" "not" "reach" the "shore" of the ocean; while "whoever" "holds" wood and so forth, in reliance on that, "will" "reach" the "far shore" of the "ocean". "Likewise", "whoever" "possesses" "faith" seeking perfect enlightenment and has "attained" inspiring "pure trust", but "does" "not" "follow" "after" the "perfection" "of" "wisdom", the "Mother" of the Jinas, which is the path to that perfect enlightenment, does "not" touch the fruit, the enlightenment of a Sugata. Thus, "whoever" "has" "constantly" "wandered" "in" that great "ocean" "of" "saṃsāra", "which" is "agitated" "by" the "waves" of "birth", "aging", "death", "and" various "sorrows", "if" they "become" "completely" "held" "by" the "supreme" perfection of "wisdom", which realizes lack of intrinsic nature, they become "skilled" in the unerring "nature" "of" "things" "and" "will" "teach" "the ultimate". In that way, "if" "those" "qualities" "of" "method"—such as the faith of seeking perfect enlightenment of "those" individuals—"become" "completely" "held" by the non-apprehending "wisdom", they will "swiftly" "touch" the "supremely" "wondrous" "enlightenment" "of" a "Sugata"—just as, for example, those people who hold on to wood and so forth reach the far shore of the ocean. ^1-218

###### A New Vase ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-4-2-3-2-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-219]]
