Second, there are two stanzas starting with "For example, someone": "For example, if" "someone" causes "water" to be "carried" in "clay" that is "unbaked", one "should" "know" that the pot "will" "swiftly" "perish", because its aspect of hardness is weak. "If" "water" is caused to be "carried" "in" a "baked" "pot", there is "no" "fear" of anticipating that the "pot" will "perish" "on" the "path" wherever one goes, "and" it "will" "safely" "reach" one's own "home" once more; "likewise", "any" "bodhisattva" who, "even" "though" their inspiring and longing "faith" toward perfect enlightenment "is" "abundant" or great, "if" they "completely" "decline" "from" the path that accomplishes it, the perfection of "wisdom", "will" "swiftly" become "unfit", meaning they will fall into an inferior path and so forth, and decline from the path of the Great Vehicle. "If" the mindstream of that person "becomes" "completely" "held" "by" "faith" and "wisdom", that is, by the perfection of wisdom accompanied by faith, they will "thoroughly" "pass" "beyond" the "two" "grounds" of śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas "and" "will" "attain" "supreme" "enlightenment". ^1-219

###### Building a Boat ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-4-2-3-2-2-2-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-220]]

Third, there are two stanzas starting with "For example, well-constructed": "For example", a "ship" "that" has "not" "been" "well" "constructed" regarding materials, dimensions, joints, and so forth, "will" be destroyed or ruined "in" the "ocean", "together" "with" the "wealth" "and" "together" with the "merchants"; whereas "if" "that" "ship" "is" well examined and "well" "constructed", that ship "will" "not" "perish" or decline, but "together" "with" the merchants "and" "wealth", "will" "reach" the "shore" at the edge of the ocean. "Likewise", "if" "any" "bodhisattva", "even" "though" their mindstream is "completely" imbued "with" "faith", "does" "not" rely on the perfection of "wisdom", they "will" "swiftly" decline "from" the qualities of supreme "enlightenment". "If" "that" very one who has faith "is" endowed with the "supreme" "perfection" "of" "wisdom", they are "unharmed" by the faults of an inferior path, and without declining "from" the path of the supreme vehicle, they "will" "touch" the "enlightenment" "of" a "Jina". ^1-220

###### Explanation through the Example of an Old Man ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-4-2-3-2-2-2-4-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-221]]

Fourth, there are two stanzas starting with "One hundred and twenty years": "For example", a "person" who has "reached" "one" "hundred" "and" "twenty" "years" of age and, having reached the limit of their lifespan, "becomes" "aged" "and" "afflicted", "even" "if" they have "risen" from their seat, "are" "not" "able" to "go" to another place by their "own" power, because they are aged and decrepit. "If" "that" person "is" "supported" "from" "both" "right" "and" "left" "by" "people" endowed with strength, there is "no" "fear" "of" "falling" on the path, "and" they "will" "easily" "go" wherever they wish; "likewise", "any" "bodhisattva" whose "wisdom" that realizes suchness "has" "little" "strength", "even" "though" they "have" "thoroughly" "entered" the path of supreme enlightenment, "will" "thoroughly" "decline" "before" attaining the fruit. But "if" the mindstream of "that" very person "becomes" "completely" "held" "by" "method", such as faith, "and" "by" the "supreme" "wisdom" that realizes selflessness, they "will" "touch" the "enlightenment" "of" a "Tathāgata" without "declining" "from" the path. ^1-221

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-222]]

Explaining the condition: the spiritual friend. ^1-222

###### Relying on a Spiritual Friend as the Condition ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-5-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-223]]

Fifth, regarding the condition, relying on a spiritual friend, there are two parts: relying on the spiritual friend who is to be relied upon, and explaining the disciple who relies. ^1-223

###### Relying on the Spiritual Friend to be Relied Upon ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-5-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-224]]

Regarding relying on the spiritual friend who is to be relied upon, first, there are two parts: how one must rely on a spiritual friend endowed with the defining characteristics, and how that spiritual friend teaches instructions. ^1-224

###### The Way One Must Rely on a Qualified Spiritual Friend ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-5-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-225]]

First, there are two parts: the actual topic and the reason why one must rely in that way. ^1-225

###### Actual ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-5-1-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-226]]

First, there is one stanza starting with "bodhisattva": Thus, "any" "bodhisattva" who "abides" "on" the "stage" "of" a "beginner" (i.e. a beginner) and who, "with" "superior" "resolve" focused exceptionally on the benefit of others and perfect enlightenment, has "entered" by seeking the path and fruit of the "supreme" "enlightenment" "of" a "Buddha"—"those" "good" "disciples" endowed "with" "respect" "for" the "guru" "should" "always" "rely" "on" "wise" "gurus" whose view and conduct are good and who are able to teach the intended meaning of the Mahāyāna. ^1-226

###### The Reason One Must Rely in That Way ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-5-1-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-227]]

Second, there is one stanza starting with "If [you ask] why": Regarding the instruction that beginners who have entered the Mahāyāna should earnestly rely on a holy spiritual friend, "if" [you ask] "why" "that" is, it is because all "qualities" of being "learned" in the path and fruit of the Mahāyāna "arise" "from" "that" spiritual friend, since "those" spiritual friends "teach" this "perfection" "of" "wisdom", the path of the Mahāyāna. Therefore, the "Jina", the perfect Buddha who "possesses" the "supreme" "of" "all" "qualities", "spoke" "these" "words": "The attainment of the "dharmas" "of" a "Buddha" "is" "dependent" "on" a "spiritual" "friend"." This is because meditating on the path of skill in means, reflecting, and hearing each depend respectively on the latter, and that hearing also depends on a spiritual friend. ^1-227

###### The Way That Spiritual Friend Teaches the Instructions ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-5-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-228]]

Second, regarding how instructions are taught, there are two parts: how the instructions are taught and stating the praise of teaching in that way. ^1-228

###### How the Instructions are Taught ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-5-1-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-229]]

First, there is one stanza starting with "Giving and moral discipline": Thus, when imparting instructions to someone who has newly entered the vehicle, one must teach a path where method and wisdom are inseparable, in accordance with the level of their mindstream: "You should engage in the six perfections that are the path of the Jinas—'giving', 'and' 'moral' 'discipline', 'patience', 'and' 'likewise' 'diligence', 'and' 'concentration', 'wisdom'—complete with the three [stages of] preparation, main practice, and conclusion, and with the mental engagement that desires omniscience, 'completely' 'dedicate' all those roots of virtue 'to' ultimate, great 'enlightenment'." Instructing in this way is the instruction on the side of method. [Additionally:] "Having 'clung' 'to' the 'entities' of the 'aggregates', such as form, 'do' 'not' 'apprehend' what is called 'enlightenment', the ultimate attainment you desire, as 'supreme' or with excessive attachment." If you ask why, it is because all dharmas, exemplified by enlightenment, are without inherent nature, and instructing them to understand that is the instruction on the side of wisdom. "In" "that" "manner", "one" "should" "teach" "thus" "to" "that" "beginner" who aspires to enlightenment. ^1-229

###### Expressing Praise for Teaching in That Way ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-5-1-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-230]]

Second, there is one stanza starting with "Acting in that way": "Those" "moons" "of" "speech", who are the spiritual friends of the Mahāyāna "acting" "in" "that" "way", that is, who "well" teach the instructions, and who have become a great "ocean" of good qualities that are difficult to realize, endowed with the light rays of the genuine dharma, "are" a "refuge" who dispel the suffering "of" "beings" by teaching the dharma that is the cause of benefit and happiness, "and" an "ally" who establishes them in the happiness of nirvāṇa, "and" a "haven" who dispels the causes of suffering. Likewise, they are a "support" for going to enlightenment in this way, "and" an "intellect" that knows what is beneficial and what is not beneficial, "and" an "island" for those sinking in the river of craving for existence—"island" referring to a suitable resting place in the middle of water, the word dvīpa being the name for what exists between two waters. By teaching the dharmatā of non-arising, they are like a captain and so on who "desires" "benefit", or accomplishes benefit, "completely" "leading" one out of the mud of saṃsāra, and a "clarifier" by teaching the selflessness of persons, and a great "lamp" "of" "dharma" by teaching the selflessness of dharmas, and "undisturbed" or fearless in "speaking" "of" the "supreme" "of" "dharmas", emptiness, by means of perfect specific discrimination; because they themselves have deep-rooted trust in the profound dharma, their confidence to be completely victorious in teaching its path and fruit is fully perfected. Alternatively, it is also acceptable to explain these individually: "completely leading" means entering the path by guiding them like a captain and so forth, and "desiring benefit" means spontaneously accomplishing the benefit of others out of compassion, and "speaking of the supreme dharma" refers to that of suchness, and they are not overwhelmed or "undisturbed" by opponents such as māras and tīrthikas. Explaining the relying disciple. ^1-230

###### Explanation of the Student Who Relies ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-5-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-231]]

Second, regarding the explanation of the disciple who listens to profound wisdom, there are two parts: teaching the disciple endowed with trust in the profound, and teaching that such trust is difficult to find. ^1-231

###### Explanation of the Student Endowed with Conviction in the Profound ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-5-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-232]]

First, there are two parts: the manner in which their mind is profound, and teaching the qualities of that profundity. ^1-232

###### The Way the Mind is Profound ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-5-2-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-233]]

First, there is one stanza starting with "Those endowed with great renown": Whoever has entered the Mahāyāna, the path that is supremely profound and vast, "those" who are "endowed" with "great" "renown" of the name 'bodhisattva' in the infinite fields of the Jinas "don" the "armor" of what is "difficult" "to" "do". Thus, they generate the mind, saying, "I shall cause all infinite sentient beings, equal to space in conventional terms, to transcend sorrow into the expanse of non-abiding nirvāṇa", and furthermore, they are free from apprehending affliction and purification, so thus they "do" "not" don the "armor" that apprehends as entities the "aggregates", "and" "elements", "and" "sense" "sources", and they are "free" from the "perception" of clinging to the "three" "vehicles", "and" they abide in the perfection of wisdom "without" any dharmas whatsoever being "completely" apprehended or "grasped". Exactly because of that, since they trust in the profound meaning, they "do" "not" "turn" "back" from attaining the fruit of perfect enlightenment endowed with the three great purposes, "and" they are "unwavering" elsewhere from the method of the Mahāyāna path, and since their mindstream cannot be afflicted by any adverse condition, māra or opponent, they "are" "of" the "nature" of being "undisturbed". This is as it is spoken in the extensive sutra: "Bhagavān, those bodhisattvas who don the armor to cause all sentient beings to transcend sorrow, yet who do not apprehend sentient beings, are doing what is difficult," and "Subhūti, that armor is not connected to form," and so forth. ^1-233

###### Explanation of the Qualities of That Profundity ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-5-2-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-234]]

Second, there is one stanza starting with "Those are endowed with such": In that way, "those" bodhisattvas "are" "endowed" "with" "such" "dharmas" that are profound and difficult to fathom, and by the power of that, they are "without" the "fabrications" of any apprehensions, and are "free" from "doubt", "hesitation", and "two-mindedness" concerning the profound meaning, "and" are "endowed" "with" the "meaning" of trust. Here, hesitation and so forth are just synonyms for doubt, teaching that they do not have any coarse or subtle doubts, such as those that are actively operating; alternatively, they can be viewed as distinct isolates, such as having no two-pointed mind regarding the path and fruit in the manner of "doubt" regarding the present, "hesitation" regarding the past, and "two-mindedness" regarding the future. By means of that trust free from doubt, having "heard" the profound "perfection" "of" "wisdom", their mind "does" "not" "shrink" "back" from its meaning, and since they have brought the profound dharma under their own experience through their own intellect, they "do" "not" "rely" "on" the "kindness" "of" "others", meaning that it is not the case that they lack certainty, relying on others and entering wherever they are led; and because they have become like that, one "should" "know" that they "do" "not" "turn" "back" from the path and fruit of the supreme vehicle. ^1-234

###### Explanation That Such Conviction is Difficult ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-5-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-235]]

Second, regarding the way that profound nature is difficult to realize, there are two parts: teaching that the dharma to be practiced is profound, and the "reason" it is profound in that way. ^1-235

###### Explanation That the Dharma to be Practiced is Profound ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-5-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-236]]

First, there is one stanza starting with "Of the guides": "This" "dharma" of the perfect Buddhas, the "guides", the Great Mother, is supremely "profound"; all foundational dharmas are non-arising and, being mere suchness, cannot be apprehended, and so because they have primordially transcended the domain of the grasped and the grasping mind, they are "difficult" "to" "see". Therefore, during the phase of the path it is "not" "realized" "by" "anyone", "and" at the time of the fruit it is "not" something that "will" "be" "attained". If it were to exist in the nature of something realized and attained, it would not be suitable as genuine realization and attainment; this is just as is stated again and again in other sūtras as well, in the manner of: "Since enlightenment was not found even by the Buddha himself, what need to speak of others? Because the nature of enlightenment is primordially isolated." Since this profound dharmatā of no seeing, no realization, and no attainment is profound and difficult to fathom, "therefore", "having" "found" the great unexcelled "enlightenment", the "compassionate" perfect Buddha who "performs" "benefit" for all sentient beings "showed" "little" "inclination" to teach the dharma, thinking, "In the multitude of 'sentient' 'beings', 'who' could 'know' 'this' kind of profound dharma that 'I' have realized?" And as it is said in the Lalitavistara: "Profound, peaceful, free from fabrication, luminous, unconditioned, / I have found a dharma that is like nectar. / Yet to whomever I teach it, they will not understand it, / So I will remain silent and dwell in the forest." And as it is also said in the Mūlaprajñā: "Therefore, having known that the depth / Of this dharma is difficult for the weak to realize, / The Muni's mind / Completely turned away from teaching the dharma." ^1-236

###### The Reason It Is Profound in That Way ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-1-5-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-237]]

Second, there is one stanza starting with "Sentient beings": If one asks what is the reason that this profound dharma is difficult to realize by most: In this way, because they have been habituated to the mind clinging to entities since beginningless time, "sentient" "beings" "delight" "in" the "domain" of craving, the manner of self-grasping and so forth, "and" "desire" the apprehended "objects". They "abide" "in" the mind that "grasps" them, "and" because they thus fall into the domain of the fabrications of the grasped and the grasping, they are "unwise" regarding the meaning of suchness that is distinguished by the absence of grasped and grasping, and, not knowing how to engage their intellect in its manner, they become "foolish" and "deluded", just "like" abiding in the midst of "darkness". As for the meaning of the Mother, the "dharma" that is to be taught and "to" "be" "attained", it is "without" any object "to" "abide" in through apprehension, and is "without" "grasping" at anything whatsoever. Thus, those supramundane dharmas are not in harmony with the entire world and are especially exalted, and "therefore", "dispute" "arises" "with" "the" "worlds". This is because worldly beings grasp entities as existing, while the other teaches them to be non-existent. Therefore, worldly beings dispute, not having seen the manner of the dharma that teaches non-entities, but since the Tathāgata sees the nature of entities exactly as it is, he engages in conventions in harmony with the world, according to how those illusion-like dharmas—which, though lacking a nature, appear to the perspective of worldly beings—appear in the world. For that reason, it is said in other sūtras: "The worldly beings dispute with me, but I do not dispute with the world. Whatever the world asserts to exist, I also assert to exist," and so forth. ^1-237

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-238]]

The way that training genuinely accomplishes the fruit. ^1-238

###### The Way the Result of That Training is Perfectly Accomplished ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-239]]

Second, regarding the way that training genuinely accomplishes the fruit, there are two parts: identifying the fruit to be attained, and the way it is not attained if one lacks this path. ^1-239

###### Identifying the Result to be Obtained ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-240]]

Regarding the first, there are two parts: the example and the meaning. ^1-240

###### Example ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-2-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-241]]

First, there is one stanza starting with "The realm of space": In this way, "the" "realm" "of" "space" is "boundless" in the "eastern" "direction", "and" the "southern" "direction", "and" "likewise" the "western" "direction", "and" the "northern" "direction", meaning however many limitless limits there are in the four directions; "and" "likewise" it "is" present "in" "however" "many" limitless expanses of the "ten" "directions", namely "above" "and" "below" "and" the four intermediate directions, so that there is nowhere not pervaded by space; and since it lacks a nature that is an entity itself, that space of the different directions "has" "no" "difference", "and" in its own essence it "has" "no" "distinctions" whatsoever of being dissimilar in shape and so forth. ^1-241

###### Meaning ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-2-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-242]]

Second, there is one stanza starting with "The suchness of the past": Just as in the example space has no distinctions, likewise, "whatever" is "the" "suchness" of dharmas of "the" "past" time, and "whatever" is "the" "suchness" of the "future", and "whatever" is "the" "suchness" of the "present", and whatever is the suchness of persons such as "arhats", and "whatever" is "the" "suchness" of "all" "dharmas" to be known as the ground, and whatever is "the" "suchness" of the "Jina" as the fruit, and whatever is the suchness of the dharmas of the path such as the realization of emptiness—thus, "all" "these" "suchnesses" "have" "no" "distinctions" established individually whatsoever, but are of a single taste as the identity of the dharmadhātu. In this way, all things divided by dualistic dharmas such as existence and peace, past and future, and so forth, appear as distinct and are designated as such merely as conventions in the manner of appearance, but in the manner of abiding, the genuine reality, all dharmas are unarisen and unceasing, and by their very nature they become the equality of the nature of the dharmadhātu. Therefore, they cannot be differentiated by the three times, self and other, good and bad, and so forth. All abide as the nature of the dharmadhātu, the single bindu. Having realized this with wisdom endowed with a single instant, attaining complete and perfect awakening regarding all dharmas in the essence of the dharmakāya endowed with the supreme of all aspects of naturally occurring wisdom—this great awakening that is free from decrease and increase, unchanging and untransferring, is the ultimate fruit. ^1-242

###### The Way That is Not Obtained Without This Path ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-243]]

Second, there are two parts: a brief teaching and a detailed explanation. ^1-243

###### Brief Indication ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-244]]

First, there is one stanza starting with "The awakening of the Sugatas": Thus, if "any" "bodhisattva" "desires" "to" "attain" "this" ultimate fruit to be attained, "the" "awakening" "of" "the" "Sugatas", which has the character of space and "is" "freed" "from" "distinct" "dharmas" such as that which is to be realized and that which realizes, and which possesses the nature of the body of non-dual naturally occurring wisdom or the body of dharmatā, they must apply or exert themselves in the "supreme" "perfection" "of" "wisdom" that realizes the suchness of all dharmas, by means of "possessing" "methods" such as faith that seeks perfect awakening. Without "this" perfection of wisdom, the Mother of the "Victorious" "Guides", they "will" "not" ever "attain" that great awakening to be attained, because the cause of that ultimate fruit is definitively this very non-conceptual wisdom, the supreme of all paths, which bears the name of the perfection of wisdom. ^1-244

###### Detailed Explanation ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-245]]

Second, there are two parts: the way the ultimate fruit is not accomplished if one lacks this path, and the way one enters this path having understood that. ^1-245

###### The Way the Final Result is Not Accomplished if Separated from This Path ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-2-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-246]]

Regarding the first, there are two parts: the example and the meaning. ^1-246

###### Example ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-2-2-2-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-247]]

First, there is one stanza starting with "A bird with a large body": Taking a completely hypothetical assumption as an example, if there "is" "a" "bird" "with" "a" "large" "body" measuring "one" "hundred" "and" "fifty" "yojanas", "whose" "wings" "are" "depleted" or non-existent, "and" thus it "does" "not" "have" "the" "strength" to be able to fly, and if "that" bird were to throw itself "from" "the" "abode" "of" the gods of the "Thirty-three" down "to" "this" "Jambudvīpa", on that occasion that bird would be "ruined", and it "would" become physically injured and so forth, with no other possible outcome. ^1-247

###### Meaning ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-2-2-2-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-248]]

Second, there is one stanza starting with "Of the Jinas": What is the meaning that resembles the example just explained? Even if "these" "five" "perfections" such as generosity, which are the path "of" "the" "Jinas", "are accomplished" with perseverance for "many" "koṭis" and "niyutas" of "eons", "and" even if one "constantly" "relies" in this "world" upon "limitless", "vast" "aspiration prayers" that repeatedly engage in the vast path and fruit, that alone does not amount to a complete cause for attaining great awakening. The unerring cause of great awakening is the "immeasurable" skill in "means" of the Great Vehicle, such as dedicating the roots of virtue to omniscience, and the "wisdom" that realizes all dharmas as equality. If these two, means and wisdom, which are like the pair of wings for a bird flying in the sky, "are" "not" "separated", [one will attain great awakening]. If someone lacks such means and is separated from the wisdom that realizes suchness, they "will fall" "into" "the very" fruit "of the śrāvakas". Just as, for example, if a bird has a large body but lacks wings and leaps, it will fall downwards. If one possesses the perfection of wisdom of the ultimate abiding nature, means and wisdom are inseparable within the essence of that pristine wisdom, because it is the subject possessing the inseparability of the two truths. ^1-248

###### The Way of Entering This Path After Knowing That ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-2-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-249]]

Second, there is one stanza starting with "The vehicle of the Buddha": Thus, "whoever" "desires" to "definitively" "emerge", meaning to reach, the state of great awakening by relying "upon" "this" unsurpassable "vehicle" "of" "the" "Buddha", the perfection of wisdom, which is the path of the single journey that is the cause of the ultimate great awakening—if it is asked what manner they should possess when entering it? Since the Great Vehicle is rooted in compassion focused on the welfare of others, acts conjointly with means, and has as its fruit the performance of the welfare of sentient beings for as long as space remains, the manner of engaging in it and the manner of the person who has engaged is as follows: Generating an "equanimous" "mind", without bias, "towards" "all" limitless "beings", they abide in "the" "perception" "of" them as "parents", "and" the perception of them as brothers and sisters, and so forth. With "a" "mind" "of" ultimate "benefit" "and" "a" "mind" "of" "loving-kindness" desiring their temporary happiness, they "should" "endeavor", meaning strive, for the welfare of those sentient beings. "Having" "no" "malice" such as anger and violence, and having a "straightforward" continuum without deceit or guile, they should "speak" "gentle" words, such as teaching the Dharma with speech and speaking harmoniously, and they should also perform bodily actions of loving-kindness. Because, for one who has generated a mind of unfeigned benefit for sentient beings, it is impossible to act in a manner discordant with that, such as with deceit, guile, or malicious intent. ^1-249

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-250]]

Teaching the person who applies themselves to that. ^1-250

###### Explanation of the Individual Who Trains ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-251]]

Third among the root topics, regarding the explanation of the person who applies themselves, there are two parts: a brief teaching by way of a question, and a detailed explanation by way of an answer. A brief teaching by way of a question. ^1-251

###### Brief Indication Through a Question ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-252]]

First, there is one stanza starting with "To the protector of the world": "The" "elder" "Subhūti" "fully" "requested" "to" the Buddha, the Blessed One, the "protector" of the three "worlds": "Please" "teach" me what are the "signs" and marks of the "absence" "of" "afflictions" of the bodhisattvas who possess a limitless "ocean" of "qualities". "How" is it that these bodhisattvas of "great" "power" who accomplish the two welfares "will" "not" "turn" "back" again from the path of great awakening, meaning how will they "be" known as irreversible bodhisattvas, as indicated by which signs? "Thus", "may" the "Jina" "prophesy" to me "just" a "fraction" or a part of the limitless "qualities" of those great bodhisattvas. Thus he requested. A detailed explanation by way of an answer. ^1-252

###### Detailed Explanation Through an Answer ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-253]]

Second, there are three parts: identifying the irreversible ones as indicated by signs, the particular conduct of those irreversible ones, and teaching the difference between reversible and irreversible ones. ^1-253

###### Identifying the Irreversible One Indicated by Signs ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-254]]

Identifying the irreversible ones as indicated by signs. First, this is taught by six stanzas starting with "different" and "perceptions": Regarding what was requested by Subhūti in this way, the Sugata proclaimed whatever signs of irreversibility there are: Because a bodhisattva who has realized the profound dharmatā sees all dharmas as inseparable within suchness, they are "free" from "the" "perception" "of" "difference" with regard to all dharmas of affliction and purification, "and" when teaching profound dharmas, they "speak" "words" "possessing" "logic" that measures in accordance with the abiding nature, which are praised by the wise and cannot be overturned by others. Because they themselves have attained irreversible confidence in the teacher of this path, they "do" "not" "rely" "upon" "other" non-Buddhist "ascetics" "and" "brahmins" with the perception that they are showers of the path, and they do not place their trust deep down in the various paths taught by them. Since they are "skilled" in the nature of dharmas such as infallible cause and effect and have confidence, by "abandoning" manifest non-virtue, they attain the state of "abandoning" "the" "three" "lower" "realms" "at" "all" "times", "constantly", as well as all states lacking leisure indicated by them. "Those" wise ones naturally abide "in" "the" "paths" "of" "action" "of" "the" "ten" "virtues", "and" they "clearly" "endeavor" in establishing others in them as well. "Without" a mind looking for "material" "things" such as gain and honor, they "teach" "the" "Dharma" of the perfections and so forth "to" "beings" "in" "accordance" with how it was taught by the buddhas and bodhisattvas. Having confidence in the meaning of the profound Dharma, they "single-pointedly" "delight" "in" "the" "holy" "Dharma", "and" without making much of other activities, they make the Dharma alone their priority. Having trained their mind in benefiting others, they "possess" the three actions of body, speech, and mind of loving-kindness, such as "constantly" speaking "gentle" "words". By being accustomed to conscientiousness regarding virtuous dharmas, "when" "walking" afar, pacing nearby, "lying" down, "and" "sitting", whatever of these four postures they adopt, they abandon unconscientiousness and "are" "fully" "endowed" with mindfulness and "introspection". Moreover, if asked how that is, thus, even when going on a path, they "walk" "looking" ahead "only" "the" "distance" "of" "a" "yoke", "and" they "lack" "a" "confused" "mind" that arises from constant unconscientiousness, such as losing their way or injuring their body. By the power of a wise and conscientious mind, they abide in blameless dharmas, and in order to abandon the non-faith of other beings, all their outer and inner behavior... ^1-254

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-255]]

"Making" things "clean" and "wearing" "clean" "clothes" and so forth, they engage in clean behavior that agrees with the minds of those to be tamed. "The" "three" avenues of body, speech, and mind are "isolated" from misdeeds "and" "pure" like a flawless crystal. Seeing the intrinsic flaws of conditioned phenomena, they are not overly attached to them, and by adopting ascetic qualities and the like, they "do" "not" "desire" "gain". Having outshone the discordant factors of the perfections such as stinginess, their mindstreams are beautified by supreme qualities, and since they make the benefit of others their main priority, they naturally become the "best" of beings. "Constantly" "desiring" the profound "Dharma"—meaning that since the Dharma and their own mindstream have blended, at all times they proceed endowed with dharmatā—because they have brought the profound dharmatā into their experience, their mind naturally does not separate from it, and whatever they say or do accords with it and does not transcend it, just as, for example, all the behavior of passionate beings proceeds endowed with passion. Because they have attained receptive faith in the profound Dharma, even if demonic emanations of the Evil One teach another path or manifest hells and so forth to frighten their minds about saṃsāra, trying by various means to turn them away from the Mahāyāna path by showing a facsimile path, they recognize them as māras; therefore, because they are unharmed by the various activities of māras, they "have" "passed" "beyond" "the" "domain" "of" "māra" "and" "do" "not" "depend" "on" "others", meaning they possess their own mental strength that is not reliant on others. Although they meditate on or attain meditative equipoise "in" "the" "four" "dhyānas", they "do" "not" "abide" "in" "that" "dhyāna" by savoring its taste and so forth. Because they know phenomena to be like an illusion, they themselves "do" "not" "desire" fame and "renown", and because their mindstreams are tamed by the holy Dharma, their "minds" are "not" disturbed or completely entangled "by" "anger". ^1-255

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-256]]

Not being attached to desires themselves and being skillful in means, "even" "if" they "become" "householders" for the sake of others, they "constantly" let go of "all" "entities" "without" "attachment". Being influenced by the realization of selflessness and a compassionate mind for others, they abandon all wrong livelihoods and extreme actions that arise from holding their own benefit as paramount. Moreover, how is this? "For" "the" "sake" "of" their "own" "livelihood", they "do" "not" "seek" "wealth" through means that are "light" in their ripening results, such as killing or flattery. They "do" "not" "perform" "spells" of destructive magic involving "fierce" "actions" that harm others for their own sake. They "are" "not" ones who perform "spells" that subjugate "women" or bring them under their control through the power of attachment. In order to proclaim themselves, they "do" "not" verbally "prophesy" regarding "embodied" beings "of" "the" "desire" realm who are residing in the womb or are about to be born, saying, "This" "will" "be" a "boy" "or" a "girl". As indicated by these, they possess the signs of completely pure livelihood and extremes of action. By being "completely" "isolated" from various distractions that delight in the activities of the three doors—namely action, speech, and proliferation—they "endeavor" "in" "the" "supreme" "perfection" "of" "wisdom". Since their mindstreams have become peaceful, wherever they reside, they are "free" from mutual "strife" "and" "disputes" and so forth, "and" their "mind" "of" "loving-kindness", which cannot be taken away by circumstances, is "extremely" "stable". ^1-256

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-257]]

With a mind that single-pointedly delights in the "omniscient" Buddha himself, they look at him and make offerings, "and" "desiring" to attain that state themselves, they have the aspiration. Their "mind" is "constantly" aspiring to the "teaching" of the Buddha, the holy Dharma, and is "directed" "toward" it, and they are "endowed" with engaging in it by way of the ten Dharma activities. Because they have obtained mastery over birth through aspiration prayers or through primordial wisdom, having "completely" "abandoned" the "borderlands", the "lands" "of" "barbarian" "people", and the "borders" of lands where the Dharma does not spread, they naturally attain residing in lands endowed with the Dharma. Because they have become certain "without" "doubt" or hesitation "in" their "own" "ground's" qualities and so forth, they are not carried away by other paths, and are "constantly" stable "like" "Mount" "Meru". "For the sake of" the holy "Dharma", they have no difficulty "giving" "up" "even" their "lives", "and" they "completely" "endeavor" in the "yoga" of retaining the Dharma and taking its meaning to mind. As for these, they are individuals dwelling on the path of junction who have struck the vital point of certainty from experiencing the profound Dharma, and individuals who have seen the truth; "these" "should" "be" "known" "to" "be" the "signs" of their being "irreversible". Endowed with the yoga of the path of means and wisdom, if one is endowed with such personal signs of certainty and familiarity with it, by those signs one can be certain of being irreversible, just like fire from smoke. ^1-257

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-258]]

The distinctive features of the conduct of that irreversible one. ^1-258

###### Distinguishing the Particular Conduct of That Irreversible One ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-259]]

In the second, there are three: the profound conduct of the ultimate subject, the vast conduct of the relative subject, and the conduct of skillful means that unifies them. ^1-259

###### The Profound Conduct Taking the Ultimate as its Object ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-260]]

The profound conduct of the ultimate subject. In the first, there are two: recognizing the profound meaning that is to be practiced, and how to practice it. ^1-260

###### Identifying the Profound Meaning to be Practiced ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-261]]

Recognizing the profound meaning that is to be practiced. In the first, there are two: explaining profoundness itself, and the manifesting of merit by practicing it. ^1-261

###### Explanation of Profundity Itself ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-1-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-262]]

The first. Form, feeling, and so forth is one verse. "Form", "and" "feeling", "and" "perception", "formation", "and" "consciousness"—that is, phenomena—are "profound". If one asks how they are profound, they are empty because they are isolated from an intrinsic "nature"; they are "without" "marks" such as being suitable as form, "and" because the wishing of fixating on them is "completely" "peaceful", they cannot be apprehended by the intellect. So, "for" "example", "like" "searching" to "reach" the "depth" "of" "the" "ocean" "with" "an" "arrow", one does not find anything and say, "It is like this." Likewise, "if" one has "examined" and investigated "by" means of the "wisdom" that analyzes reality itself, one "does" "not" "find" the "depth" "of" "the" "aggregates". ^1-262

###### Accumulating Merit by Practicing That ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-1-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-263]]

In the second, there are two: the actual subject and abandoning objections to it. ^1-263

###### The Actual Subject ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-1-1-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-264]]

The first is the commentary on the summary. In this, there are two: a brief teaching and an extensive explanation. ^1-264

###### Brief Indication ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-1-1-2-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-265]]

The first. "Bodhisattva" and so forth is one verse. "Whatever" "bodhisattva", having remained in "that" "which" is "without" "attachment" because of not conceptualizing in the supreme "vehicle" (the ultimate subject), the nature or abiding reality of the phenomena to be realized being "thus" "profound" in essence; "what" else "is there" that is more supreme "than" "that" "truly" "established" "merit" "of" "realizing" "this" profound "Dharma"—that the "aggregates" "and" "elements" "and" "sense sources" "are" "not" established as an identity? That is the true establishment of supreme merit. ^1-265

###### Detailed Explanation ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-1-1-2-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-266]]

The second. There are four: the benefits of keeping that profoundness in mind, of retaining it, of meditating on it during meditative equipoise and post-meditation, and the benefit of viewing even such merit as like an illusion. ^1-266

###### Bringing That Profundity to Mind ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-1-1-2-1-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-267]]

The first. "For example, desire" and so forth is one verse. Moreover, from the extensive sūtras: "Whoever, by considering, weighing, and ascertaining this profound state endowed with the perfection of wisdom, practices yoga for even a single day, saying, 'I shall remain just as is taught in the perfection of wisdom!'—if one asks how much activity they perform on that single day, it is taught [in the passage] beginning with, 'For example, someone engaging in desire...' and ending with, '...they turn their back on saṃsāra for that many eons and abandon the faults of turning away from perfect awakening, thus performing that much activity.' As it is taught there, here as well: "For" "example", "a" "man" "engaging" in mental "qualities" of "desire" "makes" an "appointment" with a "woman", and if he does "not" "meet" with "her", various conceptual thoughts "of" the "mind" "of" "that" man arise consecutively during "that" "one" "day", and by the power of attachment he thinks of that object alone again and again; "however" "many" moments of such conceptual thoughts of the mind "occur", a "bodhisattva" who practices yoga on this Dharma for a single day "will" "attain" the virtue accumulated for "that" "many" "eons", and even with each single moment of mind, they will gather a measureless accumulation of "virtue". ^1-267

###### Retaining That Profundity ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-1-1-2-1-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-268]]

The second. "Bodhisattva" and so forth is one verse. "Whatever" "bodhisattva", for "many" "thousands" of "ten millions" of "eons", "gives" "gifts" to "arhats", "pratyekabuddhas", and so forth, or gives gifts for the sake of the fruit of śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas, and "maintains" pure "discipline"—still, "whatever" other person recounts for just one day the "Dharma" "endowed" with the "supreme" "perfection" "of" "wisdom", that "gift" "and" "discipline" "cannot" "bear" even a "fraction" of the "goodness" of that "recounting". ^1-268

###### The Benefits of Meditating during Equipoise and Post-Meditation ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-1-1-2-1-2-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-269]]

The third. "Bodhisattva" and so forth is one verse. "Whatever" "bodhisattva" "meditates" on the "supreme" "perfection" "of" "wisdom" during meditative equipoise, "and" "arising" "from" "that", in post-meditation, "recounts" the flawless Dharma, that "untroubled" Dharma that accords with their meditation, untroubled by conceptualizations of marks and so forth—or alternatively, it is also said, "recounts" the Dharma untroubled by the conceptualization of seeking gain, honor, and so forth. "And" "if" they "dedicate" that teaching of Dharma "for" the "sake" "of" "beings" as a "cause" of supreme "awakening", "there" "is" "no" "virtue" "equal" "to" "that" "in" the "three" "worlds"—below the earth, on the earth, and above the earth. ^1-269

###### The Benefits of Viewing Even Such Merit as an Illusion ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-1-1-2-1-2-4-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-270]]

The fourth. "That merit, too" and so forth is one verse. Thus, although conventionally there is immeasurable, incalculable, and unfathomable merit in considering and explaining the perfection of wisdom and so forth, ultimately, even merit has no nature. Thus, thoroughly "knowing" that "that" "merit", "too", arising from explaining and so forth, is "vain" because it is not established as its own characteristic, "and" "likewise" is "emptiness" because it is empty of aspects such as suffering, "and" is "hollow" because it is signless, "and" is "insubstantial" because it arises dependently without being established by its own essence, "and" is "coreless" because it is devoid of anything to be apprehended by objectification; "if" one "thus" "courses" in the "course" on the "perfection" of "wisdom" "of" the "sugatas", at the "time" of coursing in such a course, one "fully" "grasps" "unfathomable" "merit". Here, it is also explained that because it lacks its own characteristic, it is vain; and emptiness and the other two are emptiness. By reason of the three beginning with the emptiness of emptiness, it is vain. By reason of the five beginning with the emptiness of the conditioned, it is hollow. By reason of the emptiness of nature and so forth, it is coreless. In reality, regarding the way entities are natureless, from the perspective of conceptual isolation, because it is accumulated from many things and lacks firmness like something spongy, it is vain; because it is like an artificial reflection such as a lion and so forth, it is hollow; and because it cannot stand on its own but depends on others, being fickle and unreliable, it is insubstantial—thus it is taught by way of synonyms for false words. ^1-270

###### Dispelling Objections ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-1-1-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-271]]

The second is abandoning objections. In this, there are two: abandoning the objection that awakening is unjustified because ultimately the fruit of merit does not increase and there is no abandoning or attaining, and abandoning the objection that attaining awakening even conventionally is unjustified. ^1-271

###### The Objection that Achieving Buddhahood is Unreasonable Because Ultimately the Result of Merit Does Not Increase and There is No Abandoning or Attaining ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-1-1-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-272]]

The first. In this, there are two: there being no exhaustion or increase ultimately, and the way awakening is justified conventionally even though there are none. ^1-272

###### Ultimately There Is No Decrease or Increase ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-1-1-2-2-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-273]]

The first. "Explained by the buddhas" and so forth is one verse. Whatever has been briefly indicated and "explained" "by" the transcendent and accomplished conquerors, the "buddhas", "and" its meaning has been indicated "and" "thoroughly" "applied" extensively, and whatever "Dharma" has been "thoroughly" "taught" along with justified reasoning that proves it—"all" "this" that has been spoken, although in reality all expressions and things expressed are unapprehendable and inexpressible, "knowing" that it "exhausts" "merely" in names of "expression" conventionally, "even" "if" one were to indicate measureless doors of Dharma and "express" them in the manner of refuting or proving for "many" "ten millions" of "hundred billions" of "eons", though the words of expression would not be exhausted, by that there is "no" "exhaustion" of faults "and" "no" "increase" of qualities whatsoever "as for" the essence of the "Dharmadhātu"—it remains eternally as equality, inexpressible, and free from decrease or increase. Therefore, in true reality, all phenomena, being without exhaustion or increase, are primordially in nirvāṇa as equality; there is not the slightest thing to be awakened to again. ^1-273

###### How Achieving Buddhahood Is Reasonable Conventionally Even Though There Is None ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-1-1-2-2-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-274]]

The second. "Of the conquerors" and so forth is one verse. "Even" "those" "phenomena", "whatever" is "so-called" the path of the "perfection" "of" the "conquerors", are "fully" "proclaimed" "merely" by "name" without being established in essence; nevertheless, merely conventionally, a "bodhisattva" who "fully" "dedicates" all roots of virtue to unexcelled, perfect awakening, and who is endowed with the union of means and wisdom "without" a "mind" of "conceit" regarding this or that, having known the path and fruit to lack nature, "does" "not" "fall" "away" from the genuine path by way of infallible dependent origination, "and" "will" "touch" the "supreme" "awakening" "of" a "buddha"—this exists conventionally, because the attainment of awakening—distinguished by the purity of freedom from adventitious defilements, resulting from directly realizing just as it is that naturally pure abiding reality wherein all phenomena are primordially in nirvāṇa—exists infallibly from the perspective of how things appear. ^1-274

###### Dispelling the Objection that Attaining Buddhahood Is Unreasonable Even Conventionally ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-1-1-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-275]]

The second is abandoning objections conventionally. In this, there are two: the justification for abandoning what is to be abandoned, and the way attaining qualities is justified. ^1-275

###### Abandoning What Is to Be Abandoned Is Reasonable ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-1-1-2-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-276]]

The first. In this, there are two: the example and the meaning. ^1-276

###### The Example ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-1-1-2-2-2-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-277]]

The first. "The core of grains or oil" and so forth is one verse. Thus, if one says: "Even in terms of conventional designations, the mind that attains awakening is momentary and cannot be accumulated; therefore, how does that mind attain awakening, free from obscurations? Because the former mind alone does not abandon obscurations, and the latter alone, not depending on the former, does not abandon them either, and the two are not accumulated together." First, to use an example well known in the world: just as when the two, the "core" "of" oil such as "grains" and so forth, which is liquid oil, and the "flame" of a lamp "burning" "meet", it is "not" "burned" up completely "by" that "first" momentary flame alone, "nor" is "it" "burned" "without" "that" first one; and the "core" of the oil is "not" "burned" by the "last" "meeting" of the liquid oil and the "flame" alone, "nor" is the "core" "burned" "without" the "last" "flame"; nevertheless, when the continuum of moments from the first momentary flame to the last is complete, the core of the oil or butter is all burned by the fire and completely exhausted—this exists. One should understand this by using it as an example. ^1-277

###### The Meaning ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-1-1-2-2-2-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-278]]

The second. "Even the first mind" and so forth is one verse. Like the example just explained, the "supreme" "awakening" free from all that is to be abandoned is "not" "touched" "by" the "first" "mind" just after generation alone; "nor" "can" "that" awakening be "touched" "without" "it"; and the great "awakening" where all stains are "pacified" is "not" "attained" "by" the "last" "mind" of the continuum on the verge of attaining awakening alone either, "nor" "can" "that" awakening be "attained" "without" "it"; nevertheless, the attainment of the great awakening where all that is to be abandoned has finally been abandoned by the continuum of mind where each subsequent moment has become more distinguished than the preceding is the nature of dependent origination, undeniable. ^1-278

###### How Attaining Qualities Is Reasonable ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-1-1-2-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-279]]

The second is the way of attaining qualities. In this, there are two: the justification for attaining qualities, and the justification for the completion of qualities. ^1-279

###### Attaining Qualities Is Reasonable ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-1-1-2-2-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-280]]

The first. In this, there are two: the way it is not unjustified, and explaining how it is definitely justified by way of dependent origination. ^1-280

###### How It Is Not Unreasonable ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-1-1-2-2-2-2-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-281]]

The first. "From a seed" and so forth is one verse. If one objects: "If the first mind that attains awakening abides until the fruit is attained, it would be permanent. If it ceases without abiding, then since awakening does not arise from each of those many momentary minds individually, this arising of the qualities of awakening accomplished over many eons is unjustified conventionally"—the answer to this is taught. Although all conditioned things are momentary, when the moments of the cause have preceded, their fruit is inevitable; this is the nature of dependent origination. Thus in the world, a "sprout" and from that a stalk, a "flower", "and" "fruits" "arise" sequentially "from" a "seed", and at the time of the fruit their respective causes cease; nevertheless, it is "not" the case that "that" seed "ceases" "and" its fruit, the "tree", "is" "nonexistent" either—rather, even though the seed has ceased, the fruits like the sprout and so forth sequentially appear. "Likewise" here on the occasion of investigating the fruit of the path, "even" the initially generated mind, or the "first" "mind", "is" the "cause" of "awakening", and it is "not" the case that "that" mind "ceases" "and" its indirect fruit, "awakening", is "nonexistent"; by way of infallible cause and effect, the attainment of the fruit, awakening, from the causes, the prior and subsequent minds of the stages of the path, exists infallibly. ^1-281

###### Explaining How It Is Definitely Reasonable Through Dependent Arising ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-1-1-2-2-2-2-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-282]]

The second. "Having found a seed" and so forth is one verse. Thus, the way of dependent origination is as follows. "Having found", that is, having come to possess, a "seed" itself that is endowed with the collection suitable to generate its own fruit, its fruit, various "grains" "and" "rice" and "so forth", "will arise", and "the" "fruit" "of" "that" seed is "not" "existent" in "that" seed from the beginning, nor is it "nonexistent" in the manner of being suitable to arise, because whatever exists previously does not need a cause, and if it were nonexistent in the manner of being suitable to arise, the meaning of "cause" would become nonexistent. If it is like that, when its nature is investigated, although it is free from the nature of an entity such as existent or nonexistent, arising infallibly as an appearance in the manner of an illusion and so forth is the way of the dharma of dependent origination. In that manner, "this" "awakening" "of" the "Victorious Ones" "also" "will" "arise" by the power of dependent origination; when investigated, it is "free" from the "nature" of a truly established "entity", "so" one should know that it "will" "arise" "like" an "illusion". ^1-282

###### Perfecting Qualities Is Reasonable ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-1-1-2-2-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-283]]

The second is the justification for the completion of qualities. "By drops of water" and so forth is one verse. If one asks: "Since entities are momentary and cannot be accumulated, even if one habituates for countless eons, it is impossible for them to gather together and increase, so how does one attain the fruit of awakening where all qualities are complete and perfected?" Although all entities are momentary, because each subsequent mind becomes distinguished in qualities in dependence upon each preceding mind, there is ultimately a complete perfection of qualities. Thus, when a "pot" is filled "by" accumulating "drops" "of" "water" from one to many, just "as" accumulating "gradually", "little" by "little", from the "first" drop to the "last", finally "fills" "that" pot "completely", thus, the attainment of buddhahood in dependence on completing the number of mind-generations gradually over the ten grounds and five paths is "likewise" according to that example: "even" the "first" "mind" generated during the stage of earnest conduct is the "cause" of "supreme" "awakening", and although qualities are not completely perfected by that alone, one "will" attain "buddhahood" through the door of "perfecting" "white" "qualities" "gradually" higher and higher, just as a pot is not filled completely by one drop of water but is filled by many. By means of filling with many instead of filling with one, it becomes the example for attaining buddhahood through the completion of many moments of mind generation; and although the entity of water is momentary, just as the pot is filled by the mutual gathering of the continuum of drops, one should understand that each subsequent moment becoming distinguished from the preceding by the continuum of momentary minds is also in that manner. ^1-283

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-284]]

How to practice this. ^1-284

###### How to Engage in That Profoundity ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-285]]

The second is how to practice that profound nature. In this, there are two: the actual practice, and explaining its qualities. ^1-285

###### The Actual Meaning ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-1-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-286]]

The first. "Empty, signless" and so forth is one verse. Thus, because all dharmas are in their own essence "empty", the meaning of thoroughly pacifying all views that apprehend them; and because the basis or cause of such apprehension is "signless", the meaning of thoroughly abandoning all conceptions and discernments; and because of that, because one has no clinging to it or is "wishless" for a fruit, the meaning of being free from prayers for all three realms—thus, "practicing" the profound "dharma" of realizing the three doors of liberation, the meaning of the equality of existence and peace; and by the power of that, "not" "touching" "nirvana", which is the one-sided peace, and "not" "practicing" the "signs" of clinging to entities in existence, is "for" "example", when a "skilled" "ferryman" "goes" to the far shore and near shore of the ocean, he "does" "not" "abide" at the "two" "extremes" of far and near, "nor" "is" he "stationed" "in" the "ocean". Moreover, on this occasion of the perfection of wisdom, whatever synonyms are taught, such as the three liberations, non-arising, suchness, and reality, all accord in a single meaning: whatever is emptiness is also signlessness, wishlessness, and so forth, so although those various synonyms differ in their isolates, they should be understood as one in the meaning of the dharmadhātu to be realized, like the single taste of honey. ^1-286

###### Explaining Its Qualities ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-1-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-287]]

The second. "Practicing like that" and so forth is one verse. The "bodhisattvas" "practicing" the meaning of the equality of existence and peace "like" "that" "do" "not" "have" the arrogant "mind" "thinking", "I" "will" be "prophesied" by the Victorious Ones possessing the "ten" "powers", and thinking, "I will 'touch' 'awakening', which is to be attained." Having understood "that" "in" "this" meaning of the natural state, "there" "is" "nothing" "whatsoever" to be eliminated, to be posited, to be attained, or to be abandoned, they become profound, "without" "fear" of being unable to bear with their minds "awakening", which has the characteristic of space; "practicing" "like" "that" "is" "practicing" the profound "wisdom" of the "Sugatas". ^1-287

###### The Vast Conduct of the Conventional Subject ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-288]]

The vast practice possessing the conventional object. The second, the vast practice possessing the conventional object, purifying the field. "Wilderness of the world" and so forth is one verse. Whatever impure appearances there are in this realm "of" the "world" in the ten directions—the faults of the environment, such as being destitute of water, towns, and wealth, like a terrifying "wilderness" "path" that is a harsh place full of fierce wild animals and so forth, "and" "seeing" the sentient beings who are its inhabitants "with" "disease", with the "famine" of being impoverished of the wealth of dharma and material goods, with the diseases of physical and mental suffering, and with various inner afflictions—one is "without" "fear" of all of them due to a mind that knows them to be like an illusion, "and" "then", "donning" the great "armor" of purifying the field in this way, one enters: "Alas, these sentient beings have little merit; being thus tormented by various afflictions and sufferings, I will thoroughly purify the field for the sake of all of them, and whenever I become a buddha, in that buddhafield, such faults will be entirely nonexistent and excellent wealth will be accomplished!" For that purpose, one "always" "exerts" oneself in the accumulations of the perfections until reaching the "limit" "of" "future" eons, "and" moreover, by means of "thoroughly" "knowing" them to be without nature, one "does" "not" "generate" "even" a "particle" "of" a "mind" "completely" "wearied" by that method. The unified practice of skillful means. ^1-288

###### The Conduct of Skillful Means of Unity ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-289]]

The third, teaching the unified practice of skillful means, has three parts: brief teaching, extensive explanation, and summary. ^1-289

###### Brief Indication ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-3-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-290]]

The first. "Bodhisattvas" and so forth is one verse. The "bodhisattvas" in all respects "practice" and "abide" "in" the mother of the "Victorious Ones", the perfection of "wisdom". How is that? By the power of the subject, wisdom, having "known" "all" "these" dharmas, the "aggregates" and so forth, to be "empty" "and" "without" "arising" "from" the "beginning", they rest in equipoise on the meaning of dharmatā in a non-dual manner; yet just as during the occasion of "not" being "in" "equipoise", great "compassion" "engages" "the" various and measureless "realms" "of" "sentient" "beings", they do not abandon the welfare of sentient beings by abiding solely in the space of peace through resting in equipoise, because they have attained the exceptional profound yoga of the unity of wisdom and compassion. "Also" "in" "that" "meantime" of engaging in the various welfares of sentient beings, they "do" "not" "completely" "degenerate" "from" the non-abiding abiding of the "dharma" "of" the "Buddhas", the perfection of wisdom, because they have attained the wisdom of skillful means in which wisdom and compassion are non-dual. Here, "not in equipoise" is also commented upon as meaning that compassion engages the confused realms of sentient beings who are not in equipoise on the profound meaning of dharmatā; and that compassion engages the realms of sentient beings during the occasion when the bodhisattvas themselves are not in equipoise; and that during those occasions like abiding in the middle or between, which is the unity of wisdom and compassion, they do not completely degenerate from the dharmas of the Buddha, the powers and so forth, so because they possess such skillful means they do not actualize mere peace. In any case, it teaches the essence of skillful means, the unity of wisdom and compassion. ^1-290

###### Extensive Explanation ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-3-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-291]]

The second is taught by means of eight examples: from among the eight—not abandoning sentient beings by the example of an illusion, perfecting the force of prayers by the example of a vessel, not falling despite having no support by the example of a flying bird, remaining until completion by the example of shooting an arrow, not tiring of the welfare of others even while remaining by the example of magical emanations, not falling to an extreme by the example of holding a parasol, accomplishing the benefit and happiness of oneself and others by the example of a captain, and being skilled in the path by the example of a merchant— ^1-291

###### Not Abandoning Sentient Beings through the Example of an Illusion ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-3-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-292]]

The first. "For example, a person" and so forth is three verses. If one asks what the skillful means of the unity of wisdom and compassion is like, it is explained progressively by examples; from among them: The first. Having briefly taught, '"For" "example", a "person" is "endowed" "with" "all" the "qualities" of being "skilled" in the means of taming enemies', if one asks what qualities he is endowed with, explaining that method extensively: he himself is "endowed" "with" "strength" of body and mind and has "exerted" himself in actions that "cannot" be "defeated" by others; if one asks what that is, being thus "skilled" in the activities "or" "methods" of "martial arts" such as physical leaping, he "has" "reached" the "perfection" "of" "archery", such as shooting arrows, "and" "many" other "fields" "of" "craftsmanship", "and" not only that, but he "knows" how to "accomplish" the method of displaying various "illusions", and "thoroughly" "desires" the "welfare" "of" "those" "traveling" on the path—or, as the commentary says, he has compassion that exerts itself for the welfare of other wanderers. "That" such a person, "having" "completely" "gathered" his "father", "mother", "and" "wife", "has" "gone" "on" a "wilderness" "path" "with" "many" "hostile" enemies, "and" ^1-292

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-293]]

When encountering that host of enemies, "that" "person", being undaunted himself, is "brave" "and" manifestly "emanated" "many" "courageous" "persons" capable of attacking the opponent, ensuring that no harm whatsoever arose from the enemies and so forth, "went" "happily" to whatever destination he desired out there, "and" "like" that "again" "returned" happily "to" his "home"; just as in "that" example, here too during the occasion of the bodhisattva accomplishing the path, "at" "that" "time" of practicing for the welfare of wanderers, "also" the "bodhisattva" who is "skilled" in "means" "generates" "great" "compassion" "in" "all" the "realms" of "sentient" "beings", "and" "having" "completely" "passed" "beyond" the obstacles to the Mahāyāna path—the "four" "māras" such as the Devaputra māra, "and" "also" "from" the "two" "levels" of śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas, "abiding" "in" the "supreme" "samādhi" endowed with the three gateways to liberation, "does" "not" "touch" "enlightenment" by way of actualizing only the space of peace, the perfect limit. Although one might think, "Since the perfect limit in general is solely devoid of all fabrications, is it possible that great bodhisattvas on the pure bhūmis, whose non-conceptual wisdom of meditative equipoise has fully ripened and merged with that in a single taste, might pass into nirvāṇa prematurely solely in that space of peace?", that is not possible; because they are completely embraced by skillful means, they do not actualize the perfect limit until the dharmas of the Buddha are perfected; and as for what is said in other sūtras about preventing the extreme of peace by way of the Buddhas rousing them from peace through exhortation and so forth, it is not because the essence of the dharmadhātu, the perfect limit, has some basis for falling into the extreme of peace. If it did, it would not be the perfection of wisdom, the basis that by its very nature does not abide in existence or peace. Therefore, realizing it would not even be fit to be the knowledge of the basis that is near to the motherly result, and so forth, and thus, missing the vital point of the Mahāyāna, the perfection of wisdom would become a path common to the three vehicles, but would not be suitable as the uncommon path of the Mahāyāna. Well then, what is it? Because the dharmadhātu, the perfect limit, is in its own essence the meaning of the ultimate natural state, the equality wherein the two truths are indivisible, it does not abide in existence or peace; and since actualizing it just as it is is Buddhahood itself, it is impossible to actualize it on the path of training because the causes for actualizing it are incomplete. Well then, one might think, "Since there is no need for doubt about that, why do they speak of skillful means for not actualizing it prematurely?" During the path of training, because the parts of means and wisdom are present in greater or lesser degrees, it is from the perspective of grasping the perfect limit in terms of its lack of referent and thinking, "If by some chance one were to cling solely to that, it would become one-sided peace," and thereby teaching skillful means to clear away the grasping at extremes, in order to understand that the perfect dharmadhātu, the ultimate emptiness endowed with all supreme aspects, is a profound dharma into which even those on the pure bhūmis must enter further; it is the nature of things that on the eighth bhūmi, by the power of one's inner skillful means, and as an outer condition for one abiding in that, the Buddhas also exhort them, saying, "Son of noble family, you have not attained my dharmas, the powers and so forth, so make effort! Generate compassion for the immeasurable realms of sentient beings!"; and because that eighth-bhūmi bodhisattva, though having attained the effortless yoga of abiding in the emptiness endowed with all supreme aspects, has a predominance of the samādhi of the aspect of wisdom abiding in the space of peace, they still have not attained equality with the Buddhas, whose domain is to rest in equipoise on the ultimate where means and wisdom are subsumed into a single unity without unevenness, the fully pure nature of the dharmadhātu endowed with all supreme aspects; so the meaning is that they must enter the path higher and higher again. The meaning is not in any way that there could by some chance be someone on the pure bhūmis who would pass into nirvāṇa prematurely. Even from the first bhūmi, because they have manifestly seen the dharmatā in which the two truths are indivisible, they are certain to be in the Buddha family, yet just as on the seven impure bhūmis, because they have not attained non-conceptual wisdom in the same way as on the pure bhūmis, there is a predominance of abiding on the side of skillful means. Regarding that, if one asks, "Do not the sūtras say that if by some chance they were not exhorted by the Buddhas, they would undoubtedly pass into nirvāṇa?" That is indeed so; but that is stated as an example of hypothetical extreme reasoning in order to understand that the samādhi of the eighth bhūmi is exceptionally peaceful. It is entirely impossible for the Buddhas not to exhort them, for it is the nature of things that by the power of their inner wisdom of skillful means, the outer condition of exhortation acts in that way to accomplish the perfect gate; and not only that, but since this is so even from when they are born into the Mahāyāna family and attain the first bhūmi of non-returning, what need is there to speak of this? Therefore, if one passes into nirvāṇa prematurely without attaining the ultimate result, the great non-abiding nirvāṇa of Buddhahood itself, it could only be the one-sided nirvāṇa of peace; and while the skillful means for not passing into that is nothing other than the perfection of wisdom itself—the non-duality of emptiness and compassion which is the subject for which the two truths are indivisible—it is a very crucial point to understand that what is taught here is an occasion of practice on the pure bhūmis, an uncommon way of engaging in such skillful means. I have explained this by analyzing slightly a highly important point of doubt. ^1-293

###### Perfecting the Power of Aspiration through the Example of a Vessel ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-3-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-294]]

Second, two verses starting with "wind resting on space": When the world forms and remains, first the maṇḍala of wind rests on space and forms. On that wind rests a mass of water, and on that water rests this great earth. On that earth rest the beings, such as the four continents, that remain on the earth. Such is the cause or basis for sentient beings to experience the karma they have accumulated in common. Even though the elements thus come to rest on one another, ultimately, on what does space rest and remain? Think about this meaning, and you will understand how it does not rest on anything. ^1-294

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-295]]

Likewise, the bodhisattva remains in emptiness, which is like space. Moreover, knowing the conventions of sentient beings and the ultimate nature of those beings, through objectless great compassion they make immeasurable aspirations for their sake. Supported by this—that is, based on these aspirations—they display various kinds of activities for the benefit and happiness of all realms of sentient beings. Yet they do not touch nirvāṇa, which is a one-sided result, nor do they remain in emptiness of the path alone. ^1-295

###### Not Falling Even without Support through the Example of a Flying Bird ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-3-2-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-296]]

Third, two verses starting with "Whenever": Whenever training on the path of the Great Vehicle, a bodhisattva thus becomes wise regarding the ultimate truth and illuminates by dispelling the darkness of others' ignorance. When engaging in this supreme samādhi of peace, pacifying elaborations and taking emptiness as its object, at that time during their training on the path, they do not meditate at all on signs to be apprehended. Remaining in the signless meaning, they experience peace, the pacification of the concepts of saṃsāra, and supreme peace, the pacification of the concepts of nirvāṇa. Just as a bird flying in the sky does not have another place to rest on, and in that space it does not abide anywhere, yet it does not fall to the ground, similarly, the bodhisattva who engages in the doors of liberation does not touch nirvāṇa as an extreme through skillful means, and does not engage in the signs of existence through wisdom. Thus, they do not abide in any phenomenon and do not fall into any extreme. ^1-296

###### Remaining until Perfected through the Example of a Shot Arrow ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-3-2-4-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-297]]

Fourth, two verses starting with "For example, trained in archery": For example, a person well trained in archery shoots an arrow into the sky along with a flat piece of wood. Then, with other arrows following that one, by continuously attaching them one behind the other, linking them together, that previous arrow finds no opportunity to fall to the ground and is pushed upward. If those people wish to put aside that effort and do not shoot, those arrows will fall to the ground. Like that example, those who engage in the supreme perfection of wisdom during the phase of the path, engaging through objectless wisdom and means such as compassion and full dedication, as well as the powers of faith and so forth, and miraculous abilities such as displaying whatever body is desired—as long as their roots of virtue, which perfectly complete, ripen, and purify, have not been perfected, they will not attain that supreme emptiness, the undefiled expanse, in the meantime. The meaning is that they accumulate the provisions until the actions fulfilling their aspirations are entirely perfected. ^1-297

###### Not Tiring of Benefiting Others Even while Remaining through the Example of Miraculous Power ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-3-2-5-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-298]]

Fifth, two verses starting with "For example, a monk": For example, a monk endowed with the supreme power of miraculous abilities abides in space and simultaneously displays various miracles, showing various actions of walking, pacing, lying down, and sitting; yet he has no mental weariness from those various actions, and his body has no fatigue. Like that example, a wise bodhisattva abides in the meaning of emptiness, which is like space, and, having reached the far shore of wisdom and miraculous abilities through skillful means, does not abide in any phenomenon in an apprehending manner. Yet, they display boundless various actions for the benefit and happiness of beings, appropriate to each of them; and for billions of immeasurable eons, they have no mental weariness and no bodily fatigue whatsoever. ^1-298

###### Not Falling into Extremes through the Example of Holding a Parasol ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-3-2-6-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-299]]

Sixth, two verses starting with "For example, some people": For example, some people stand at the edge of a great abyss and, holding two parasols in their two hands, jump into space and cast their bodies into the great abyss; yet, through the power of the wind lifting the held parasols, they do not immediately fall to the bottom of the abyss, but go slowly for as long as that distance is. Likewise, if a bodhisattva who is wise in suchness and whose mind abides in compassion fully holds the parasols of the two, means and wisdom: by thus holding the parasol of wisdom, they realize phenomena as empty, signless, and wishless, and by holding the parasol of means, they do not touch the one-sided peace of nirvāṇa, and they also see all phenomena whatsoever. ^1-299

###### Accomplishing the Benefit and Happiness of Self and Others through the Example of a Caravan Leader ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-3-2-7-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-300]]

Seventh, two verses starting with "For example, precious things": For example, a person of noble descent who desires precious things in order to make their relatives and others happy goes to an island of precious things. Having found many precious things, when returning back to their own home, it is to be understood that it is not the case that the merchant lives happily just by what he found himself and causes unhappiness to the host of relatives and friends by not giving them anything at all. Why is that? Like that example, when a bodhisattva goes to the island of precious things of the samādhi of emptiness and obtains immeasurable qualities such as the joy of dhyāna, the five faculties, and the five powers, they do not delight only themselves by touching nirvāṇa in that way, and they do not place other sentient beings in suffering by failing to benefit them with those qualities, because they accomplish those phenomena for the sake of sentient beings. ^1-300

###### Being Skilled in the Path through the Example of a Merchant ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-3-2-8-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-301]]

Eighth, two verses starting with "For example, seeking": For example, merchants seeking precious things, in order to know the route of the path, go to whatever large cities, towns—that is, medium-sized cities—and small villages there are between their own home and the island of precious things. Yet, knowing that staying there is pointless, they do not remain in those intermediate cities and so forth. Also, knowing that staying on the island of precious things will not benefit their own home, they do not remain even on the island of precious things. As they go along the path, knowing and understanding the route of the path, they do not remain even in their own home, but, being wise in traveling the path, they will go along the path with effort. ^1-301

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-302]]

Likewise, a bodhisattva who seeks the benefit and happiness of sentient beings, while training on the path of means and wisdom, becomes clear in mind about that way: thus, having become wise in the path knowledge of śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas, which is like the intermediate cities and so forth—that is, in all the ways of wisdom and the result of full liberation—they themselves do not remain in that inferior path and result; and they do not remain even in the wisdom of a buddha, which is like the island of precious things, in an obsessing manner; and they do not remain even in the unconditioned expanse of nirvāṇa, which is like one's own home, in a savoring manner. Yet, having known the ways of the three paths of śrāvakas, pratyekabuddhas, and bodhisattvas, which is like temporarily traveling on the path, it should be known that it is indeed to accomplish the benefit of immeasurable sentient beings of those respective affinities. ^1-302

###### Summary ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-3-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-303]]

Third, summarizing the meaning, there are two: the meaning and ^1-303

###### Meaning ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-3-3-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-304]]

the examples.
