First, one verse starting with "Whenever towards beings": Whenever, having connected with all beings by holding them with great love, the bodhisattva courses in the supreme samādhi, which has the nature of emptiness, signlessness, and wishlessness, there is no basis to be able to designate that they will attain only the unconditioned expanse of nirvāṇa, or that they are a conditioned being in saṃsāra. ^1-304

###### Example ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-2-3-3-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-305]]

Second, one verse starting with "For example, a magically emanated": Thus, even though it is impossible to designate them as being either in saṃsāra or nirvāṇa, that bodhisattva does not become entirely unknown and unseen by anyone in all aspects. For example, it is not the case that the body of a magically emanated person does not appear to anyone, and it can also be designated by name; and it should be known that there are also expressions of its manner in the world. And even though designated by name, that emanation does not become another person who is human or non-human. Likewise, the bodhisattva who courses in the door of the three gateways to liberation, even though not abiding in existence or peace, appears in the world and can also be designated by name. Teaching the difference between turning back and not turning back. ^1-305

###### Teaching the Features of the Conduct of the Irreversible One: The Distinction Between the Reversible and the Irreversible ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-306]]

Third, regarding the difference between turning back and not turning back, there are two: a brief teaching and a detailed explanation. The brief teaching. ^1-306

###### Brief Indication ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-307]]

First, one verse starting with "If the conduct": If any person fully asks any bodhisattva what the conduct of abiding as a child of the victorious ones is like, and what the faculties—the faith, wisdom, and so forth by which that person has conviction in and considers the profound way—are like; and if that bodhisattva does not fully teach the profound phenomena of emptiness and signlessness to be realized, and does not explain—that is, is unable to explain—the phenomena, signs, and aspects of the stage of not turning back, and so forth, in order to be able to trust that the person is one whose faculties have ripened, it should be known that that one has not been prophesied as irreversible. For example, when illustrated by asking, "How does a bodhisattva who desires to awaken to buddhahood become familiar with emptiness and yet not actualize it?" and when asked what the conduct, faculties, and signs of the irreversible are like, those who have not internalized it themselves cannot explain it. Likewise, the sūtra says: "Those who course in awakening are many, but those who give the answer are few." When someone who has themselves actualized the phenomena of the path of irreversibility answers whatever is asked in a very definitive manner, one can trust them, thinking, "This one is irreversible due to internalizing such profound phenomena"; otherwise, one cannot be certain that they are irreversible. ^1-307

###### Detailed Explanation ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-308]]

Detailed explanation. Second, there are two: the signs of irreversibility and the distinction of being deceived by Māra. ^1-308

###### Signs of the Irreversible One ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-309]]

The signs of irreversibility. First, three verses starting with "The level of an arhat": Through becoming certain in the Mahāyāna path, the union of means and wisdom, "the level of śrāvaka arhats and buddhas of conditions"—which refers to the "wisdom" of pratyekabuddhas—thus the extreme of peace, and "the three realms" of existence, let alone wanting them in actuality, they "do not desire even in dreams"; and "seeing buddhas" surrounded by an ocean of retinues, and seeing them "also teach the Dharma to beings" in dreams and so forth—it should be known that such a one has been "prophesied as irreversible" from unsurpassed awakening into a lower path. Furthermore, the bodhisattva who possesses a compassionate mind, "in dreams," "sees the places of sentient beings in the three lower realms," and "in that instant" "makes aspiration prayers to cut off the continuity of the lower realms," and sees their sufferings being pacified; or again, when asleep or awake, seeing a town blazing with fire, that bodhisattva thinks, "If I am prophesied as irreversible, by this truth and these words of truth, may this fire be pacified!"—through such "blessings of truth," the power of "that mass of fire" being "pacified" is accomplished—it should be known that such a one has been "prophesied as irreversible." ^1-309

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-310]]

In the "human world," for various "demonic possessions" and "many illnesses," through speaking words of "truth" "possessing a loving heart" that "wants to benefit" and wants to protect them from suffering, and so forth, by the "blessing" of truth, those things "are pacified." "Yet," "without" becoming "conceited" about that ability, thinking "Because I am able to do that, I possess power," and "without developing pride" thinking that they themselves are particularly superior, it should be known that they have attained the "prophecy as irreversible." Thus, showing certainty in the path is the inner warmth; from that, seeing buddhas and so forth in dreams as outer conditions; and whoever is able to display the power of inner qualities manifestly without conceit—that is taught as the sign. The distinction of being deceived by Māra. ^1-310

###### The Features of Being Deceived by Māra ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-311]]

Second, there are two: the actions of Māra, and teaching the exertion in the methods to abandon them. ^1-311

###### The Actions of Māra ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-312]]

The actions of Māra. First, there are three: the māra of being conceited about one's qualities, the māra arising from the basis of a name, and the māra of being conceited about isolation. ^1-312

###### The Māra of Conceit Regarding Qualities ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-313]]

First, one verse starting with "If they": "If" "that" person, by the power of themselves, or others, or māras, and so forth, because "various blessings of truth" such as pacifying demons are "accomplished or possessed," "develops conceit" thinking, "Because 'I' possess such qualities, I am prophesied"; or "if" conceit arises, thinking, "I will be prophesied and my fame will be proclaimed 'by other bodhisattvas'"; because they possess that conceit of "abiding in conceit" about "one's own" fame and renown, they "should be known" as having a "small mind" and being immature, a suitable target to be deceived by Māra. That is the entry of the māra that arises from the basis of blessings. ^1-313

###### The Māra Arising from the Basis of Names ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-314]]

Second, two verses starting with "From the basis of a name": The "māra" of conceit "arises" "also from the basis of a name." What is that? It is like this: the evil māra, in any guise such as a buddha, "approaches" that person and "speaks thus": "Such-and-such is 'your' 'name', and 'that of your parents', and the 'names' of 'your' 'lineage' 'back to the ancestors of seven generations'." Uttering all their names, in accordance with what they had previously thought in their own mind and so forth, he says, "And 'when' 'you' 'become a buddha', 'your name' 'will be' such-and-such!" He clearly states exactly what kind of ascetic practices they are presently abiding in, such as eating almsfood and using a single seat; and that they "possess the vows" of pure discipline; and "what kind" of "yogas" of reflection, meditation, and so forth they will "possess." He "states", "In 'past' times 'too', 'your' 'manner of qualities' such as ascetic practices 'was similar' to 'this' present one." "Hearing" "what" the māra "spoke", that "bodhisattva" who "develops conceit" based on the basis or cause of merely the "names" stating their own qualities and so forth, "should be known" as "having their conceit completely provoked by māra" and having a "small mind". ^1-314

###### The Māra of Conceit Regarding Solitude ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-1-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-315]]

Third, there are two: the brief teaching and the detailed explanation. ^1-315

###### Brief Indication ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-1-3-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-316]]

First, one verse starting with "Fully isolated": Without encountering others, they "thoroughly rely on" and abide in whatever "fully isolated" "villages", large "cities", "mountain valleys", "wildernesses" far from towns, unpopulated "isolated places", or "forests"; however, thinking "Abiding in isolation is supremely praised by the Tathāgata, and I abide in isolation," they develop conceit about that basis of isolation. "That bodhisattva" who "praises themselves and deprecates others" who do not abide in isolation "should be known" as having their mind-stream "completely corrupted" or agitated "by māra", and having a "small" capacity of "mind". ^1-316

###### Detailed Explanation ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-1-3-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-317]]

Second, there are two: teaching inner isolation, and explaining the fault of not knowing that isolation. ^1-317

###### Indicating Inner Solitude ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-1-3-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-318]]

First, one verse starting with "Always in villages": "Always" "abiding in" "villages" such as Videha, "countrysides" such as Campaka, and "towns" such as Pāṭaliputra, they "mature sentient beings" there and "exert themselves in" the path of "awakening"; "apart from" that, "that" bodhisattva "never" "develops" "desire for" the results of "arhats" and "pratyekabuddhas". "This" "is perfectly taught" as the "isolation" "of the sons of the Sugata", the bodhisattvas; for merely being physically isolated does not make the mind isolated from stains, but when the inner mind is isolated from attention to one's own benefit and attention to the lower paths, wherever one abides, one abides in supreme isolation. That is, for example, like the story occurring in the Noble Ratnakaraṇḍa Sūtra, where the Noble Mañjuśrī formerly promised to observe the summer retreat in the Jeta Grove, but without staying there in the assembly of the saṅgha, he matured sentient beings in the retinue of King Prasenajit's queens. ^1-318

###### Explaining the Faults of Not Knowing That Solitude ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-1-3-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-319]]

Second, there are two: the fault of being devoid of inner isolation, and teaching the fault of despising those who possess inner isolation. ^1-319

###### The Faults of Being Devoid of Inner Solitude ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-1-3-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-320]]

First, one verse starting with "Whoever [...] yojanas": Even if "whoever" "abides" "for ten million" "many" "years" "somewhere" that is an uninhabited "mountain valley" "which is" isolated from the comings and goings of people for up to "five hundred" "yojanas", and "full of" venomous "snakes" making it an unbearable place to stay, "that" "bodhisattva" "who does not know" "this" authentic inner "isolation", "having acquired" "superior" "conceit" about themselves, "abides" "mixed with" attention to the lower paths. The reason for this is that isolation means the state of being unmixed. With what is it inappropriate to be mixed? Since it is inappropriate for the mind-stream of a bodhisattva to be mixed with excessive attachment to themselves and attention to the lower paths, the meaning is that if they do not know such isolation, but develop conceit on account of mere physical isolation and "slack off" from working for the benefit of sentient beings, their mind becomes mixed with afflictions, which are disharmonious factors, and thus they do not abide in isolation. ^1-320

###### Indicating the Faults of Despising Those Who Possess Inner Solitude ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-1-3-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-321]]

Second, there are two: the actual meaning, and teaching that therefore it is difficult to evaluate others. ^1-321

###### Actual ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-1-3-2-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-322]]

First, one verse starting with "Bodhisattva": "Towards that" "bodhisattva" who is "exerting" themselves "for the benefit" "of wanderers", and "having attained" the four "dhyānas", "and" the five "powers", "and" the five "faculties", and the "samādhis" of the three doors of "liberation", wanders in villages and cities and displays various ways of acting for the purpose of maturing sentient beings, those who do not know their profound intent and conduct and who "despise" them, thinking, "'This one' 'is not' 'practicing' in 'wildernesses' and 'isolated places'"—"that" despiser "is" abiding in the "domain" "of māra", meaning the domain where māra finds an opportunity, "thus" "the Victor" "has taught". This is just as is taught in the Sarvadharmāpravṛttinirdeśa Sūtra and elsewhere, in the story of the dharma-preaching monks Viśuddhacāritra and Cāritramati. ^1-322

###### Indicating That Therefore It Is Difficult to Gauge Others ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-1-3-2-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-323]]

Second, one verse starting with "Whoever [...] in villages or": Whether "whoever", a bodhisattva, physically "abides" in "villages" "or" "wildernesses", "if" their inner mind is "free from" the "mind" "of the two" śrāvaka and pratyekabuddha "vehicles" and becomes "certain" of the path of "supreme" "awakening", "this" manner "is" the authentic "isolation" "of" bodhisattvas "engaged in" "the benefit of" "wanderers". Not knowing this, if another bodhisattva "evaluates" or takes the measure of "this" "bodhisattva" who possesses such isolation, judging them by thinking, "This one does not abide in isolation" and so forth, "that" evaluator "will" "deteriorate" "themselves" from the qualities they have attained and have not yet attained. Therefore, it is taught in the sūtras, "A person should not take the measure of a person, except for myself and those like me," and "A bodhisattva should examine their own mind-stream, but should not seek out the faults of others." Teaching to exert oneself in the methods to abandon that. ^1-323

###### Indicating the Method to Abandon the Actions of Māra ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-324]]

Second, regarding teaching the methods to abandon the activity of māra, there are two: the brief teaching and the detailed explanation. ^1-324

###### Brief Indication ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-325]]

The brief teaching. First, one verse starting with "Therefore": Since many activities of māra arise for those seeking the path, "therefore", in order to reverse them, one should rely on outer and inner upholders. What are they like? Who relies on them? By one who "possesses" a "strong" "intent" "seeking" "supreme" "awakening", and who possesses "skill" in the methods to accomplish it. How do they rely? Having "definitely" "destroyed" "conceit" in their own mind-stream, with great respect for another, a spiritual friend, "just as" a "multitude" of "sick people" "rely" on another good "physician" "to" "cure" their own illness, even during the occasion of listening to the dharma, possessing the four recognitions of oneself as a sick person, the dharma preacher as a physician, the dharma as medicine, and earnest practice as curing the illness, they should rely on them "without" "slackening". Upon whom do they rely? A "spiritual" "friend". Here, "virtue" (spiritual) is the dharma of the mahāyāna, and the one who teaches it well is a spiritual friend, because they are able to completely uphold one so as not to deteriorate from the path by authentically teaching the path, making one recognize the interruptions to the path, the activities of māra, and teaching the methods to transcend them. Therefore, they should completely "rely" upon such a one. ^1-325

###### Detailed Explanation ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-326]]

The detailed explanation. Second, there are two: explaining the two, the outer and inner upholders, and explaining the inner upholder in particular. ^1-326

###### Explaining the Two Outer and Inner Upholders ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-327]]

Explaining the two, the outer and inner upholders. First, one verse starting with "Buddhas, bodhisattvas": Who is that spiritual friend to be relied upon? It is as follows: One should "rely" on the transcendent conquerors, the "Buddhas", "and" the "bodhisattvas" "engaged" in "supreme" "awakening", "along with" the path of the "perfection", "as" "spiritual" "friends". Moreover, "these" Buddhas and bodhisattvas "teach" this mahāyāna to others, so they are called outer upholders. And "this" path of the perfection, which is taught by them, is the "ground" or state "to be accomplished" or practiced, so it is called the inner upholder. Thus, because those outer and inner upholders uphold one so as not to fall from the path of the supreme vehicle to other paths, "by" "those" "two" "types" of "causes"—relying on those two upholders—one "will" "swiftly" "realize" the "awakening" of a "Buddha", because the excellent causes have been gathered without anything missing. Explaining the inner upholder in particular. ^1-327

###### Explaining in Particular the Inner Upholder ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-328]]

Second, regarding explaining the inner upholder in particular, there are two: how the perfection of wisdom is a spiritual friend, and how to rely on that spiritual friend. ^1-328

###### How the Perfection of Wisdom Is a Spiritual Friend ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-329]]

How the perfection of wisdom is a spiritual friend. First, one verse starting with "Conquerors of the past": While it is widely renowned that buddhas and bodhisattvas are spiritual friends, one might wonder how the perfection of wisdom becomes a spiritual friend. The "conquerors", the perfect buddhas who "have" already appeared "in the past", "and" the future buddhas who have "not" yet "come", and those who have awakened and "who" "abide" "in the ten directions" right now—the "path" "of all" those conquerors of the three times "is" "this" very "perfection" "of" "wisdom", and it "is" "not" "other" than this. It is completely impossible for there to be another path to awakening without relying on the perfection of wisdom, because it is certain that one cannot awaken through another path lacking the wisdom that realizes the equality of existence and peace. The conquerors "have stated" "that" "this" "perfection" "of wisdom" "is" what becomes the "illuminator", "and" becomes the "lamp", "and" becomes the "light", "and" becomes the "supreme" "teacher" "of those" "engaged" "in" the manner of "supreme" "awakening". This is because it teaches, respectively, the selflessness of persons, the selflessness of phenomena, all worldly and transcendent phenomena, and the path of the ultimate result, the awakening of omniscience. How to rely on that spiritual friend. ^1-329

###### How to Rely on That Spiritual Friend ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-330]]

Second, there are two: the nature of the perfection of wisdom to be relied upon, and the instruction on the necessity of relying on it. ^1-330

###### The Essence of the Perfection of Wisdom to Be Relied Upon ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-331]]

The nature of the perfection of wisdom to be relied upon. First, there are two: the actual [meaning], and eliminating the objection that if that were so, affliction and purification would be unreasonable. ^1-331

###### Actual ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-332]]

First, one verse starting with "Just as the wisdom": In the Noble Eight Thousand-Line Prajñāpāramitā, in response to a question about the defining characteristic of the perfection of wisdom, it states, "The perfection of wisdom is characterized by emptiness," thus teaching the ultimate characteristic without teaching the conventional characteristic; for conventional modes of abiding, such as form being characterized by suitability as form, are not established ultimately, but are merely imputed by the mind and cannot withstand analysis. The genuine mode of abiding of all phenomena is emptiness, and since this is the flawless natural state, it was taught as such. Here, too, in the same way, "just as" the "characteristic" "of" the "perfection" "of" "wisdom" is emptiness, not only is the characteristic of that alone emptiness, but one also "knows" the "characteristic" of "all" "these" "phenomena", such as form, to be emptiness, "similar" "to" "that" perfection of wisdom, "and" "if" one "thoroughly" "knows" that "all" designated "phenomena" whatsoever are empty in essence "and" "without" "marks" of apprehension, "that" "practice" "in" "that" "manner" "is" "practicing" the "perfection" "of" "wisdom" of the "sugatas". ^1-332

###### Dispelling the Objection That Affliction and Purification Are Unjustified ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-333]]

Second, there are three: the actual meaning, its example, and the concluding summary of what was taught in that manner. ^1-333

###### Actual Meaning ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-1-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-334]]

First, one verse starting with "Sentient beings, through thorough": In the sutra, in accordance with the meaning of the answer to the question, "If all phenomena are isolated and empty, how do sentient beings become afflicted and purified?", teaching that affliction and purification are reasonable: Due to becoming possessed of improper "thorough" "conceptuality", "sentient beings" "generate" "desire" "for" "food" and the qualities of desire that cause that conceptuality to increase, "and" due to that desire, "those" "possessed" "of" a "mind" "attached" "to" cyclic "existence" "constantly" "wander" in cyclic existence, like a pig moving in impure mud. Therefore, the side of affliction exists in the manner of appearance. Although it exists in the manner of appearance through the power of delusion, it has never existed like that in the mode of abiding. The "two" "phenomena" "of" "self" "and" "belonging" to "self", as apprehended by that thorough conceptuality, "are" "not" "authentically" established but are "empty", because they are unproduced from the very beginning. Although there is no object, "foolish" "children" "themselves" apprehend the non-existent as existent, which is, for example, like thinking one has "cast" a "knot" "in" the "sky". Thus, although there is no affliction in the characteristic of all phenomena being emptiness, affliction exists for those with delusion who do not apprehend [phenomena] in accordance with their characteristic. ^1-334

###### Its Example ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-1-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-335]]

Second, one verse starting with "For example, by the apprehension of suspicion": If one wonders how the conceptuality that apprehends that arises if it does not exist in factual reality, "for" "example", when through one's own "apprehension" "of" "suspicion" "poison" "is" "caused" to rise, meaning that the conceptuality of poison arises manifestly, thinking that one has ingested poison, then even "though" in factual reality "that" "poison" "has" "not" "gone" "into" one's "stomach", through the power of that conceptuality one thinks one is sick from poison and out of fear one "even" "faints". This is like cowards who tremble just from being threatened in the manner of spirits or when an enemy says "I will kill you" and so forth. Just as fainting and so forth occur from apprehending it through one's own conceptuality even though one has not ingested poison, "likewise", through "foolish" "children", ordinary individuals, apprehending a "self" to exist even though it does not exist, "and" being habituated to "accepting" as "belonging" to "self" the phenomena included in one's own continuum, there arises the "unauthentic" "conceptuality" "apprehending" that object called "self", and by its power there occurs the deluded appearance of continuously experiencing "birth" "and" "death" in cyclic existence. ^1-335

###### Concluding What Was Taught Thus ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-1-2-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-336]]

Third, one verse starting with "Just as it is apprehended": "Just" "as" one "apprehends" self and what belongs to self to exist even though they do not exist in the mode of abiding, it "was" "taught" that "likewise", through the power of one's own conceptuality, one becomes "afflicted". And if one does "not" "apprehend" "self" "and" what belongs to "self" in accordance with the factual mode of abiding, it "was" "stated" that one becomes "purified". Although there is affliction and purification conventionally in that way, in "this" authentic natural state there is "no" person "whatsoever" becoming "afflicted" and that affliction "also" becoming "purified"; rather, the "bodhisattva" "realizes" the "perfection" "of" "wisdom", which is equality from the very beginning. Instruction on the necessity of relying on that perfection of wisdom. ^1-336

###### Instruction on the Need to Rely on That Perfection of Wisdom ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-337]]

Second, regarding the instruction on the necessity of relying on that perfection of wisdom, there are three: the instruction to exert oneself in that Dharma by way of necessity, how to exert oneself by way of an example, and teaching the benefits of having exerted oneself in that way. ^1-337

###### Instruction to Strive in That Dharma by Way of Its Purpose ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-338]]

Regarding the first, there are three: accomplishing great merit with little effort, becoming an object of generosity for all beings, and becoming one who shows the path to disciples. ^1-338

###### Accomplishing Great Merit with Little Action ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-2-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-339]]

First, two verses starting with "In Jambudvīpa": To make an example by taking analysis to its limit, if "however" "many" "sentient" "beings", "without" "exception", that exist "in" "Jambudvīpa", attained a human body, and "all" of them "having" "generated" the "mind" "for" unsurpassable "supreme" "awakening", and "having" "given" extensive "generosity" to all Buddhas and sentient beings "for" "many" "tens" "of" "millions" of "years", "thoroughly" "dedicated" "all" those roots of virtue "for" the "sake" "of" "beings" as the "cause" of supreme awakening, they would indeed have immeasurable benefits. Yet "whoever" "manifestly" "exerts" "themselves" "in" the supreme "perfection" "of" "wisdom", let alone for a long time, "even" "if" they "do" what is "in" "accordance" with that perfection of wisdom—such as reflecting on it oneself and teaching it to others—for "just" "one" "single" "day", "that" "heap" "of" "generosity" explained above "cannot" "rival" "even" a "fraction" "of" the "merit" for "that" merit. "Therefore", one "should" "engage" in this perfection of wisdom "always" "without" "idleness". ^1-339

###### Becoming an Object of Generosity for All Beings ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-2-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-340]]

Second, one verse starting with "The perfection of wisdom": "Yogis" "practicing" the "supreme" "perfection" "of" "wisdom", "although" they "generate" "great" "compassion" for all realms of beings, do "not" come to have a perception of "sentient" "beings". "At" "that" "time", when they possess such objectless compassion, "those" "wise" ones "become" holy "objects" "of" "generosity" "for" "all" "beings", and, performing alms-gathering and so forth in the "kingdoms" "always", they "thoroughly" "partake" of "alms" "meaningfully", because what is offered to them has great meaning. ^1-340

###### Becoming One Who Shows the Path to Disciples ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-2-1-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-341]]

Third, one verse starting with "Whichever bodhisattva": "Whichever" "bodhisattva", "in" "order" "to" "liberate" from bondage and suffering the "sentient" "beings" "of" the "three"—"gods", "humans", "and" "lower" "realms"—meaning the five continuums, "who" have been "connected" as parents, children, and so forth for a "long" "time" in the past "in" "this" state of cyclic existence, "desires" "to" "show" "to" the "realm" "of" "sentient" "beings" the "other" shore of cyclic existence, nirvana itself, in reliance on the "spacious" "path", from which the three awakenings arise and which opens the opportunity for immeasurable sentient beings, "should" "exert" themselves "day" "and" "night" "in" the "perfection" "of" "wisdom". If one exerts oneself in that, one becomes a teacher who speaks of the immeasurable qualities of the Buddha. ^1-341

###### How to Strive by Way of an Example ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-342]]

Second, how to exert oneself: Two verses starting with "A person previously": For example, if "a" "person" "at" "another" "time" "came" "to" "find" "one" "supreme" "jewel", "not" "found" "previously", meaning they had never found it before, and "from" being thoroughly "joyful" in mind they "became" "careless" "as" "soon" "as" they "found" it, "and" if that jewel "became" "lost", then because that hard-to-find thing "became" "lost", they "would" "always" "suffer" while "manifestly" "desiring" such a "jewel" in mind. "In" "that" "manner" of the example, by having "entered" the path of "supreme" "awakening", one "should" "not" "abandon" doing "yoga" "in" this "perfection" "of" "wisdom", "which" "is" "like" the hard-to-find glory of a "jewel". For example, "having" "found" that "jewel" "again" later after it was lost, "having" "taken" it with joy, "having" "wrapped" it inside one's clothes and so forth "with" "great" "exertion", "and" "having" "hidden" it so it is not wasted, out of fear that it might be lost or stolen by others, one's "going" "swiftly" to one's home "is" "like" how all the sorrow of being separated from the jewel and the suffering of poverty "become" "pacified". ^1-342

###### Teaching the Benefits of Striving Thus ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-2-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-343]]

Third, regarding the benefits of having exerted oneself in that way, there are five: overpowering other practices, becoming a place of hope for gods and humans, defeating Mara, gathering all perfections, and the benefit of others rejoicing in one's virtue. ^1-343

###### Outshining Other Practices ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-2-3-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-344]]

First, two verses starting with "The sun and clouds": For example, when "the" "sun", "free" "from" "clouds" and having a "network" "of" "light" "rays", which "subdues" "thick" "black" "darkness" and "all" other "darkness", "rises", it "overpowers" "all" four lights: "fireflies", which have a subtle light in just one part of their body; "and" "all" "creatures" and "beings" that illuminate just their immediate vicinity through the power of holding their own body's light or lamps and so forth; "and" the "multitude" "of" "stars" that illuminate slightly; "and" the "light" "of" the "moon" that illuminates mostly. "Likewise", "the" "bodhisattva" who "practices" the "supreme" "perfection" "of" "wisdom", who is like the sun free from the clouds of obscurations, being "learned" in "practicing" "emptiness" "and" "signlessness", thoroughly emits the supreme network of light rays of Dharma; and thereby "having" "destroyed" the "thick" black darkness of "viewing" self and phenomena without exception, they "overpower" "all" firefly-like "beings", "and" other creature-like "arhats", "and" star-like "pratyekabuddhas", "and" "many" moon-like other "bodhisattvas" who are devoid of the skillful means of this perfection of wisdom. Through wisdom, they are distinguished above all other practices that do not possess this. ^1-344

###### Becoming a Source of Hope for Gods and Humans ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-2-3-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-345]]

Second, two verses starting with "For example, a king's": "For" "example", "if" "a" "king's" "son" "gives" "wealth" to protect the subjects, and, being learned in the treatises on statecraft, "desires" the "meaning" of fully protecting the kingdom—meaning he has the nature of knowing what is meaningful and being endowed with meaning, not lacking meaning—he "becomes" the "foremost" "of" "all" the victorious one's sons, and since all the subjects revere and esteem him, he becomes "worthy" of their "manifestly" "going" to him with hope for their meaningful purposes. If "this" prince "makes" "many" "sentient" "beings" "joyful" "even" "now", "during" the "time" he has not yet become king, "having" "attained" the "means" of taking power over the royal seat, such as being empowered on the royal seat and having the diadem bound, and "having" become a king who "abides" in the "kingdom", "what" "need" "is" "there" "to" "mention" that he will accomplish the purpose of the subjects and make them joyful? "In" "that" "manner" of the example, the "learned" "bodhisattva" endowed with meaning "also", who "practices" the perfection of wisdom, "gives" the "ambrosia" of Dharma to sentient beings "and" "makes" "gods" "and" "humans" "joyful", so gods and so forth come to them, make them a place of hope, and revere them. If "this" bodhisattva "exerts" themselves in "benefiting" "many" "sentient" beings "even" "now", "during" the occasion of training on the path, "having" become the "king" "of" "Dharma" and "abiding" in the wisdom of a Buddha, "what" "need" "is" "there" "to" "mention" that they will benefit sentient beings! ^1-345

###### Defeating Māra ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-2-3-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-346]]

Regarding the third, there are two: terrifying the māras, and how the māras are unable to harm them. ^1-346

###### Terrifying the Māras ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-2-3-3-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-347]]

First, one verse starting with "At that time": "At" "that" "time", when the bodhisattva who is endowed with the vital point of the perfection of wisdom practices in that way, the sinful "māras" of the great trichiliocosm think this: "This bodhisattva will definitely rise above my realm, and by becoming a Buddha, my realm will become empty." With that thought, for that reason, their minds "become" "endowed" "with" "darts" of pain; and thinking, "It is difficult to overpower them even with my might," they become "tormented" "by" "sorrow"; and by the power of that, their bodies experience "suffering" "and" their "minds" experience "displeasure", and, overpowered by the bodhisattva's qualities, they become "feeble", without pride in their own might. Having become like that, those māras, unable to bear it, think, "How" will "the" "mind" "of" "this" "bodhisattva" "shrink" back from the path of omniscience and "become" turned away? "Thus" thinking, and wondering, "Will we find an opportunity to disturb even just a single thought of omniscience?", "in" "order" "to" "show" great "fear" to that bodhisattva, they display various magical emanations of māras, such as "burning" "the" "directions" with fire "and" "sending" disturbed "meteors" and so forth. ^1-347

###### The Way Māra Is Unable to Harm Them ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-2-3-3-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-348]]

Regarding the second, there are two: how they do not find an opportunity, and incidentally showing the causes for the opposing side finding an opportunity. ^1-348

###### The Way No Opportunity Is Found ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-2-3-3-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-349]]

First, one verse starting with "Whenever the learned": "At" "which" "time" the display of the māras' magical emanations occurs like that, "those" "learned" bodhisattvas are "endowed" with an unshakeable and "very" fierce "intention" toward the path of the Mahayana, "and", by way of application, they look "at" the "meaning" of the "supreme" "perfection" "of" "wisdom" constantly, "day" and "night". "At" "that" "time", having done so, "these" "bodies" "and" "minds" of that bodhisattva "travel" without fear of the precipices of existence and peace, "like" a "bird" "going" "in" the "sky"—which is due to the power of abiding in the non-abiding perfection of wisdom. Therefore, "how" "could" an "opportunity" be "found" against them "by" the "dark" "partisans"? It is like how a bird going in the sky cannot be harmed by dogs and so forth. ^1-349

###### Incidentally Teaching the Cause for the Opposite, Finding an Opportunity ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-2-3-3-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-350]]

Regarding the second, there are two: showing that the main cause of finding an opportunity is dispute, and showing the means of despising that fault. ^1-350

###### Teaching That the Principal Cause for Finding an Opportunity Is Dispute ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-2-3-3-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-351]]

Regarding the first, there are two: dispute in general, and specifically, the drawbacks of a bodhisattva who has not obtained a prophecy disputing with a bodhisattva who has obtained it. ^1-351

###### Dispute in General ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-2-3-3-2-2-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-352]]

First, two verses starting with "Whenever bodhisattvas": Although māras do not find an opportunity against a bodhisattva who is endowed with skillful means and the armor of wisdom as explained above, they do find an opportunity against those of lesser capacity who are not endowed with that method. Furthermore, the Aṣṭasāhasrikā mentions seven faults by which māras find an opportunity, and the Pañcaviṃśatisāhasrikā mentions twelve; what is taught here is the last of them: "At" "which" "time" "bodhisattvas" "fight" with their bodies "and" "dispute" with their speech with one another, "and", in mutual "disharmony", "become" "endowed" "with" "angry" minds, "at" "that" "time", the sinful "māras" "rejoice", "and" however much the dispute increases, to that extent they "become" "supremely" "joyful"; the reason for that is because they "think": "Due to the fault of that dispute, which contradicts the path of skillful means and wisdom, 'both' of 'those' bodhisattvas 'become' 'distant' 'from' the 'wisdom' 'of' the 'Victorious' 'Ones', and we have found an opportunity." In this way, "both" of "them" "become" "distant" from the wisdom of a Buddha, "and", with untamed mindstreams, they "become" "like" "flesh-eating" demons, such as rākṣasas and pretas. "Both" the one who gets angry and the one who retaliates against anger "will" "violate", through dispute, "their" "own" "vow" to attain perfect awakening out of compassion for the sake of others and to practice its path, the perfections; for disputing with anger contradicts in every way compassion, bodhicitta, the wisdom that sees selflessness, and so forth. For one who "hates" "and" is "devoid" "of" its antidote, "patience", "how" "could" "there" "be" the attainment of "awakening"? It is like a sprout from a burnt seed. "At" "that" "time", when such dispute has occurred, the "māras", "together" "with" those who belong to their "own" "partisan" side, "will" "rejoice", because it has been accomplished according to their intentions. Therefore, it is extensively stated in the sūtras that for a bodhisattva, the arising of anger for even an instant is a greater fault than the arising of the fault of attachment for an eon, because it contradicts compassion in every way; and in particular, anger directed toward the object of a bodhisattva is a great fault. As it is said in the Dharma discourse of the Ratnakūṭa called Maitreyamahāsiṃhanāda: "One should flee a hundred yojanas away from a place with angry disputes; one should not generate an angry mind. Fighting and generating an angry mind toward another bodhisattva is a far heavier fault than scolding, striking, and attacking with weapons all the sentient beings of the great trichiliocosm, and by it a bodhisattva is wounded and degenerates; for example, just as iron is not cut by things other than iron such as clods of earth and so forth, the roots of virtue of a bodhisattva are also exhausted by anger toward another bodhisattva, whereas they cannot be exhausted by other things; therefore, they should treat one another with reverence and respect, and one should generate the perception of the Teacher even toward a bodhisattva who has first generated the mind." ^1-352

###### In Particular, the Drawbacks of One Who Has Not Obtained a Prophecy Disputing with a Bodhisattva Who Has Obtained One ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-2-3-3-2-2-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-353]]

Second, one verse starting with "Has not obtained a prophecy": Specifically, "by" "whoever" is a "bodhisattva" "who" "has" "not" "obtained" "a" "prophecy" of non-return, having become "angry" in "mind" "at" a bodhisattva who has "obtained" "the" "prophecy's" signs, "engages" in "dispute" of body and speech, and as long as they do not relinquish that mind through regret and confess it, and "if" they do not relinquish the dispute, then "however" "many" "moments" "of" a "mind" "endowed" "with" "faults"—which is like "hard" "ground" where the untamed mindstream's seeds of virtue do not flourish—arise, "for" "that" "many" "eons", "from" "the" "foundation", they "must" newly "don" "the" "armor" of entering the path. This means that for as many eons as there are faulty moments of a disputing mind that have arisen, they will be born in hell, and since anger has exhausted the roots of virtue accumulated over that many eons and sin has increased to that extent, for the number of that many eons, until they newly don the armor for the entire path such as generosity "from the foundation"—meaning newly—and train, the path will not be restored, and therefore they will distance themselves from unsurpassed awakening for that long. ^1-353

###### Teaching the Method of Purifying That Fault ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-2-3-3-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-354]]

Regarding the second, showing the means of despising that fault, there are two: confession by way of the four powers being complete conventionally, and confession by viewing without apprehension ultimately. ^1-354

###### Conventionally, Confessing by Means of the Complete Four Powers ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-2-3-3-2-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-355]]

First, one verse starting with "Buddhas by patience": If the "Buddhas" "touch" "awakening" "by" "the" "perfection" "of" "patience", they "think", "This 'mind' of mine, which is anger, the opposite of that, 'is' 'not' 'good'; it has the nature of burning the roots of virtue from many eons and strongly contradicts the path to awakening." Thus they "generate" "mindfulness" that knows a fault as a fault, "and" being supremely regretful of the sin they committed is the power of revulsion. "In" "order" "to" "confess" that sinful entity "individually", they visualize the Buddhas and bodhisattvas of the ten directions to make them directly present and rely on them, which is the power of support; they engage in any sequence of Dharma practice, such as reading profound sūtras, reciting dhāraṇīs, and resting evenly in the profound meaning, which is the power of the antidote; "and", "later" "also", they "vow" not to commit such a fault, which is the power of restoration. "And" thinking, "Such an untamed thing is not suitable to be done by me, a bodhisattva," by way of a revulsed mind, "whoever" "does" "not" "rejoice" in the nature of dispute and so forth, "they" "train" "in" "this" "Dharma" "of" the "Buddhas", and do not train in the dharma of māras. ^1-355

###### Ultimately, Confessing by Means of the View of Non-Apprehension ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-2-3-3-2-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-356]]

Regarding the second, there are two: the actual [meaning], and the benefits of knowing in that way. ^1-356

###### The Actual Subject ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-2-3-3-2-2-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-357]]

First, one verse starting with "Whoever, when training": "When" "whoever" "trains" on the path, they "do" "not" "accept" any action called "'training'" by way of manifest adherence. They "do" "not" "apprehend" either themselves, the "trainer", "and" the "dharmas" to "be" "trained" in by them, the "perfections"; "and" they "do" "not" "conceptualize" "either" "these" "two": "training", which is the antidote, "and" "non-training", which is its discordant class. "Knowing" all dharmas to be of a non-apprehensible nature, "whoever" "trains" "like" "that", "they" "train" unmistakably "in" "this" "Dharma" "of" the "Buddhas", the perfection of wisdom that is the cause of omniscience. ^1-357

###### The Benefits of Knowing Thus ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-2-3-3-2-2-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-358]]

Second, two verses starting with "Bodhisattva": "Whoever" is a "bodhisattva" who "knows" "this" "training" of no-more-learning "such" as that, "they" will not "ever"—meaning at any time—"diminish" from the "training" of wisdom and samadhi, nor "will" their "discipline" "be" "impaired", because they have abandoned the view with apprehension which is the cause of faults, just as when there is no poison seed, its leaves and fruits do not grow. Such a bodhisattva, "they", "train" "in" "such" a profound path of wisdom "in" "order" "to" "accomplish" or to "have" "found" the "Dharma" "of" the "Buddhas" such as the powers. Also, it appears to be commented on as for the sake of abiding in the dharma that pleases or delights the Buddhas. That bodhisattva, "they", increase "higher" in the "training" of mind and discipline and "are" "skilled" "in" the "training" of wisdom, and the reason for that is because they abide on a "non-apprehensible" path, for they think that if they were with apprehension, they would be obscured by views of self and so forth, and thus it would not become the higher training. Thus, one should "train" "like" "that" in the "wisdom" "of" "those" bodhisattvas who are "skilled" in the two truths, "making" the "light" of Dharma appearance. When training like that, they "do" "not" "generate" "even" "one" "non-virtuous" "mind" with afflictive conceptualizations that contradict the path to omniscience. Because their mindstream is pervaded by the light of profound wisdom, there is no opportunity for the darkness of bad conceptualizations to remain, "like" how "in" "the" "space" "before" "it", which has "become" "overwhelmed" by the rays of the "sun" "moving" "in" the "sky", "darkness" "does" "not" "remain" entirely. ^1-358

###### Summarising All the Perfections ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-2-3-4-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-359]]

Regarding the fourth, there are two: how it becomes training in all perfections, and how it becomes training in all vehicles. ^1-359

###### Training in All the Perfections ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-2-3-4-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-360]]

Regarding the first, there are two: the example of becoming the root, and the example of becoming the essence. ^1-360

###### The Example of Being the Root ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-2-3-4-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-361]]

First, one verse starting with "Perfection of wisdom": "Of" "those" "having" "done" the "training" "in" the "perfection" "of" "wisdom" like this, "all" "perfections" such as generosity "will" "be" "subsumed" "in" "this", their basis of training, the perfection of wisdom, for there is not even a little that is not included within it. Why is that? The perfection of wisdom of the path is genuinely skilled in the way of the two truths; through that wisdom it annihilates the perfections' discordant classes such as miserliness, and through skill in means it does not abandon the result, great awakening; and because it does not conceptualize the three spheres in that either, all paths become the essence of the single perfection endowed with all supreme aspects, and it makes all of them have a single taste in the omniscient aspect of the wisdom of emptiness endowed with all supreme aspects as the result. Therefore, if this perfection of wisdom exists, the trainings of all perfections will be completely present without missing anything, so the arising of all of them in reliance on this root is, for example, the sixty-two views taught in the Brahmajāla Sūtra—the eighteen views relying on the past limit such as the four proclaimers of permanence and so forth, and the forty-four views relying on the future limit such as proclaiming the existence or non-existence of perception, proclaimers of annihilation, and so forth. Since all of them hold the subject, the self, to exist and investigate its various specific qualities, those "sixty-two" "views" are "subsumed" in self-grasping or "in" the "view" "of" the "transitory" "collection", because they arise in reliance on that basis and become mere differentiations of it, and if it does not exist, all the others will not exist either. Knowing that, "like" "that" example, all these perfections are also "taught" to be "subsumed" "in" "this" "perfection" of wisdom. ^1-361

###### The Example of Being the Essence ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-2-3-4-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-362]]

Second, one verse starting with "For example, the life": "For" "example", even if there are multiple faculties such as the eye, "if" the "life" "faculty" residing in this world "were" "to" "cease", "all" "those" "other" "faculties" belonging to this life, "however" "many" "there" "are", "will" "cease". "Like" "that", "all" "other" "perfections", the training "of" "bodhisattvas" who are "supreme" "scholars" "who" "practice" the "perfection" "of" "wisdom", are "taught" to be "subsumed" "in" "this" perfection of wisdom; for if this does not exist, all of them will not arise either, and due to this existing, they all become supramundane perfections and thereby Buddhahood is attained, but if one practices generosity and so forth for however long without connection to the realization of selflessness, one cannot even be liberated from saṃsāra, so how could it become the perfection path to great awakening? It is impossible. ^1-362

###### Training in All the Vehicles ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-2-3-4-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-363]]

Second, how it becomes training in all vehicles, one verse starting with "Bodhisattvas skilled": A "bodhisattva" who is "skilled" in the perfection of wisdom becomes skilled in the three vehicles. Just as a bre measure is included in one khal, if one knows this perfection of wisdom which is the supreme path where the aspects of means and wisdom are thoroughly perfected, one knows in passing the path of the śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas which is limited in means and wisdom. Therefore, it is said that the paths of the three vehicles are taught extensively from this perfection of wisdom, and it is taught that those who wish to train in the śrāvaka ground should also train in this. Just as there are various jewels in the treasury of a great king, so the substances needed by the lower ones can also be taken from there. Likewise, the paths and results of those who have entered the path of limited self-interest are deeply known in passing through this. Although that is the case, those bodhisattvas "train" unconfusedly "in" "whatever" "qualities" of the "śrāvaka" training "and" "likewise" "all" "qualities" of the training of the "pratyekabuddhas", and they guide beings of those families through those ways, but "those" bodhisattvas do "not" themselves "abide" "in" "those" lower paths, and "desire" for them "does" "not" "arise" either. Yet they think, "I" "must" "train" "in" "this" way, because the realization of personal selflessness and so forth are similarly desired here, and the aspect that becomes a lower path due to limited means and wisdom is to be known and surpassed by oneself, and it is suitable to be taught to others of those families in order to guide them, and "therefore", they "train" in all vehicles. ^1-363

###### The Benefits of Others Rejoicing in That Virtue ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-2-3-5-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-364]]

Regarding the fifth, there are two: the actual topic and its reason. ^1-364

###### The Actual Subject ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-2-3-5-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-365]]

First, one verse starting with "Irreversible awakening": Endeavoring in the perfection of wisdom like that has immeasurable qualities; let alone the merit generated by the endeavorer, even others who rejoice in that endeavor attain great merit. What is that? If another person "genuinely" "rejoices" in "that" "generation" "of" "the" "mind" of a person, a bodhisattva, "who" has "firmly" "entered" "into" "supreme" "awakening" "irreversibly" through intense intention and the application of practicing the perfection of wisdom, it "is" "possible" "to" "measure" the "Mount" "Merus" existing in this great "trichiliocosm" by "weighing" them on a "scale", but "that" "virtue" "of" "rejoicing" is "not" measurable "like" "that". ^1-365

###### The Reason for That ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-3-2-3-2-2-2-2-2-2-2-3-5-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-366]]

Second, one verse starting with "Seeking virtue": Why is the benefit so great like that? By directly rejoicing in the virtue of that bodhisattva alone, one has indirectly "rejoiced" "also" in the "heaps" "of" "merit" "of" "all" those who "seek" "virtue" for themselves in the world "and" "however" "many" "beings" "there" "are" who "desire" "to" "benefit" others. "Why" "is" "that?" Because a bodhisattva's root of virtue has become the benefit of all beings, "they", those irreversible bodhisattvas, "having" "attained" the "qualities" "of" the Guides, the "Victorious" "Ones", through that path, "give" the gift of "Dharma" "in" all "the" "worlds" "in" "order" to permanently "exhaust" many types of "suffering", and through that, they completely fulfill the intentions of those who seek virtue and those who desire to benefit others. For example, if one lovingly nurtures a great medicinal tree, one is directly growing the tree, but indirectly it has become the nurturing of many beings; it is similar to that. ^1-366

###### The Greatness of the Person Who Engages in That Way ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-4-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-367]]

The greatness of the person who enters in that way. Regarding the fourth aspect, the greatness of the person, there are three: attaining non-conceptual conduct, the example of how it is not contradictory to perform actions even though non-conceptual, and the qualities of having attained such realization. ^1-367

###### Attaining Non-Conceptual Conduct ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-4-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-368]]

Regarding the first, there are two: the meaning and the example. ^1-368

###### The Meaning ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-4-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-369]]

First, one verse starting with "Bodhisattvas": "Whatever" "bodhisattva" abides in the "non-conceptual" and "thoroughly" "knows" "all" "phenomena", subsumed under affliction and purification, to be "empty", "and" "signless", "and" "unelaborated" or wishless, "that" one does "not" "seek" "awakening" "by" grasping at the "two" extremes with "wisdom" that conceptualizes in the manner of affliction and purification, apprehended and apprehender, or near and far. "That" "yogin" free from dualistically apprehending concepts is one who "endeavors" in the "supreme" "perfection" "of" "wisdom". ^1-369

###### The Example ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-4-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-370]]

Second, one verse starting with "That element of space": Thus, the manner of being free from all concepts such as grasping at near and far regarding awakening is, for example, that "that" "element" "of" "space's" own essence "and" what is "contradictory" "in" "that" "space"—that which is unsuitable to coexist with space—both do "not" "exist", because since space does not have its own essence from the beginning, "that" is "unattained" "even" "by" "whatever" phenomenon. That which has no attained essence is not near to any phenomenon and not far or contradictory to any. "Likewise", all phenomena are the nature of awakening, and awakening is like space, which ultimately will not be newly attained by anyone, and since it is not far from anyone, the "bodhisattva" who is "skilled" in "practicing" the profound "wisdom" that abides in equality from the beginning is "also" "like" "space", possessing the greatness of "practicing" where all concepts are "pacified". ^1-370

###### The Example Showing That Although Non-Conceptual, Performing Actions Is Not Contradictory ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-4-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-371]]

Regarding the second, there are three: "not" conceptualizing objects as sentient beings through the example of an illusory person, not conceptualizing an agent through the example of an emanation, and not conceptualizing a result through the example of a carpenter's creation. ^1-371

###### Not Conceptualizing the Object as Sentient Beings Through the Example of an Illusory Person ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-4-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-372]]

First, two verses starting with "In the midst of people": If one asks, "What is it like when one who has thus attained the domain of non-conceptuality works for the benefit of sentient beings and so forth?", it is explained through an example. For example, "in" "the" "midst" of many "people" gathered for a spectacle, an "illusory" "person"—a person who appears by the power of illusory mantras and so forth—certainly does "not" "think" "like" "this": "Through this appearance in the form of a deity or the like, I will "please" "this" host of many "people"." Nevertheless, they "also" "do" "that" action that pleases the eyes of the spectators; even "if" that host of people "sees" them "exhibiting" "various" "miracles" such as moving and shaking in this way, "that" illusory person has "no" "body" established and "no" "mind" either, so being empty of a basis of designation, they do "not" "have" an established "name" "either". "Likewise", non-conceptual bodhisattvas who "practice" the objectless meaning of the perfection of "wisdom" certainly "never" "think" "like" "this": "Having "realized" great "awakening", I will "completely" "liberate" "beings"." Nevertheless, those who manifest "various" "births" in the world realms "and" "possess" "many" "actions" of taming disciples "practice" in a "non-conceptual" manner regarding this and that, even "though" they "manifest" "like" an "illusion"—appearing while not existing, for example. ^1-372

###### Not Conceptualizing the Agent Through the Example of an Emanation ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-4-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-373]]

Second, one verse starting with "For example, a Buddha": "For" "example", like the deeds of the Tathāgata Praśāntamati, an "emanation" emanated as a Buddha by the "Buddhas" certainly "performs" all the "deeds" of a "Buddha" such as teaching the Dharma, but "in" "that" "performing" of its own deeds of a Buddha by that emanation, "not" "even" "a" "little" "pride" arises, thinking, "I am performing the deeds of a Buddha," because it is merely the appearance of an emanation. "Likewise", a "bodhisattva" who has attained the non-conceptual domain, who is "skilled" in having familiarized and gained confidence in "practicing" the "wisdom" that realizes all phenomena as isolated in nature and appearing like an illusion, "also" "manifests" "all" "actions" such as teaching the Dharma "like" an "emanation" and an "illusion", but conceptualization does not arise. ^1-373

###### Not Conceptualizing the Result Through the Example of a Carpenter's Creation ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-4-2-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-374]]

Third, one verse starting with "A carpenter's creation": When an "artisan" "carpenter" who is "skilled" in their work "creates" a "creation" "similar" "to" a "man" "or" a "woman", "that" created man or woman "also" "performs" "all" "actions" such as walking, yet does not conceptualize the result of the action, thinking "I have done this," because it has no mind. By "that" example, "likewise", "that" "bodhisattva" who is "also" "skilled" in "practicing" non-conceptual "wisdom", even though not conceptualizing anything, "performs" "all" "actions" of perfecting, ripening, and purifying while abiding in "non-conceptual" "exalted" "wisdom". In this way, all phenomena are isolated in their own essence from the beginning, while appearing like an illusion, and when the clarity of the mind habituated in that way reaches its utmost limit, all phenomena will only appear as having that character, and therefore non-conceptuality arises like an illusory person, and demonstrating various spontaneously accomplished deeds through the power of that non-conceptual exalted wisdom should be known to be like a wish-fulfilling jewel; moreover, that non-conceptual exalted wisdom should be understood from being a concordant one during the stage of the path of training up to being completely perfected on the ground of a Buddha. ^1-374

###### The Qualities of Attaining Realization in That Way ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-4-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-375]]

Regarding the third, there are two qualities: the gods bowing down and defeating Māra. ^1-375

###### Homage by the Gods ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-4-3-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-376]]

First, one verse starting with "Skillful in practicing thus": "Many" "hosts" "of" worldly "gods" such as Brahmā and Indra "bow" down their bodies with their "palms" "joined" "to" "those" who are "skillful" in "practicing" the non-conceptual practice "thus", "and" they "also" "pay" "homage" with great devotion. Even "however" "many" "Buddhas" of the "world" "realms" "of" the "ten" "directions", who have become the gods of gods, "reside", "they" "also" "completely" "express" continuous "garlands" "praising" the "qualities" of that bodhisattva who has attained the non-conceptual domain, for example, just as the Buddhas now express praises of Mañjuśrī and Samantabhadra. ^1-376

###### The Quality of Defeating Māra ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-4-3-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-377]]

Regarding the second, there are two: the actual topic and its reasons. ^1-377

###### The Actual ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-4-3-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-378]]

First, one verse starting with "Equal to the river Ganges": If one "fully" "hypothesizes" "all" "however" "many" "sentient" "beings" there are "in" the "realms" "equal" to the number of grains of sand of the great "river" "Ganges", for example, if they "became" "Māra", "and" even "if" from "every" "single" "hair" of each of those Māras they "emanated" "that" "many" Māras and sought an opportunity against that bodhisattva who has attained non-conceptual exalted wisdom, "all" "of" "them" would "not" "be" "able" "to" "cause" "even" a "hindrance" as slight as moving a pore of a hair "to" "that" "skilled" one. ^1-378

###### The Reason for That ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-4-3-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-379]]

Second, one verse starting with "By four reasons": If one asks why they cannot cause hindrances in that way: "By" "four" "reasons", "that" "bodhisattva" who is "skilled" in means "and" "possesses" the "strength" of having realized the nature of phenomena is "difficult" "to" "overcome" "by" the "four" "Māras" such as the Māra of the devaputras, "and" they "cannot" "be" "shaken" from the path. What are the four reasons? The wisdom whose mind "abides" in the meaning of "emptiness"; the compassion that does "not" "completely" "abandon" "sentient" "beings"; the firm vow that "practices" irreversibly "just" "as" one previously "spoke" the "words" of one's promise, making a pledge such as, "I will accomplish unsurpassable awakening for the sake of all sentient beings"; "and" possessing the entrance of the "blessings" "of" "the" "Sugatas" into their mindstreams by the nature of phenomena since they have pleased the Buddhas by completely accumulating the two accumulations. Thus, by means of these four reasons, they are able to defeat all Māras. ^1-379

###### The Greatness of the Dharma to Be Trained In ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-5-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-380]]

Regarding the fifth main section, the greatness of the Dharma to be practiced, there are three: entering the single path that travels to the result, being the supreme of those who abide on the path, and teaching the greatness of being the supreme of all trainings to be known. ^1-380

###### Entering the Single Path to the Result ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-5-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-381]]

First, one verse starting with "Mother of the Tathāgatas": "When" "this" perfection of "wisdom", the "Mother" "of" all "Tathāgatas", is "being" "explained", "if" "any" "bodhisattva" is devoted to this way from the core of their heart "and" "genuinely" "exerts" "themselves" "in" practicing or "accomplishing" this very thing, "that" "gentle" one whose three doors are peaceful and tamed—that bodhisattva—"should" "be" "known" as having "entered" the way "of" "omniscience" "itself", because this is the sole, non-dual path that travels to the result of omniscient exalted wisdom. ^1-381

###### The Supreme Among Abidings on the Path ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-5-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-382]]

Being the supreme of those who abide on the path: Regarding the second, there are two: recognizing the perfection of wisdom as the abiding of the bodhisattva, and teaching that very thing as the supreme of all other abidings. ^1-382

###### Identifying Prajñāpāramitā as the Abiding of the Bodhisattva ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-5-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-383]]

First, two verses starting with "The dharmadhātu and thusness": If one asks what the abiding of a bodhisattva is, it is abiding in the perfection of wisdom. What is that like? Since no phenomenon whatsoever is observed other than the dharmadhātu, all phenomena simply become the nature of the dharmadhātu. Having known this, the convention of "the mind abiding in the dharmadhātu" also means not abiding in anything. Since thatness, which is free from all reference points, is designated as the dharmadhātu, there is no basis to be observed in it. Therefore, they "do" "not" "abide" in a referential manner even "in" the reality of entities, the "dharmadhātu", or "thusness", "and" since that is not abiding in anything, it is conventionally called "the abiding of non-abiding". That exalted wisdom that realizes the emptiness of all referential domains as equality "abides" "without" "abiding" in any phenomenon, "for" "example", "like" a "cloud" "in" the "sky" that does not rely on anything. ^1-383

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-384]]

Though not abiding in anything, achieving mastery over the ocean-like liberations of perfecting, maturing, and purifying through the power of that profound abiding of non-abiding is as follows: "Just" "as" a "holder" "of" "vidyā-mantras" "wandering" "in" the "sky" "without" a house or the like made in order for them to "dwell" in, "when" "wishing" "to" "dwell", by the power of vidyā-mantras instantly "blesses" and manifests in that expanse of the sky beautiful parks and so forth as desired, "endowed" "with" pleasing "flowers", fruit trees, and the like, even "if" "it" "is" "not" "time" and they do not grow from the earth and so forth; "likewise", "that" "skilled" "bodhisattva", "whose" mindstream is "lucid" for others, "engaging" in the ways of perfecting, maturing, and purifying just as desired by the blessings of the profound perfection of wisdom of abiding in non-abiding having become the unsurpassable vidyā-mantra, even though they engage in inconceivable perfecting, maturing, and purifying, they "do" "not" "observe" for their own sake the phenomena of the path to "be" "realized" "and" "the" "qualities" "of" "a" "Buddha" to be attained; ^1-384

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-385]]

"and" they do "not" "observe" what is to "be" "explained" for the sake of others "and" "those" "desiring" "Dharma", i.e., those "viewed" as the objects of explaining the Dharma, because they realize the unobservable nature of everything. "This" kind of way "is" merely conventionally the "abiding" of bodhisattvas who "desire" the suffering of other sentient beings to be "pacified" and who themselves "delight" in the "qualities" of the Mahāyāna path and result. ^1-385

###### Showing It to Be Supreme Among All Other Abidings ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-5-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-386]]

Second, three verses starting with "the liberation of the arhats": "Except" for the abiding of the "Tathāgata" himself, who abides in the excellent samādhi of "liberation" that has "destroyed" the "enemies" of the two obscurations, "this" "abiding" of the bodhisattva is "supreme" "among" "all" the abidings of other "śrāvakas" "and" "pratyekabuddhas", "however" "many" there are, "endowed" "with" the undefiled "bliss" of the "utterly" "peaceful" "samādhi" where all afflictions are "pacified", "and" since those others are "not" "above" this, this is the unsurpassable abiding. Even though they abide in the abiding of the samādhi where all fabrications are pacified like that, they do not become one-sidedly peaceful; for example, "although" "birds" "dwell" "in" the unsupported "sky", they "do" "not" "fall" to the ground, and "although" "fish" "dwell" "in" "water", their ears and noses and so forth do not suffocate and they "do" "not" "die"; "likewise", the "bodhisattva", "through" the "strengths" "of" the "dhyāna" of abiding in non-abiding, has "reached" or realized the "far" "shore" of saṃsāra, the expanse of peaceful nirvāṇa, yet even though they "abide" in "emptiness", because they are unsupported they do not fall into the extremes of existence or peace, like a bird; and because they are not attached even to emptiness, just as a fish does not suffocate in its own dwelling of water, they "do" "not" "pass" into the "one-sidedly" peaceful "nirvāṇa". ^1-386

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-387]]

Therefore, whoever "desires" to "go" "to" the "supreme" of all the "qualities" of "all" realms of "sentient" "beings", however many there are, "and" desires to "touch" the "exalted" "wisdom" of a "Buddha", which is supremely "wonderful" and "marvelous", the "supreme" among all phenomena, "and" "desires" to "give" the "gift" "of" "Dharma" that is "excellent", "supreme", "and" "sublime" to sentient beings, they should "rely" "on" "this" "supreme" "abiding" "of" the Victors and their Sons, "who" "act" for the "benefit" of sentient beings. Regarding this, although it appears that it is correlated with going to the state of the dharmakāya that transcends the entire world as the supreme of the qualities of all sentient beings, the saṃbhogakāya endowed with the marvelous exalted wisdom of a Buddha, and the nirmāṇakāya that gives the gift of Dharma to the world; and it is also correlated with the excellent gift of Dharma as the path of merit, the supreme as the path of liberation, and the sublime as the Dharma of the supreme vehicle and so forth, it is also fine to not correlate them individually but treat them as synonyms for the greatness of qualities. Teaching the greatness of being the supreme of all trainings to be known: ^1-387

###### Showing the Greatness of Being the Supreme of All Trainings to Be Known ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-5-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-388]]

Third, regarding teaching that it is the supreme of all trainings, there are two: teaching that it is supreme by way of its essence, and teaching that it is supreme by way of inexhaustibly bringing about the result. ^1-388

###### Showing It to Be Supreme by Way of Essence ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-5-3-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-389]]

Teaching that it is supreme by way of its essence: First, one verse starting with "This training": "This" "training" of the perfection of wisdom is the "supreme" "of" "all" the "trainings" "completely" "declared", "however" "many" are taught, by the perfect Buddhas, the "Guides", by way of various dharma doors as immeasurable "trainings", "and" since there is no training whatsoever that is superior to this, it is "unsurpassable"; therefore, "whichever" "wise" one "desires" to "go" to the "far" "shore", or culmination, "of" "all" "trainings", they "should" "train" "in" "this" perfection of "wisdom", the "training" of a "Buddha". ^1-389

###### Showing It to Be Supreme by Way of Inexhaustibly Bringing Forth the Result ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-5-3-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-390]]

Teaching that it is supreme by way of inexhaustibly bringing about the result: Regarding the second, there are three: brief teaching, extensive explanation, and summary of the meaning. Brief teaching: ^1-390

###### Brief Presentation ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-5-3-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-391]]

First, one verse starting with "This is the dharma treasure": "This" perfection of wisdom is the source of all the inexhaustible dharmas to be explained by the Buddhas of the three times; thus it is the "supreme" great "treasure" "of" "Dharma", "and" since the precious qualities of the result—the strengths and so forth—arise, and all the various jewels of the qualities of the path—the perfections, dhāraṇīs, factors of awakening, and so forth—also arise, it "is" the marvelous, "sublime" "treasury" "of" vast "Dharma", and it is the "lineage" from which "all" "Buddhas" arise, because they are born from it. It is the "treasury" where all the physical and mental "happiness" "and" "joy" "of" "beings" "abide"; for from the realization of this very profound perfection of wisdom arise all profound and vast dharmas, and however many excellent paths there are, up to worldly right view, arise. One should understand that however many worldly and supramundane excellences there are arise by way of observing the base perfection of wisdom, by way of practicing the path perfection of wisdom, and from obtaining the result perfection of wisdom; in the manner of concomitance, if it is absent they are all averted, and if it is present they are present. "Although" "all" "those" "Protectors" "of" the "world" in the "ten" "directions", however many "Buddhas" there are, "past", "future", "and" those residing now, "are" "born" "from" practicing "this" perfection of wisdom, the "dharmadhātu" that constitutes its essence is never "exhausted", remaining free from decrease and increase. Moreover, if the base perfection of wisdom were absent, there would be no practicing it having realized it properly; and if the path were absent, there would of course be no result; but one should know the way that the "wisdom" that realizes and teaches as it is the base dharmadhātu—the non-dual meaning abiding as the lineage or dharmatā—is also the cause. Extensive explanation: ^1-391

###### Detailed Explanation ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-5-3-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-392]]

Second, there are two: explaining by way of outer dependent origination, and explaining by way of inner dependent origination. ^1-392

###### Explaining by Way of Outer Dependent Arising ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-5-3-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-393]]

Regarding the first, there are two: the example, outer dependent origination, and the meaning, explaining the perfection of wisdom. ^1-393

###### The Example: Outer Dependent Arising ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-5-3-2-2-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-394]]

First, one verse starting with "Trees and fruits": Like this, even though there is no change from the essence of the dharmadhātu, the unceasing display of the subject, dependent origination, is that even outer phenomena arise dependently; "however" "many" "trees" "and" "fruits" "and" "flowers" "and" "forests" there are, "all" "these" "perfectly" "arise" "from" the "earth" "and" "grow" upon the earth. "Nevertheless", even though it produces that many trees and so forth again and again, for the "earth" there is "no" complete cessation of its continuity from before, so it is "not" "exhausted" "and" "does" "not" "increase"; because it produces results it alters from its initial state, yet it "does" "not" "diminish" "at" "all", "and" it "does" "not" "conceptually" "fabricate", thinking, "I will produce these results," "and" even though it produces results again and again, it "does" "not" "tire". In this way, due to the cause of the karma of sentient beings, as long as the world remains, when grasses, trees, and so forth grow from the great earth, it is the manner of dependent origination devoid of any other creator; illustrated by this, one should understand that all outer phenomena also grow from their respective causes and form without conceptual fabrication. ^1-394

###### Explaining the Meaning: The Perfection of Wisdom ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-5-3-2-2-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-395]]

Second, one verse starting with "Sons of the Buddhas and": The "sons" of the "Buddhas"—the bodhisattvas—"and" the "śrāvakas", the "pratyekabuddhas", "and" the "gods", "and" the other "beings", humans and so forth, "all" of their "happiness" "and" joyous "dharmas", "however" "many" there are, "all" "these" "arise" "from" the "supreme" "perfection" "of" "wisdom". For, Buddhas arise from the perfection of wisdom, and from their teaching the Dharma, all the qualities of higher states and definite goodness arise; whereas, without their teaching it, one would definitely not even give rise to the worldly right view that understands the cause and effect of virtue and non-virtue, because one is unable to know them by one's own power alone. If worldly right view is absent, all worldly happiness will also not be accomplished, thus it will not arise; let alone the authentic supramundane path and result. Alternatively, the perfection of wisdom means unsurpassable wisdom; that is the intellect that engages unerringly, exactly as it is, in the nature of the two truths. Thus, all intellects that are unerring regarding the way of the two truths are also included within it and belong to its parts, so all wisdoms that accomplish higher states and definite goodness also depend on it, directly or indirectly. In this way, although all the happiness of saṃsāra and nirvāṇa arises from the perfection of wisdom, "for" "that" "wisdom" there is "never" "exhaustion" or "increase", remaining just as it is. ^1-395

###### Explaining by Way of Inner Dependent Arising ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-5-3-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-396]]

Regarding the second, there are two: teaching by way of the example, the forward dependent origination of saṃsāra, and the meaning, the reverse dependent origination of complete purification. ^1-396

###### The Example: The Dependent Arising of Saṃsāra in Forward Order ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-5-3-2-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-397]]

First, one verse starting with "The lowest beings": In this world, in terms of body and resources, the "lowest" "beings" are the three lower realms, the "middling" are humans, "and" the "supreme" are the gods; "however" "many" types of beings there are, "all" "these" "arise" "from" the root, "ignorance", as "stated" "by" the "Sugata".
