As it is said, "All dharmas arise from a cause; that cause the Tathāgata has taught," the conceptual fabrication grasping at "I" and "self", a mental factor that does not see the authentic meaning and grasps erroneously, is called ignorance, and since from that ignorance there arise formations and so forth, the twelve links of dependent origination, when "conditions" of afflictions such as defiled karma and existence "assemble", "this" "machine" "of" "suffering", having the nature of becoming manifold and revolving again and again, "fully" "arises", and "that" "machine" of "ignorance" of the realm of beings "is" "not" "exhausted" by the arising of its result, "nor" "does" it "increase", but remains just as it is. Therefore, because it appears while being unestablished in nature, the non-manifestation of increase or decrease in existence, which is like a magical illusion, is taught from the perspective of convention in terms of the general realms of the universe, which are as vast as the dharmadhātu. ^1-397

###### Showing the Meaning by Way of the Dependent Arising of Complete Purification in Reverse Order ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-5-3-2-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-398]]

Second, one verse starting with "The ways and doors of wisdom": The "wisdom" of a Buddha, the path of no-more-learning, and the path of meditation which is in accord with its "ways", and the path of seeing which is its very "doors", and the "methods" for producing that, the path of preparation, "and" its "root", the path of accumulation, "however" "many" there are, "all" "these" "arise" "from" the "supreme" "perfection" "of" "wisdom", because all these arise from realizing selflessness. When the "conditions", such as accumulating merit through the three kinds of wisdom and spiritual friends, "assemble", the machine of actions making one supramundane, which abandons what is to be abandoned on one's own path and produces the qualities of realization, or this "machine" "of" "wisdom", "fully" "arises", and for its root, the "perfection" "of" "wisdom", there is "no" "exhaustion" by its continuity ever being cut, "nor" "does" it "increase" beyond its prior state, remaining just as it is. Thus, it is also said in the Sūtra Requested by Gaganagañja: "If one fully knows all dharmas / that arise based on causes, / one's treasury will be inexhaustible; / the treasury of Dharma is inconceivable. / The protector of the world has taught / that these four dharmas are inexhaustible: / sentient beings, space, bodhicitta, / and likewise the dharmas of the Buddha. / If they were substantial entities, / they would be completely exhausted; / because they are absent, they do not exhaust; / therefore, they are taught to be inexhaustible." ^1-398

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-399]]

Summarising the meaning. ^1-399

###### Summary of the Meaning ^1-3-2-2-1-1-3-5-3-2-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-400]]

Third, summarising the meaning, one verse starting with "The bodhisattva": In this way, knowing that all dharmas of saṃsāra and nirvāṇa are mere appearances of dependent origination is understanding the profound, cherished point of the Buddha's Dharma, and since it is taught that one who realizes this attains the illumination of Dharma and sees the dharmakāya of the Buddha, when dividing into two truths, relative truth should be understood as the mode of infallible dependent origination. "Any" "bodhisattva" who properly analyses with reasoning "this" dharma of "dependent" "origination", apart from dependent arising being merely unable to withstand analysis, ultimately, has "no" "arising" by its own essence and "no" "exhaustion", that is, no cessation, being primordially equal as the very essence of the dharmadhātu; if one "knows" and becomes "accustomed" to "such" a profound meaning of the "wisdom" perfection where the two truths are indivisible, its function is "like" a "cloudless" "sun" "dispelling" all "darkness" by "emitting" "rays" of "light": having completely "destroyed" the adventitious obscurations, the "density" of "ignorance", "attaining" "naturally" "arising" wisdom non-dual from the dharmadhātu, they "will" "awaken" as Buddhas. Explaining the other five accompanying perfections. ^1-400

###### Explaining the Other Five Perfections ^1-3-2-2-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-401]]

Second, regarding explaining the other five perfections, from the five—meditative concentration, diligence, patience, discipline, and generosity— ^1-401

###### Explaining the Perfection of Concentration ^1-3-2-2-1-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-402]]

Explaining the perfection of meditative concentration. Regarding the first, explaining the perfection of meditative concentration, there are four: training in the meditative concentration that becomes the support for the supreme result, having no attachment to one's own purpose even while training, engaging in the purpose of others by way of non-attachment, and concluding by way of how it becomes supreme or inferior due to the categories of intention. Training in the meditative concentration that becomes the support for the supreme result. Regarding the first, there are two: training in the four meditative concentrations, and training in the four formless meditative absorptions. ^1-402

###### Training in the Four Concentrations ^1-3-2-2-1-2-1-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-403]]

First, one verse starting with "Possessing great power": The bodhisattva "possessing" "great" "power" for accomplishing qualities "dwells" in and also "familiarizes" themselves "with" "the four" "meditative" "concentrations", and at that time, they "do" "not" "rely" on them through apprehending signs, "nor" "do" they "dwell" relishing their taste. "Nevertheless", "these" "four" main parts of "meditative" "concentration", "endowed" with their respective "branches" such as investigation and analysis, "become" the "support" for "attaining" the abandonment and realization of "the supreme" "sacred" "awakening", because the path of seeing and so forth rely upon the mind of meditative concentration. ^1-403

###### Training in the Four Formless Absorptions ^1-3-2-2-1-2-1-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-404]]

Second, one verse starting with "Attaining supreme wisdom": "Dwelling" "in" "meditative" "concentrations", the bodhisattva who has "attained" "supreme" "wisdom" "experiences" the "four" "sacred" "formless" "meditative" "absorptions" but does not become attached to them. Because "these" formless "meditative" "concentrations", being free of sin and suffering and becoming the favorable conditions for clairvoyance and so forth, become conditions that "benefit" the attainment of "the supreme" "sacred" "awakening", they train in them, but "the bodhisattva" "does" "not" "train" in them in "order" to actualize the result of one-sided peace by "exhausting" the "defilements" in their own continuum like a śrāvaka. Having no attachment to one's own purpose even while training. ^1-404

###### Though Training, Being Free from Attachment to One's Own Benefit ^1-3-2-2-1-2-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-405]]

Regarding the second, there are two: the brief presentation and the detailed explanation. ^1-405

###### Brief Indication ^1-3-2-2-1-2-1-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-406]]

First, one verse starting with "This is the quality": "This" "is" "the wonderful" and "marvelous" thing of the bodhisattvas "who" "accumulate" all "qualities". What is it? "Dwelling" "in" the "meditative" "absorption" of "concentration", they realize it to "lack" "signs" through wisdom. "If" the bodhisattvas "dwelling" "in" "that" meditative concentration have their "bodies" "destroyed" and change their lives, they "take" "rebirth" "in" "the desire" "realm" "exactly" "as" they "intended" for the sake of ripening sentient beings and so forth. ^1-406

###### Detailed Explanation ^1-3-2-2-1-2-1-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-407]]

Regarding the second, there are two: the example and the meaning. ^1-407

###### The Example ^1-3-2-2-1-2-1-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-408]]

First, one verse starting with "For example, in Jambudvīpa": Why is it that, in this way, even while attaining the cause—the meditative absorption of concentration—they are not born as the result—a god of meditative concentration—but are born down in the desire realm? It is for the sake of perfecting the dharmas of their own qualities without being attached to meditative concentration. "For" "example", "if" "a human" "of" "Jambudvīpa" who had "never" "before" "gone" "to" "the supreme" "realm" "of" "the gods", such as the "city" "of" "the gods", Sudarśana, later "went" "there" and "saw" "those" delightful "places" "occupied" by the gods, such as palaces and pleasure groves, but without being attached to them "came" "back" "to" "this" Jambudvīpa, just as that person, despite seeing the arrangement of the realm of the gods, is not attached and does not stay there, they "do" "not" "become" "attached" to the places and so forth of this Jambudvīpa. ^1-408

###### The Meaning ^1-3-2-2-1-2-1-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-409]]

Second, one verse starting with "Likewise, the bodhisattva": "Likewise", "holding" "supreme" "qualities" "of" "a bodhisattva" such as wisdom and compassion, "exerting" themselves in the "yoga" of meditative absorption, "dwelling" "in" the "meditative" "absorption" of the four "concentrations" and so forth, without becoming attached to them, they "dwell" "back" in the "desire" "realm", and they have "no" "attachment" to the previous meditative concentration nor to the dharmas of the desire realm. Although born in the desire realm, they are not tainted by the desire and so forth of the desire realm, because, for example, "just" "as" "a lotus" is not tainted "by" "water", they "do" "not" "dwell" "in" "the dharmas" "of" "fools". Engaging in the purpose of others by way of non-attachment. ^1-409

###### Engaging in the Benefit of Others through Being Free from Attachment ^1-3-2-2-1-2-1-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-410]]

Regarding the third, there are two: the brief presentation and the detailed explanation. ^1-410

###### Brief Indication ^1-3-2-2-1-2-1-3-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-411]]

First, one verse starting with "The great being": The purpose for which that bodhisattva, even though attaining the concentrations and formless absorptions, does not take rebirth there but again takes rebirth in the desire realm, is "only" for "that" "great" "being" to "accomplish" the "aim" of "ripening" "sentient" "beings" by means of teaching the Dharma and so forth, "purifying" the buddha-"field", and "perfecting" the "perfections", and not for any other reason. If one thinks that they could just accomplish these three in the formless realm and so forth, because in the formless realm there are no recipients for teaching the Dharma and giving generosity and so forth, they think 'there one "will" "fall" "short" of accomplishing the immense "perfections" of generosity and so forth, which are the causes for generating the "qualities" of "awakening"', and "thus" that bodhisattva "does" "not" "seek" "rebirth" "in" the "formless" "realm". ^1-411

###### Detailed Explanation ^1-3-2-2-1-2-1-3-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-412]]

Regarding the second, there are two: the example and the meaning. ^1-412

###### The Example ^1-3-2-2-1-2-1-3-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-413]]

First, one verse starting with "For example, a person": "For" "example", "even" "after" "a person" "finds" "treasures" "of" "jewels", "then" they "do" "not" "generate" an attitude of personal attachment or "desire" "for" that treasure and leave without taking it. But "at" "another" "time", "that" person "takes" "those" great treasures for the sake of others, and "having" "taken" them, "goes" "to" their own "home" and, "without" "becoming" "attached" to them, gives them to their relatives; one should understand this "just" "as" the example is set out. ^1-413

###### The Meaning ^1-3-2-2-1-2-1-3-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-414]]

Second, one verse starting with "Likewise, the bodhisattva": "Likewise", "the wise" "bodhisattvas" who "give" temporary "joy" "and" ultimate "bliss" to sentient beings, "having" "attained" the "four" "peaceful" "meditative" "absorptions" of "concentration" which pacify the flaws of discordant factors, without becoming attached to being "endowed" with the "bliss" of "concentration", "let" "go" of the "attained" "meditative" "absorptions" of "concentration" which result in rebirth there. "Endowed" "with" "compassion" "for" "beings", they "enter" "back" into the "desire" "realm" because, since there is much suffering in this desire realm, it invigorates the objective focuses for purifying the field; and since there are recipients for accomplishing the perfections, the accumulations are swiftly perfected; and since by teaching the Dharma one can make sentient beings feel renunciation and ripen them, bodhisattvas desiring to swiftly attain buddhahood are born in this desire realm because the accumulations are swiftly perfected here. Concluding by way of the manner in which they become supreme or inferior due to differences in intention. ^1-414

###### Concluding by Way of How They Become Superior and Inferior through Differences in Intent ^1-3-2-2-1-2-1-4-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-415]]

Regarding the fourth, there are two: the inferior manner to be abandoned and the supreme manner to be adopted. ^1-415

###### The Inferior to Be Abandoned ^1-3-2-2-1-2-1-4-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-416]]

First, one verse starting with "If the bodhisattva": "If" the "bodhisattva", even while "dwelling" in the "meditative" "absorptions" of liberation and the four "concentrations", "generates" an "attitude" "desiring" the "vehicle" of the śrāvaka "arhats" and "pratyekabuddhas", from the perspective of the Mahāyāna path, such a state is "unconcentrated", "agitated", "and" their "mind" "becomes" "distracted". For what reason? Because their mind is captivated by that mental engagement with the inferior path, they are distracted from the goal of the Great Vehicle, and therefore they "completely" "fall" "short" of the great goal, the "qualities" of a "buddha" result, "like" a "boatman" whose "boat" "breaks" and does not reach the island of jewels. ^1-416

###### The Way of the Superior to Be Adopted ^1-3-2-2-1-2-1-4-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-417]]

Second, one verse starting with "Furthermore, forms and": "Furthermore", in a manner different from that, it is like this: "even" "if" one is joyful and attached "to" the "five" "qualities" of "desire"—"forms" "and" "sounds", "and" "likewise" "smells" "and" "tastes" "along" "with" "tangibles"—and "becomes" "manifestly" "exerted" in accomplishing them, if one is "free" from desiring the "vehicle" of "arhats" "and" "pratyekabuddhas" and generates the "mind" of unexcelled "awakening", or "delights" "in" the bodhisattva "path", "that" "hero" "should" "be" "known" "to" "be" "constantly" "concentrated". Even if they become distracted by the five qualities of desire, because they do not generate an attitude that is an obstacle to omniscience, their mind becomes one-pointed on the great goal, and so it is said they are concentrated. In this way, all the concentrations of the bodhisattva become branches for attaining the result of perfect awakening, become means embraced by compassion for the sake of others, and are embraced by the wisdom that realizes the lack of a nature, so that one engages in a non-attached manner; this way of explaining the concentration of the Great Vehicle through these three special features is likewise true for all the subsequent perfections as well. ^1-417

###### Explanation of the Perfection of Diligence ^1-3-2-2-1-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-418]]

Explanation of the perfection of diligence. Regarding the second, diligence, there are three: the instruction to exert oneself for the sake of others, exerting oneself without being discouraged, and exerting oneself in the manner of the purity of the three spheres ^1-418

###### Instruction to Strive for the Benefit of Others ^1-3-2-2-1-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-419]]

Instruction to exert oneself for the sake of others. Regarding the first, there are two: the brief presentation and the extensive explanation. ^1-419

###### Brief Indication ^1-3-2-2-1-2-2-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-420]]

First, one verse starting with "Sentient beings, persons": The "bodhisattva" who has donned the armor of accomplishing benefit and bliss "for" the sake of "all" "other" "sentient" "beings", who bear the name of "persons", and who is moreover "completely" "pure" from the stain of selfish desire, "manifestly" "exerts" themselves with respect "in" the "perfection" of "diligence" and "behaves" by means of constant application. "For" "example", "just" "as" a "maidservant" who is a "water"-"drawer", or female water-carrier, has "no" "freedom" and is under the "power" of her "master", "the" hero "bodhisattvas" "act" "under" the "power" "of", or become subject to, "all" "sentient" "beings". ^1-420

###### Detailed Explanation ^1-3-2-2-1-2-2-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-421]]

Regarding the second, there are two: not abandoning exertion for their sake even when they do harm, and the manner of constantly exerting oneself for the sake of others. ^1-421

###### Not Abandoning Diligence for Their Sake Even When Harmed ^1-3-2-2-1-2-2-1-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-422]]

Regarding the first, from between the two, the example and the meaning: ^1-422

###### The Example ^1-3-2-2-1-2-2-1-2-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-423]]

First, one verse starting with "Whether scolded or": Thus, "whether" they are "scolded" by speaking harshly with speech, "or" "even" if they are "constantly" "beaten" with the body, "that" "maidservant" "does" "not" "speak" "words" in "return" "to" her "master" that contradict his orders; not only does she not become angry in return no matter how much anger is shown to her, but "thinking", 'If I make a mistake, "they" "might" "even" "kill" "me", because I am in their power,' she is full of "fear" "with" a "very" "terrified" "mind" and obeys their commands, and is "overcome" "by" the "presence" of that very master. ^1-423

###### The Meaning ^1-3-2-2-1-2-2-1-2-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-424]]

Second, one verse starting with "Likewise, the bodhisattva": "Likewise", as in the example just explained, the bodhisattvas who have "entered" the "path" of supreme "awakening" "for" the sake of unexcelled "awakening" "should" "remain" "like" "servants" "to" "all" "beings". The reason for this is that "from" completely embracing "these" beings with compassion, I "will" "attain" "awakening" "and" the "qualities" will be "perfected"; therefore, looking to their faces and acting in accordance with their intentions is done like a maidservant. If one does not do so and becomes angry at sentient beings, just as a "fire" that "arises" "from" "grass" "and" "wood" "burns" "that" "very" grass and wood, one will be destroyed and killed by the fire of one's own anger, and the life-force of the path to awakening will be stopped. ^1-424

###### The Way of Constantly Striving for the Benefit of Others ^1-3-2-2-1-2-2-1-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-425]]

Second, one verse starting with "My bliss": Thus, because cherishing oneself draws in all the suffering of saṃsāra, while cherishing others attains all the qualities of a Buddha, having understood this, "having" "relinquished" the mind that desires "my" "bliss", "with" a "mind" "free" "of" "hope" for reward and maturation, one "should" "manifestly" "exert" oneself "day" "and" "night" "in" accomplishing the "needs" "of" "other" "sentient" "beings". "Just" "as" a "mother" "attends" "to" her "only" "child" with a mind free from weariness in all respects, "remain" "from" the "depths" of your "thoughts" "without" "weariness" regarding the welfare of sentient beings! Exerting oneself without shrinking back. ^1-425

###### Striving Without Discouragement ^1-3-2-2-1-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-426]]

Regarding the second, not shrinking back, there are two: the brief presentation and the extensive explanation. ^1-426

###### Brief Indication ^1-3-2-2-1-2-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-427]]

First, one verse starting with "Whichever bodhisattva": "Whichever" "bodhisattva", through the power of great compassion, "desires" to remain in this "saṃsāra" for a "long" "time" without limit and without letting it go, "and" by remaining there, "whichever" "yogi" "exerts" themselves in acting for the "sake" of "sentient" "beings" "and" "purifying" "realms", "if" a "weary" "mind" "does" "not" "arise" in them even for a "particle" regarding such a manner, "they" are "endowed" with the "perfection" of "diligence" and will be "without" "distraction" in accomplishing the welfare of beings. ^1-427

###### Detailed Explanation ^1-3-2-2-1-2-2-2-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-428]]

Regarding the second, there are two: that to be abandoned and the remedy. ^1-428

###### What Is to Be Abandoned ^1-3-2-2-1-2-2-2-1-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-429]]

First, one verse starting with "The bodhisattva": "If" an "unskillful" "bodhisattva" "counts" the measure in "tens of millions" of "eons" and so on, thinking, 'If I exert myself for this long, I will attain unexcelled awakening,' and, with a "perception" of such a duration as "long" and a perception of "suffering" with respect to difficult practices such as giving away one's head and limbs, "accomplishes" "awakening", "at" "that" "time", while "accomplishing" the "true" "Dharma", unexcelled awakening, through the power of those very perceptions, they "will" "suffer" for a "long" "time". "By" "this", they "degenerate" from the "perfection" of "diligence" and become "lazy". This is because the nature of diligence is joy in virtue, and if one has that joy, one is able to engage in that action without shrinking back, whereas through perceptions of a long time and so on, one's joy degenerates and one becomes discouraged. Even in the world, when one has intense joy for a certain purpose, one gladly travels even on a path of a hundred yojanas, whereas when one is without joy, one looks with dread even upon traveling a single krośa. Therefore, through that skill in means wherein the wisdom that realizes the absence of nature and great compassion enter into union, one should generate the limitless and boundless mind that thinks, 'I shall accomplish perfection, maturation, and purification boundlessly and limitlessly, equally with the boundary that reaches the limit of the boundless realms of sentient beings!' This is the armor-like diligence of a bodhisattva, and by engaging through such armor, as one becomes accustomed to the path, diligence increases accordingly, and its limit becomes immeasurable. Having understood this in accordance with the liberations of Mañjuśrī and others, one should engage in this way. ^1-429

###### The Antidote ^1-3-2-2-1-2-2-2-1-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-430]]

Regarding the second, there are two: with respect to time, and with respect to entities. ^1-430

###### In Terms of Time ^1-3-2-2-1-2-2-2-1-1-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-431]]

First, one verse starting with "Toward supreme awakening": Thus, the manner of exerting oneself in diligence that abandons the perception of time as long and the perception of things as difficult to do is as follows: "Taking" as a starting point whichever was the "first" "generation of the mind" "toward" "supreme" "awakening", "until" one finally "attains" "unexcelled" "awakening", "if" one "thinks" "in" one's "mind" that all this duration is easy to pass, like, for example, "the measure" of "a single" "day" "and" "night", and one becomes joyful, "one" "should" "know" that such a manner of "exerting" "diligence" is "by" the "luminous" wisdom of the "wise". If, despite having continuously experienced unbearable suffering in this saṃsāra without beginning since prior eons, one feels no weariness even though that has accomplished no benefit whatsoever, whereas now if one accomplishes this path that establishes the great purpose and ultimate result, it is suitable to be accomplished and progresses increasingly well, so thinking 'why generate weariness toward this?' one becomes joyful; through the power of that, the perceptions of long duration and difficulty are outshined, and thus, through the manner of a perception of short duration even toward a long duration, one's diligence will increase by way of bliss, without suffering. This will be just like, for example, upon seeing the profit of jewels, one does not think of a long and difficult path as difficult, but generates a perception of bliss. Therefore, one should know that whether something is difficult to do or not difficult to do comes about through the power of the mind, independent of the things to be done. This is like bodhisattvas generating joy and a perception of bliss even toward giving away their bodies or entering the Avīci hell for the sake of others. ^1-431

###### In Terms of Entities ^1-3-2-2-1-2-2-2-1-1-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-432]]

Second, two verses starting with "If someone": "If" "someone" speaks "this" "saying", 'Destroy "this" "mountain", "Mount" "Meru", the king of mountains, and "after" destroying "this", you will "attain" "supreme" "awakening",' and "from" "that", thinking, 'Why? This Mount Meru is so large and firm, it is difficult to destroy,' one "generates" a "weary" mind, "and" "if" one "thinks" of a "measure", wondering, 'In how many years or eons will I be able to destroy this?' then at "that" "time", "that" "bodhisattva", accompanied by such a shrinking mind, "has" "become" "lazy", because their joy has degenerated. "In" "that" "way", if "that" bodhisattva thinks, 'If unexcelled awakening can be attained by the "measure" of "just" "this" diligence, then "look", since "this" can gradually be destroyed, "what" is "difficult" about "this"? It is not difficult!' and without measuring the duration, "generates" an intensely joyful "mind" as if able to "pulverize" "that" "Mount" "Meru" into "dust" "in" "just" a "moment" of time, at that time, "that" "bodhisattva" who is "wise" regarding what is the case and what is not the case "has" indeed "exerted" "diligence". Endowed with diligence that does not shrink from what is difficult to do, through such a manner of joy for omniscience, they "will" "attain" the "supreme" "awakening" of the "Guides" "without" "taking" "long", because they have such diligence, which is the cause for attaining it. ^1-432

###### Striving by Way of the Purity of the Three Spheres ^1-3-2-2-1-2-2-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-433]]

Exerting oneself through the manner of purifying the three spheres. Regarding the third, there are two: that which is to be turned away from, and that which is to be engaged in. ^1-433

###### What Is to Be Avoided ^1-3-2-2-1-2-2-3-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-434]]

First, one verse starting with "Beings thoroughly": Thus, even when exerting diligence, one who has a referent becomes, through the power of that, completely impurely lazy: If one asks how, from the state of a perception that grasps at signs, thinking, 'Having "thoroughly" "ripened" "beings", I will "do" the "welfare" of others,' "if" one "exerts" oneself "through" "body", "speech", "and" "mind", because one "dwells" in the "perception" of "self", in relation to non-referential diligence, one "has" "become" "lazy"; the reason for that is that by that view which has a referent, the diligence does not become genuine, and having fallen into its opposite, one "will" "be" "far" "from" "omniscience", "like", for example, the great distance between "sky" "and" "earth". ^1-434

###### What Is to Be Engaged In ^1-3-2-2-1-2-2-3-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-435]]

Second, one verse starting with "Whenever body and": "Whenever" exerting diligence, one "has" "no" "perception" of the agent's own "body" "and" "mind" "and" other "beings", and "having" "turned" "away" from "perceptions" of the three spheres of exertion, "this" "conduct" in the way of "Dharma" that is "free" "from" "dual" extremes, or self and other, is the very "perfection" of "diligence" of bodhisattvas who "desire" the "unfailing", "supreme" state of great "awakening", which is "peaceful" of stains, "so" "taught" the "Benefactors", the Victors. Explanation of the perfection of patience. ^1-435

###### Explanation of the Perfection of Patience ^1-3-2-2-1-2-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-436]]

Regarding the third, patience, there are three: the patience of certain thought regarding the Dharma, the patience of not being upset by harm, and the patience of accepting suffering. ^1-436

###### The Patience of Ascertaining the Dharma ^1-3-2-2-1-2-3-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-437]]

The patience of certain thought regarding the Dharma. Regarding the first, there are two: the actual and its benefits. ^1-437

###### The Actual Subject ^1-3-2-2-1-2-3-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-438]]

First, one verse starting with "If from another": "If" one "hears" "harsh" "words" "spoken" "badly" by "another", such as blaming oneself, etc., since patience arises just through having thus found an object of patience, thinking '"this" "is" the cause of "my" "happiness",' "that" "bodhisattva" who is "wise" in means "becomes" "joyful". Furthermore, when examining from the perspective of ultimate reality, thinking, 'Who is the speaker who "speaks" "this" harsh word, and "who" "hears" it? "By" "what" cause, such as anger, is "what" bad thing spoken? "For" the sake of "what" purpose, such as disappointing another, is it spoken?', having realized the appearances of dependent origination to be like illusions lacking established essence, "one" "endowed" with the "supreme" "perfection" of "patience" "is" "wise", because of understanding the meaning that there is no reason to be angry at mere phenomena arising from conditions. Fools who do not know this get angry in response and ruin themselves and others. ^1-438

###### Its Benefits ^1-3-2-2-1-2-3-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-439]]

Second, two verses starting with "A bodhisattva": If "a" "bodhisattva" "is" "endowed" with the "good" "quality" of "patience", then "a" "mass" of "generosity" "made" by "filling" "this" "trichiliocosm" with various "jewels" and "offering" it to the "Knower" of the "World", the "Buddhas", "and" to the "arhats", and to the pratyekabuddhas, "cannot" rival even a "fraction" of the "merit" of "that" one endowed with the nature of patience. ^1-439

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-440]]

The ripened result of "dwelling" "in" "patience" is that one's "body" "will" "become" "thoroughly" "pure" like gold, being endowed with the thirty-"two" "excellent" "marks" and "endless" "power" and "strength"; the functionally active result of patience of certain thought regarding the Dharma is that one "will" "proclaim" to "beings" the "supreme" "Dharma", emptiness; the dominant result is that "in" "this" world, "beings" "will" "rejoice" "in" "that" one "endowed" with "patience" and will rely upon them; and the result of separation is that one will become "wise", free from delusion. ^1-440

###### The Patience of Enduring Harm ^1-3-2-2-1-2-3-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-441]]

The patience of not being upset by harm. Regarding the second, the patience of not caring about harm, there are two: how to practice patience, and the result of such patience. ^1-441

###### How to Practice Patience ^1-3-2-2-1-2-3-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-442]]

First, one verse starting with "If some beings": "If" "some" "beings", "having" "taken" a "parcel", i.e., a bundle, "of" "sandalwood", "rub" and anoint "it" "on" the body of "the" "bodhisattva" with a "supremely" "devoted" mind, and "another", with a harmful mind, "scatters" "glowing" "coals" "on" their "head", toward "both" of "them" "that" bodhisattva "should" "generate" "an" "equal" "and" "even" "mind", without attachment or aversion. ^1-442

###### The Result of Such Patience ^1-3-2-2-1-2-3-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-443]]

Second, one verse starting with "A bodhisattva": When "a" "bodhisattva" who is "wise" in the way of ultimate reality "has" "practiced" "patience" "like" "that", "if" "that" "generation" of "mind" endowed with patience "is" "thoroughly" "dedicated" "to" "supreme" "awakening", "that" "heroic" bodhisattva, "since" they "are" "patient" for the benefit of "all" "the" "world", "will" "outshine" śrāvaka "arhats" "and" "pratyekabuddhas", and "as" "many" "many" "realms" of other "beings" as there are. From another sūtra: "Śāriputra, the patience of a śrāvaka is limited because it abandons merely one's own afflictions, whereas the patience of a bodhisattva is immeasurable because it is for the sake of all beings," and it is taught using the examples of the difference between gold of the Jambu River and brass, Mount Meru and a "mustard" "seed", and the ocean and a drop of water on the tip of a hair. ^1-443

###### The Patience of Accepting Suffering ^1-3-2-2-1-2-3-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-444]]

The patience of accepting suffering. Showing the third, the patience of "accepting" suffering, two verses starting with "The one practicing patience thinks thus": "The" "one" "practicing" "patience" "should" "also" "generate" "the" "thought", "thinking" "thus": 'In "the" "hells", "the" "animal" realm, "and" "the" "world" "of" "Yama", i.e., the realm of preta beings, there are "many" "sufferings" that are difficult to endure, such as heat, cold, hunger, and thirst, and "powerless" "because of" the "cause" of attachment "to" "the" "qualities" "of" "desire", they "experience" those "harms", and having not accomplished any benefit for oneself or others, one experiences suffering pointlessly; "so" "for" "the" "sake" "of" unsurpassed "awakening", which accomplishes the benefit of oneself and others, "why" "should" "I" "not" "practice" "patience" right now?' Thinking, '"As" "many" "sufferings" "as" there are "in" "the" "world", such as "being" "struck" "with" "iron", "beaten" "with" "sticks", "pierced" "with" "weapons", "killed" by having one's life force stopped, "bound" "with" iron fetters and so on, "struck" "with" stones and hammers and so on, "having" "one's" "head" "cut" "off", "having" "one's" "ears", "hands" "and" "feet", "and" "nose" "cut" "off", "and" so forth, "I" shall voluntarily accept and "endure" them,' "that" "bodhisattva" "who" "thinks" thus "will" "abide" "in" "the" "perfection" "of" "patience". Explanation of the perfection of discipline. ^1-444

###### Explanation of the Perfection of Discipline ^1-3-2-2-1-2-4-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-445]]

Regarding the fourth, discipline, there are two: the benefits of being endowed with supreme discipline, and the explanation of the classification of that discipline into supreme and inferior. ^1-445

###### The Benefits of Possessing Supreme Discipline ^1-3-2-2-1-2-4-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-446]]

The benefits of being endowed with supreme discipline. First, one verse starting with "Through discipline": "Those" "desiring" "peace", i.e., nirvāṇa, "will" "become" "superior" "through" "discipline", because that discipline is like the legs for going to liberation. Regarding the specific essence, the discipline of a bodhisattva is "abiding" in the "domain" of "the" "ten" "powers", and since the one with the ten powers is the Buddha and it is the discipline that trains after him, it is unconnected with mental engagements of the inferior path and so on, and untainted by the stains of all the objects of abandonment on the Mahāyāna path, so it is "without" "impaired" "discipline"; this teaches the discipline of restraining faulty behavior. "The" "activity" of "restraint", i.e., discipline, "follows" "after" "all" virtuous dharmas, "however" "many" they are, because guarding against the discordant class and engaging in the remedies itself becomes the essence of discipline, so this is called the discipline of gathering virtuous dharmas. Guarding such discipline "for" "the" "benefit" "of" "all" "beings" and also "dedicating" it to great "awakening" is the discipline of working for the benefit of beings. Having thus briefly taught the nature of discipline, ^1-446

###### Explanation of the Divisions of Superior and Inferior Discipline ^1-3-2-2-1-2-4-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-447]]

Explanation of the classification of that discipline into supreme and inferior. Regarding the second, the explanation of the classification of that discipline into supreme and inferior, there are three classifications: by way of complete dedication, by way of motivation, and by way of the presence or absence of conceit. ^1-447

###### Division by Way of Dedication ^1-3-2-2-1-2-4-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-448]]

Classification by way of complete dedication. First, one verse starting with "If an arhat": "If" one guards discipline with a mind "desiring" to "touch", i.e., attain, the "awakening" of śrāvaka "arhats" and "pratyekabuddhas", relative to this Mahāyāna, that person has "faulty" "discipline", their intention is "unwise", and "likewise" their application, or "conduct", is "impaired"; because they fall away from the great purpose, their intention and application are impaired. "However", "if" one "thoroughly" "dedicates" that for which discipline is guarded "supremely" to the "peace" of the two obscurations, unexcelled "awakening", then "even" if that person abides in the manner of being "engaged" with the five "qualities" of "desire", they are said to be "abiding" "in" "the" "perfection" "of" "discipline", because by engaging for the sake of the supreme path and result, they are unimpaired. Classification by way of motivation. ^1-448

###### Division by Way of Motivation ^1-3-2-2-1-2-4-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-449]]

Regarding the second, there are two: the brief teaching and the detailed explanation. ^1-449

###### Brief Indication ^1-3-2-2-1-2-4-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-450]]

First, one verse starting with "Whatever dharma is certain": "Whatever" "dharma" of training is "certainly", by way of motivation, what "will" "produce" the "qualities" of supreme "awakening", the goal of bodhisattvas, "this" "is" taught as the "purpose" of the "discipline" "of" "those" bodhisattvas "endowed" with the "dharma" of "qualities"; and "whatever" "dharma", being grasped by an inferior motivation, "completely" "falls away" from great "awakening", the result to be attained by "those" "beneficial" bodhisattvas, "this" "is" "faulty" "discipline" of the Mahāyāna, "thus" it is "taught" "by" the "Guide". ^1-450

###### Detailed Explanation ^1-3-2-2-1-2-4-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-451]]

Regarding the second, the detailed explanation, there are two: the supreme to be engaged in, and the inferior to be turned away from. ^1-451

###### The Supreme Object of Engagement ^1-3-2-2-1-2-4-2-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-452]]

First, one verse starting with "If a bodhisattva": "If" a beginner "bodhisattva" "even" "engages" in the manner of being attached to the "five" "qualities" of "desire", but "has" "gone" for "refuge" from the heart to the "Buddha", the "Dharma", "and" the "noble" "Sangha", and "if" they are "not" "separated" from the "mental" "engagement" on "omniscience", "thinking", 'I "will" "accomplish" the state of a "Buddha"!', then "that" one who is "wise" regarding the ultimate meaning "should" "be" "known" "as" "abiding" "in" "the" "perfection" "of" "discipline". ^1-452

###### The Inferior Object of Avoidance ^1-3-2-2-1-2-4-2-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-453]]

Second, one verse starting with "If for ten million eons": "If", "even" though they have "practised" the "ten" "paths" of "virtuous" "action" "for" "ten million" "eons", they "generate" a "desire" "for" the stage "of" śrāvaka "arhats" "and" "pratyekabuddhas", "at" "that" "time", a "fault" "has arisen" "in" the "discipline" of the Mahāyāna, and their authentic "discipline" "is" "impaired". For a follower of the Mahāyāna to "generate" the "mind" directed towards the stage of śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas "is" an "offence" "extremely" "heavier" "than" the occurrence of the four "defeats" of a monk for that bodhisattva; because even if a defeat occurs, if they have not given up the generation of the mind set on supreme awakening, by that alone they will not be devoid of the fortune for the path of great awakening, whereas by that generation of an inferior mind, until it is given up, it renders them devoid of the fortune to proceed to the path and result of the Mahāyāna. ^1-453

###### Division by Way of the Presence or Absence of Conceit ^1-3-2-2-1-2-4-2-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-454]]

Classification by way of the presence or absence of conceit. Regarding the third, there are two: what is to be adopted and what is to be abandoned. ^1-454

###### The Object of Adoption ^1-3-2-2-1-2-4-2-3-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-455]]

First, one verse starting with "Guarding discipline": While "guarding" "discipline" "and" "completely" "dedicating" it "to" "supreme" "awakening", if they "have" "no" "conceit" through apprehending "that" discipline as a characteristic, "and" "do" "not" "praise" "themselves" by way of that discipline and so forth, "and" if they "have" "completely" "abandoned" the "notion" "of" 'I' "and" the "notion" "of" a "sentient being", "such" a "bodhisattva" "is" "said" to be "abiding" "in" "the" "perfection" "of" "discipline". ^1-455

###### The Object of Abandonment ^1-3-2-2-1-2-4-2-3-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-456]]

Regarding the second, there are two: the actual thing to be abandoned, and teaching its antidote. ^1-456

###### The Actual Object of Abandonment ^1-3-2-2-1-2-4-2-3-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-457]]

First, one verse starting with "If a bodhisattva": "If" "those" "bodhisattvas" "engaging" in the Mahāyāna, the "path" "of" the "Victors", are endowed with adherence, "having" "entered" into "various" "notions", thinking, '"These" "sentient beings" are "endowed" with "discipline" and "these" are of "faulty" "discipline"', by that very notion alone they are said to be of "extremely" "faulty" "discipline". Being endowed with adherence and thereby belittling others and so forth, by that stain, the "discipline" of the Mahāyāna is "impaired" "and" "that" person "has" "no" "completely" "pure" "discipline", because it has been impaired by that notion involving apprehension. ^1-457

###### Indication of Its Antidote ^1-3-2-2-1-2-4-2-3-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-458]]

Second, one verse starting with "In whomever": "In" a person "who" "lacks" the "notion" "of" 'I' "and" the "notion" "of" a "sentient being", who solely realizes the selflessness of persons, for that one who is thus "free" from the "notion" of adhering to a self "and" the "desire" that arises by the power of that, "how" "could" "there" "be" "non-vow", or faulty discipline? Since whatever non-virtues there are have the notion of attachment to a self as their root, it is impossible for the entity of negativity to arise in the absence of that, because the cause is absent. "In" a person "who", by way of knowing all dharmas to lack inherent nature, "lacks" the "conceit" that apprehends a duality of "vow" "and" "non-vow", or faulty discipline, "this" "is" "taught" "by" the "Guide" "as" the "vow" "of" "discipline" of a bodhisattva, because by being endowed with this manner, they are endowed with the discipline pleasing to the noble ones, isolated from all misconduct, and furthermore, being without apprehension, it has become completely pure. Explanation of the perfection of generosity. ^1-458

###### Explanation of the Perfection of Generosity ^1-3-2-2-1-2-5-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-459]]

Regarding the fifth, generosity, there are two: delighting in generosity having known the faults and qualities of giving and not giving, and the manner of giving generosity. ^1-459

###### Taking Joy in Generosity Having Known the Qualities of Giving and the Faults of Not Giving ^1-3-2-2-1-2-5-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-460]]

Delighting in generosity having known the faults and qualities of giving and not giving. Regarding the first, there are three: the manner of a bodhisattva giving generosity, the fault of ordinary people not giving in that way, and the manner of being intent on generosity having known that. ^1-460

###### The Way a Bodhisattva Gives ^1-3-2-2-1-2-5-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-461]]

First, two verses starting with "A pure sentient being": "Whatever" "pure" "sentient being", who by the power of being free from notions and attachment "as" just explained "is" "endowed" with pure "discipline", "does" "not" "view" "any" pleasing "and" "unpleasing" things as having characteristics as such. They "give" even their "head" "and" "limbs" to others, "and" at that time "have" "no" "shrinking" "mind" of inability. In that manner, they "give" "all" that is "owned" by them "and" "always" "have" "no" "attachment" to all they own. Since, "having" "known" in this way "all" "dharmas" to lack "inherent nature" and to lack a "self", being "hollow" mere dependent arisings, they "give" "even" "their" "own" "flesh" and "no" "shrinking" "mind" arises in them, "at" "that" "time", "what" "need" "is" "there" "to" "mention" "giving" "outer" "things"? "If" they "were" "to" "be" "miserly", "that" "is" entirely "impossible", because they have no attachment. ^1-461

###### The Fault of Ordinary Beings Not Giving in That Way ^1-3-2-2-1-2-5-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-462]]

Second, one verse starting with "The notion of a self": "How" "could" "fools" who are attached "by" "apprehending" "as" 'mine' "things" such as the eyes and having the "notion" "of" 'I' towards the meaning of things while they do not exist "have" a "mind" "of" "giving" like that? They do not. "The" "miserly" "will" "be" "born" "in" "the" "realm" "of" "hungry ghosts", "and" "even" "if", "by" "some" "rare" "chance", they are "born" "human", "at" "that" "time" they "will" "be" "poor". ^1-462

###### The Way to Aspire to Generosity Having Known That ^1-3-2-2-1-2-5-1-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-463]]

Third, one verse starting with "At that time, a bodhisattva": "Having" "known" "that" "at" "that" "time", being born in existence in this way, "sentient beings" have become "poor" due to miserliness, "bodhisattvas" themselves eradicate the mind of miserliness and so their intention "will" "be" "intent" "on" "giving", "and" in application they "always" "give" all they own "freely", without holding back. Knowing that "even" "if" "these" "four" "continents", "well" "adorned" with their array, were left ungiven, they become of the nature of impermanent conditioned things and lack any essence that can be relied upon, "like" "for" "example" "a" "drop" "of" "spittle"—like a drop of spittle cast away—in order to take essence from the essenceless, "having" "given" those continents to others, a bodhisattva "will" "rejoice", "but" if they were to "obtain" those "continents", they "would" "not" rejoice "like" "that", for the very reason just explained. The manner of giving generosity. ^1-463

###### The Way Generosity Should Be Given ^1-3-2-2-1-2-5-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-464]]

Regarding the second, the manner of giving, there are two: the actual manner and its benefits. ^1-464

###### The Actual Subject ^1-3-2-2-1-2-5-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-465]]

Regarding the first, there are three: giving for the benefit of others out of compassion, being endowed with complete dedication through skillful means, and giving without attachment to ripening results and things. ^1-465

###### Giving out of Compassion for the Benefit of Others ^1-3-2-2-1-2-5-2-1-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-466]]

First, three lines starting with "A bodhisattva": "That" "wise", "clear-minded" "bodhisattva", when giving generosity, even just a single morsel of food, preceded by a compassionate intention, "gives" "generosity", "having" "thought", '"May" "this" "generosity" "be" "given" to "all" "those" "sentient beings", "however" "many" there are included in the realms "of" "the" "three" "existences", so as to accomplish flawless wealth and the results of all who have entered the three vehicles, and to cause all to attain the unsurpassed result of a Buddha.' ^1-466

###### Endowed with Complete Dedication through Skillful Means ^1-3-2-2-1-2-5-2-1-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-467]]

Second, one line starting with "That too, for beings": "That" merit of giving generosity "too" "is" "completely" "dedicated" "for" "the" "sake" "of" all realms "of" "beings", so that oneself and all sentient beings will manifestly awaken "to" "supreme" "awakening". ^1-467

###### Giving without Attachment to Karmic Results and Things ^1-3-2-2-1-2-5-2-1-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-468]]

Third, two lines starting with "Having given generosity": "Having" "given" "generosity" in that way, a bodhisattva "does" "not" "rest" their mind by way of hoping for and being attached to the substance to be given and the "things" in return, or by way of clinging to them as possessing signs. Not only that, "that" bodhisattva "never" "hopes" that they will own the "ripening" of that generosity, because they give for the sake of sentient beings without attachment to things along with their ripening results. ^1-468

###### Benefits ^1-3-2-2-1-2-5-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-469]]

Regarding the second, the benefits, there are two: general and specific. ^1-469

###### General ^1-3-2-2-1-2-5-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-470]]

First, two lines starting with "Giving like that": The bodhisattva who is "wise" in "giving" "like" "that", as just explained, through possessing the mental application of skillful means, "gives" without attachment to "all" they own and its ripening results. By doing so, "even" "by" "giving" a "small" thing, they "give" "much", "immeasurable" generosity, and its ripening result too will become similarly immeasurable. ^1-470

###### Specific ^1-3-2-2-1-2-5-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-471]]

Regarding the second, the specific, there are two: the benefits of generosity endowed with skillful means such as compassion, and the benefits of giving without attachment. ^1-471

###### The Benefits of Generosity Endowed with Skillful Means Such as Compassion ^1-3-2-2-1-2-5-2-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-472]]

First, three verses starting with "In the three existences": "Supposing" as an extreme example "all" "sentient beings", "however" "many", without a single exception, "in" "the" "three" "existences", "gave" extensive "generosity" "to" the transcendent victor "Buddhas", the "knowers" "of" "the" "world", "and" the śrāvaka "arhats", "and" "pratyekabuddhas" "for" "boundless" "eons", and sought "well" the awakening of a "śrāvaka" as its result; "if" "some" "wise" "bodhisattva" of the Great Vehicle, endowed with "skillful means", in a single moment of completing mental activity, "were" "to" "rejoice" "in" "that" "thing" "consisting" "of" "merit" of "those" just explained, ^1-472

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-473]]

"and" "dedicate" it "for" "the" "sake" of all "sentient beings" to the level of a Buddha, the "most" "supreme" "awakening", by generating just a single mind "dedicating" it like that, they "outshine" the roots of virtue of "all" "beings". "For" "example", just as "a" "large" "heap" of accumulated "glass" "jewels", though "great", can be "outshone" "by" "a" "single" precious "lapis lazuli" jewel, the king of jewels, "likewise" "a" "bodhisattva", through a single, marvelous "rejoicing", "outshines" "all" the extremely "large" "heaps" "of" "generosity" "of" "all" other "beings". ^1-473

###### The Benefits of Giving without Attachment ^1-3-2-2-1-2-5-2-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-474]]

Second, one verse starting with "If a bodhisattva": "If" "a" "bodhisattva" "gives" "generosity" "to" "beings" and "does" "not" "apprehend" "that" merit of generosity "as" 'mine', and also has "no" clinging to "that" "thing" to be given, giving in a manner of being extremely unattached like that, in giving such generosity, "whichever" "roots" "of" "virtue" possessing "great" "power" to complete the accumulations and abandon obscurations "will" "increase" higher and higher. The incompatible side of generosity is acting miserly due to attachment, but here, because they have given by ensuring that attachment permanently finds no opportunity, it is, "for" "example", "like" "the" "phases" "of" "the" "waxing" "moon" "free" "from" "clouds" and endowed with clear "rays" "of" "light". ^1-474

###### The Result of the Path ^1-3-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-475]]

The results of the path. Regarding the second, explaining the results of the path, there are two: the specific results of the path and the results of the completely perfected path. ^1-475

###### The Result of the Specific Path ^1-3-2-2-2-1-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-476]]

The specific results of the path. First, sixteen lines or four verses starting with 'By the generosity of a bodhisattva' demonstrate this: Thus, there is no path of a bodhisattva that is not included within the six perfections, and therefore it is said that the Great Vehicle and the extensive texts of the perfection of wisdom are also implicitly included within them. Having well demonstrated the practices of such six perfections above, now their results are explained: 'By' the 'generosity' 'of' a 'bodhisattva', they 'cut' off the continuum of birth in the 'destinies' of 'hungry ghosts', 'and' 'poverty' in enjoyments; 'likewise', they 'cut' off 'all' 'afflictions' as well. By giving objectless generosity themselves, they abandon all afflictions such as miserliness and ignorance, and by giving the generosity of Dharma to others, they cut off all their afflictions too. Even 'when' a bodhisattva 'engages' in conduct, in all lifetimes they 'will' 'attain' 'boundless' 'enjoyments' as much as they desire due to their abundance, 'and' inexhaustible, increasing 'vastness', and having gathered them 'through' the 'generosity' of material things and Dharma, they 'completely' 'ripen' 'sentient beings' 'who' 'are' situated in the causes and results of 'suffering'. ^1-476

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-477]]

'By' 'discipline', they abandon 'the' 'nature' 'of' 'the' 'many' 'destinies' 'of' 'animals' which have become diverse in varied families, forms, and so forth, 'and' not only that, they 'abandon' all 'eight' states 'of' 'no' 'leisure' too, and 'by' 'that' discipline, they 'always' 'find' a good basis 'of' 'leisure'. 'By' 'patience', they 'will' 'attain', up to a Buddha's body well adorned with the marks and signs, 'great' 'and' 'supreme' 'beautiful' 'forms'; they 'will' 'become' 'attractive' like a 'golden' color 'and' 'worthy' 'to' 'be' 'looked' 'at' 'by' beings. 'By' 'diligence', through the power of 'qualities' 'of' 'white' phenomena 'not' 'diminishing' but increasing higher and higher, they 'will' 'find' the 'treasury' of the Victor's qualities, 'boundless' 'exalted' 'wisdom'. 'By' 'concentration', they 'cast' 'aside' what is worthy of being 'blamed', the 'sensory' 'qualities' 'of' 'the' 'desire' realm, since their faults are great and their happiness is small, 'and' 'the' 'three' 'knowledges' of former abodes, the exhaustion of defilements, and knowing others' minds, 'and' the three 'superknowledges' of the divine ear, divine eye, and magical emanation—these six combined with the former—and 'samādhis' such as the lion's stretch, they 'will' 'accomplish'. 'By' 'wisdom', 'having' 'completely' 'known' 'the' 'nature' 'of' 'all' 'phenomena' as emptiness, they abandon apprehension of entities and 'will' 'genuinely' 'transcend' 'from' 'the' 'three' 'realms' 'without' 'exception', 'and' having become a Buddha, the Dharma King, 'the' 'supreme' 'leader' 'among' 'humans' endowed with the two bodies, and having continuously 'turned' 'the' 'precious' 'wheel' of enlightened activity wielding power in all world systems, in 'order' 'to' permanently 'exhaust' all 'suffering', they 'will' 'also' 'teach' 'the' supreme light 'of' the holy 'Dharma' 'to' 'beings'. The results of the completely perfected path. ^1-477

###### The Result of the Fully Completed Path ^1-3-2-2-2-2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-478]]

Second, one verse starting with "Having completely perfected this Dharma": Whoever abides in the Mahayana family, starting from when they first generate the mind of awakening, they practice "this" "Dharma" of the Mahayana path, the six perfections as explained, without exception, for three countless eons and so forth in a manner of skillful means. What is skillful means in that? It is rolling all those perfections into a single meaning without separating them, such that they mutually act as friends to one another, and practicing all of them perfectly within each one, and also practicing them in a manner where all of them become the essence of the perfection of wisdom endowed with all supreme aspects. By practicing in that way, "having" "completely" "perfected" the practice of all perfections to the utmost limit, that "bodhisattva" attains the great exalted wisdom at the end of the continuum of the tenth ground, which is able to actualize the dharmadhatu endowed with all supreme aspects, in which all undefiled phenomena are completely perfect, through the exalted wisdom endowed with a single instant. Relying on that, that learner becomes a non-learner, becoming manifestly perfectly a Buddha on the ground of great awakening, and by the power of having found the ultimate transformation of basis, thus, they "completely" "hold" a "completely" "pure" "buddhafield" that reaches the limits of space, without being partial or restricted, the naturally appearing realm of Akaniṣṭha, "and" as retinue, completely "pure" "sentient" "beings" as its contents, the great bodhisattvas of the tenth "ground". And, by attaining the body of exalted wisdom that has become equal to "all" "Buddhas", they completely hold the "continuum" of a Buddha, the nature of the three bodies demonstrating as a teacher in the ten directions of world systems for as long as infinite space exists, possessing a character that is without limits of direction "and" ends of time; "and" they "also" "completely" "hold" the "continuum" "of" the mode of "Dharma" of transmission and realization, which is without boundaries or limits; "likewise", the "continuum" of the noble "Sangha" practicing that Dharma of the Buddha in the world systems of the ten directions, which is without boundaries "and" limits, and they "completely" "hold" "all" "phenomena" of benefit and happiness belonging to existence and peace through great enlightened activity. They attain the ultimate result, the exalted wisdom body extending to the limits of the dharmadhatu, which has reached the culmination of abandonment and realization, is permanent, steadfast, and unchanging like space, and, like a wish-fulfilling jewel, performs benefit and happiness for as long as the boundaries and limits of samsara exist through spontaneously present great enlightened activity. ^1-478

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-479]]

The conclusion to that. ^1-479

##### Concluding with the Purpose of Teaching Thus ^1-3-2-3-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-480]]

Third, concluding with the necessity for teaching in that way: one verse starting with "Curing the illnesses of beings": The Buddha is "the" "supreme" "physician" "who" "cures" "the" "illnesses" of body and mind "of" "beings" without exception by means of pacifying them from the root. This is because the Tathāgatas cure the root of all suffering that has become illness, which is the apprehension of entities, along with all afflictions arising from that, and all destitution of existence and peace arising from afflictive and cognitive obscurations, by means of the nectar of the Dharma of the perfection of wisdom. From the mouth of such a king of physicians, the Buddha, the Lord, the Lion of the Śākyas himself, "having" "taught" the subject matter, the perfection of "wisdom"—if one asks what its features are like, he "taught" "this" which is "endowed" with all the profound and vast phenomena of the Mahayana, "the" "path" "to" "supreme" "awakening". As for the "name" of this sutra, because it contains and gathers in here all the precious "qualities" of the mundane and supramundane paths and results without exception, it is called *The "Precious" "Summary" of "Qualities", "the" "Path" "to" "Supreme" "Awakening"*. As for the necessity of teaching it in that way, it was "explained" "so" "that" "all" the boundless "sentient" "beings" "would" "attain" "this" kind of "path". And as the evidence of completely fulfilling his pledge from countless eons past, "I shall become manifestly awakened in order to completely protect sentient beings," out of objectless great compassion, by teaching this ultimate profound Dharma that he had realized in his own mind to those to be tamed, the close disciples of that time heard it directly from his mouth, and the distant disciples also heard it through transmission. By hearing it in that way they gained focus, by reflecting they understood the meaning, and by meditating they experienced it, gradually becoming Buddhas. In that manner, even across boundless directions and limitless time, the Buddhas who have become equal in the body of exalted wisdom establish sentient beings upon the path of unsurpassed awakening by means of these words and this meaning. ^1-480

### The Concluding Meaning ^1-4-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-481]]

The concluding meaning. Fourth, as for the concluding meaning, there are two parts: concluding the section with the words that indicate the remainder of the text without exception, and stating the translator's colophon in order to show that the source is authentic. First, ""The" "Verses" "Summarizing" "the" "Noble" "Perfection" "of" "Wisdom" "are" "complete"." ^1-481

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^1-482]]

Second, it says, "Translated, revised, and finalized by the Indian preceptor Vidyākarasiṃha and the great editor-translator Bande Peltsek." With that, the explanation of the explicit meaning of *The Verses Summarizing the Precious Qualities of the Noble Perfection of Wisdom* is complete. ^1-482

## Explanation According to the Implicitly Taught Clear Realization of the Path ^2-0

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^2-1]]

The second main section, matching with the stages of the clear realization of the path, should be understood from a separate text. ^2-1

![[bo-mipham-lekshe#^2-2]]

Here it is said: The jewel of the *Precious Summary of Qualities* is the priceless, precious inner essence of the perfection of wisdom, which is the heart of the great ocean of the well-spoken words of the Buddha. Such a method of Dharma, which abides in the profound and peaceful space of the mind of the form body with supreme marks like a full moon, and which was well drawn forth from his glorious throat, is rare in all the worlds. I vividly recall the final testament before passing into peace left by the supreme speaker of truth, saying it is more regrettable if even a mere fraction of a word of the perfection of wisdom degenerates than if all other textual traditions of Dharma were to degenerate. My joy in explaining, listening to, and composing upon this text explicitly taught by the Sugata, whose exalted wisdom cannot be illustrated even in the slightest by tenth-ground bodhisattvas as numerous as the sands of the Ganges, increases immensely. Look, children of noble family! This Dharma is the treasury of vast Dharma, the ultimate of the profound; rejoice that it is the sacred congruent cause of the exalted wisdom of the Buddhas who have completely perfected the two accumulations. The stages of clear realization for practice are also immensely clarified by the meaning, and I have composed this flawless good tradition of explaining the intent of the explicit teaching by means of scripture, pith instruction, and reasoning. Through the virtue of this, in all my lifetimes, having well realized the profound meaning of the union of emptiness and dependent origination, may I never turn away from the good path that pleases the Buddhas. From now until attaining Buddhahood, may my wisdom increase in lifetime after lifetime, and may I act for the welfare of beings with great compassion that abandons mental engagement in one's own welfare, and with supreme skillful means. With a mind seeking to repay the kindness of the supreme Buddhas, holding the treasury of the Buddha's Dharma without exception, like the bodhisattvas Samantabhadra and Mañjughoṣa, may I propagate the enlightened activities of the Buddhas to the ends of existence. May all those who see, hear, reflect on, and so forth this text, and all who are connected to me through good or bad karma, swiftly clear away the thick darkness of views and abide on the irreversible ground. By the truth that this Dharma is well-spoken in accordance with the nature of phenomena, may all the Buddhas and their heirs generate the glory of intelligent eloquence in those who uphold this, so that they may be completely victorious in all directions. May all demonic samaya-breakers, barbarians, and so forth who harm the teachings, and the human and non-human dark forces, have their harmful thoughts and actions completely pacified even in the directions where the volumes of this text reside. By its blessings, may whatever dark illusions are fabricated as Dharma be destroyed without trace, and by the power of the blazing light of authentic Dharma, may the benefit and happiness of beings increase higher and higher. Even in this darkness where the dregs of time flourish, may the lunar phase of exceptional resolve increase, and may many beautiful things rise satisfyingly, radiating the light of ten million cooling rays of altruism. May the supreme upholders of the teachings live long, may the wealth of the patrons of the teachings also increase, and may all beings have faith in the essence of the teachings, so that the precious teachings may expand supremely. May I alone well establish the ocean of sentient beings pervading to the limits of space upon the good path of the perfection of wisdom, and may they all become perfect Buddhas. In whatever regions this text flourishes, may illnesses, poverty, and conflicts be pacified, may goals be accomplished in accordance with the Dharma, and may the festival of auspiciousness increase higher and higher. Thus, this explanation for entering without error into the intent of the Mother of the Conquerors, having as its substantial cause my own wish to compose it in order to place good imprints of the profound Dharma upon my own mind and so forth, due to my having had extraordinary devotion to this Dharma since I was young, and having as its cooperative conditions the great seeker, the yogi of the Early Translation Great Perfection, Rigdzin Natsok Rangdrol, repeatedly encouraging me with his words, and also the great follower of the Early Translation tradition, the sacred guru Lungtok, earnestly encouraging me, and being encouraged by many propounders of Dharma such as the widely heard one named Do Ngak—relying on this, I, Mipham Jampal Gyepai Dorje, whose conviction in this Dharma method became firm, relying on the eloquent explanations of the Indian and Tibetan commentaries, began it on the fourteenth day of the sixth lunar month in my forty-seventh year, the Water Dragon year of Joy, at the glorious upper cave known as Karmo Taktsang, a place blessed by the Lord of Conquerors Padma, in the place called Tashi Palbar Pema Deden Ling, and wrote as much as I could on consecutive days, adapting to when I had leisure. I completed it entirely on the twenty-eighth day of that month, an excellent time endowed with the Pushya constellation, with perfect auspicious conditions. By this, may wonderful benefits spread throughout all directions and times, unceasingly bringing benefit to the teachings and to all impartial sentient beings! Sarva maṅgalaṃ! ^2-2
